# DEBT

Known defects, deferred obligations, and unverified claims. Stable IDs so a
session can reference an item without restating it. Nothing is removed from this
file; resolved items keep their row and gain a resolution.

Severity: `defect` (shipped and wrong), `gap` (missing and known), `drift`
(two sources disagree), `unverified` (claimed but not independently established),
`design` (a decision deliberately deferred).

| ID | Sev | Found | Item | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | design | 2026-07-24 | **Root of trust is unmodelled.** Three parts of the membership design lean on it; the only implementation is ADMIN_TOKEN, a bootstrap credential that became the root of trust by accident. It is a proxy for hosting access, has no custody model (no m-of-n, no split custody), leaves no audit trail, and cannot be rotated without returning the instance to unclaimed. Deciding what a root of trust should BE for a BIO group is doctrine work of the same weight as the membership model. See `architecture/BIO_Membership_Architecture_v1.md` §9. | DOCTRINE · DEFERRED 2026-07-31 (DEC-2) until a BIO instance runs with MULTIPLE MEMBERS, from which what a real group can actually hold in custody can be OBSERVED rather than guessed — a custody model assumed in the abstract fails silently in the group it was built for. No session re-raises it before the trigger. |
| D-2 | defect | 2026-07-24 | **History paths use a non-canonical layout.** `readImage` emits `_history/<snap_key>/<path>`; canonical is `_history/bundle_<snap_key>.md`. All 30 bundles fail C-12.2, 168 errors, no content missing. `schema.mjs` line 3 settles which side moves. | fixed 0.5.0 |

This is how D-6 and D-7 shipped invisibly. | S-2 | fixed 0.6.0 |

| D-8 | drift | 2026-07-24 | **Relationship vocabulary disagrees.** State Rules 5.1 declares a closed six-value vocabulary; the catalog carries a seventh, `corroborates`. | D-9 | unverified | 2026-07-24 | **Unreferenced register rows: MEASURED and then RE-MEASURED, 2026-07-26. All 87 rows are sound.** The entry guessed "superseded bytes plus dropped transport twins", untestable because no op could read the register. `op=registeraudit` (0.22.0) reported 57 live, 30 "dropped", 0 orphan, all 30 being `migration/drive-provenance.json`, one per bundle. **That "dropped" classification was WRONG and so was the conclusion drawn from it.** `registerAudit` looks only in `files` and `history` and never in R2. `migrate/migrate.mjs` line 14 states its intent plainly: the Drive provenance is carried "verbatim as a registered drive-provenance capture, so the Drive era remains inspectable WITHOUT POLLUTING THE LIVE FILE IMAGE". It registers the capture, uploads the bytes, and deliberately keeps the file out of the bundle, which is exactly what the two-bucket architecture is for. Verified against the live plane: `op=capture&sha256=45a07df1…` returns HTTP 200 and the actual provenance JSON. **The migration IS auditable and no register row is broken.** The intermediate claim that it was not, recorded here on 2026-07-26 and now retracted, came from reading `op=file` returning null and never checking whether the bytes existed elsewhere. CONSEQUENCE: `registerAudit`'s `dropped` class is misleading and its output must not be trusted until it distinguishes `captured` (not in the image, bytes retrievable from `bio-captures`, sound) from genuinely `unbacked` (bytes nowhere). No row is known to be unbacked. | M6 · measured, sound; registerAudit needs an R2 check |
| D-45 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **An unbacked register entry is refused at RATIFY, not at promote, so one can sit in the working corpus until publication.** REWRITTEN 2026-07-26; the first version of this entry said "nothing anywhere refuses it", which was wrong and was written before reading `src/gate.mjs`. `runGate` carries the invariant explicitly, under the comment "The plane's own remaining duty: bytes the register claims must exist": it probes R2 per register row and refuses with `PLANE_MISSING_BYTES`, or `PLANE_SIZE` when the register and the stored object disagree. That runs on ratify, which is the publication boundary and the two-bucket fence, and is arguably exactly the right place for it. What remains true is narrower: `promote` verifies nothing, so a provenance claim about bytes that exist nowhere is accepted into the WORKING corpus and reported as success, and stays undetected until someone ratifies or runs `op=registeraudit`. Whether to close that at promote is a design question, not an obvious fix. The obvious fix is wrong and was tried and reverted: requiring every entry to name a file in the same payload refuses `migrate.mjs`'s deliberate and correct pattern, and `migrate.test.mjs` failed on it immediately. A real promote-time guard would need R2 and therefore could not sit inside promote's `transactionSync`. DEFAULT POSITION: leave it, because the fence is where the fence should be, and `op=registeraudit` now reports the condition on demand. | ACCEPTED · open, default is to leave it |
| D-46 | defect | 2026-07-26 | **The deployed plane enforces the SUPERSEDED v1.4 rule for project participant removal.** `projectRemove` in `store.mjs` requires an in-app administrator and answers `ADMIN_ONLY`, which is Membership Architecture v1.4 section 7.7. v2 section 7.7 REVERSES it: only a project OWNER removes a participant, and administrators hold no authority over project participation at all (v2 section 4.9), retaining only sight (7.3, 7.8). Confirmed live on biosmoke7 at 0.24.0, which answers `ADMIN_ONLY`. `projects.test.mjs` asserts the old rule and must be CORRECTED rather than exempted, per standing lesson 3. RESOLVED in 0.25.0: `projectRemove` now requires an owner and answers `NOT_THE_OWNER`, `projectInvite` lost its administrator bypass, both run through one `#isProjectOwner` helper, and the four assertions encoding the old rule were corrected. Verified live on biosmoke7. | resolved 2026-07-26, 0.25.0 |
| D-47 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **7.13 is not built: an administrator cannot add an owner to a project whose owners are all inactive.** Membership Architecture v2 7.13 gives administrators exactly one participation power, and only when EVERY owner of that project is inactive. Until it exists, a sole owner who gets deactivated leaves a project that accepts no new participants, cannot be reactivated, and cannot change hands. Not urgent on a development instance with one project and no deactivated members. RESOLVED in 0.30.0 as `op=projectownerrescue`: an administrator adds ONE owner when EVERY owner is inactive, adding rather than replacing, recorded with the acting administrator and a reason. | resolved 2026-07-26, 0.30.0 |
| D-48 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **Project name uniqueness (7.1) is enforced at FORK and nowhere else.** `Store.projectNameKey` is the single comparison and `forkProject` consults it, but an ordinary `promote` creating a project does not, so two projects created by the normal path may still collide. The rule belongs in the check catalog and at the write path, per v2 section 11 item 8. Checked against biosmoke7 on 2026-07-26: 30 bundles, one project, no collisions, so closing this costs no migration there. | SUPERSEDED — closed by 0.28.0 (PLAN S-12 item 6); the residue is D-50 · open, S-12 step 6 |
| D-49 | defect | 2026-07-26 | **Reactivating an ejected administrator restored their administrator status.** A section 4.7 removal sets `status='revoked'` and leaves `role='admin'`, so `memberSet(active)` returned them to office with one call by any single administrator, undoing a group vote and defeating 4.7's consensus-on-addition. Found while building section 1.3, not by a report. RESOLVED in 0.27.0: reactivation demotes them to `member` and says so in the response; restoring the office goes through `adminendorse` like any other appointment. Asserted in `membership.test.mjs`, written failing first. | resolved 2026-07-26, 0.27.0 |
| D-50 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **Project name uniqueness is enforced at the write path but not in the check catalog.** v2 section 11 item 8 asks for both. `promote` refuses a colliding name, so nothing can be written wrong, but `bio-checks` cannot report the condition on a corpus it is handed from elsewhere, which is what the conformance path is for. Lower priority than the write path, which is the one that prevents the damage. | M7 · open |
| D-51 | drift | 2026-07-26 | **The `expertise` column on `members` is dead.** Section 1.3 moved to the append-only `member_expertise` table in 0.27.0, because a list column cannot carry a confirmation state, a confirmer and a withdrawal per entry. `memberAdd` still accepts and stores an `expertise` argument and `memberList` still returns the column, so two places now answer the same question and one of them is never updated. Left rather than dropped, since nothing here deletes, but the reader and the writer should be retired and `memberlist` should serve the table instead. RESOLVED in 0.30.0, and it was not merely drift: v1.4 let an administrator ASSIGN expertise at invitation time, which v2 1.3 forbids. `memberAdd` now refuses the argument rather than ignoring it and `memberList` serves from `member_expertise`. The column stays, unused. | resolved 2026-07-26, 0.30.0 |
| D-52 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **An export is recorded but no administrator is NOTIFIED.** Section 8.1 says an export is recorded in the append-only history AND every administrator is notified. `export_log` delivers the first half and is readable by in-app administrators through `op=exportlog`, so an export is discoverable. There is no notification channel in this system at all, so the second half is not implemented and an administrator who never looks will not learn of an export. Closing this needs a channel decision (in-app banner on next sign-in, or the communications surface the Roadmap describes) rather than more code here. | M7 · open |
| D-53 | gap | 2026-07-26 | **BIO has no reputation or credence mechanism, and S-11 step 5 is blocked on deciding whether it needs one.** Grepped, not assumed: no credence, reputation, trust or confidence field, table or check exists in `checks/` or `src/`. Bob's framing is that information from a credible source can be ratified with trust inherited from that source, that member ratification adds that member's trust, and that trust never decreases. Two existing doctrine statements bear on it and one partly contradicts it: `BIO_Technical_Architecture_Decisions_v10` **No transitive trust** says trust is never inherited but re-established locally at every hop and credibility is never demonstrated by the producer's reputation, scoped to PEER BIO GROUPS and the R13 infiltration threat rather than to external primary sources; and `BIO_Design_Requirements_v2` section 4 says reputation accrues through consistent quality with no authority managing the process, so any mechanism must be a group's own ledger and not a shared score. Blocking finding for any design: **references target a bundle id and nothing finer** (`BUNDLE_ID_RE`), with no anchor, span or claim identifier anywhere in the catalog, so credence attached to an edge is still document-granular and cannot distinguish an authoritative table of figures from a self-interested assertion in the same document. See SESSION-KICKOFF.md for the full parked analysis. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open, blocks S-11 step 5 |
| D-10 | defect | 2026-07-24 | **A no-op update reads as success.** The installer reports "Updated to X" whenever the upload succeeds, including when X equals the version already installed. The four `TOKEN_*` bearer tokens retire only when the deployment is deleted, not when it goes idle, so the frozen Drive snapshot is not actually read-only yet. | resolved 2026-07-27 by S-9 — every Apps Script-era credential revoked in its own system and the deployment deleted; see PLAN.md S-9, which records the account topology and why each revocation was safe. The row's status had never been updated from the step id, which is how it read as open work for four days. |

| D-15 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **The index must filter derived reverse edges by the viewer's position.** CLOSED 0.23.0. The seam reserved in S-10 held: satisfying it was one function, `viewerPredicate` in `query.mjs`, and not an audit of every query path. An identified session now compiles a predicate over `project_participants`; an uninvited member sees no project at all, not its existence, name, references or participants (7.9). The EVIDENCE CORPUS STAYS SHARED by design: the filter applies to project bundles and nothing else, because compartmenting Information and Problems would fracture the record and stop a member on one project seeing what another had gathered. Machine credentials are deliberately unfiltered: `class:member` is a shared instance token with no participation to check, and anyone holding one already has instance-level access, so filtering it would buy nothing while breaking the operator path. MEASURED, because the bench drives `class:member` and therefore did not exercise the new predicate at all: a member-viewer shape was added and the filter costs 16ms against 17ms unfiltered at 20,000 bundles, the `EXISTS` arms short-circuiting on `object_type <> 'project'` for almost every row. `test/projects.test.mjs`, 25 assertions. | closed 0.23.0 |
| D-17 | defect | 2026-07-24 | **Manifest `created` was the server's wall clock**, where C-12.1 compares live `last_updated` against earlier entries' `created`. A signed ratification backdates `last_updated` to the transition instant, so an honest document read as history running backwards. The catalog's own comments record this exact deadlock freezing a bundle on 2026-07-22: satisfying C-12.1 meant editing bundle.md and destroying the ratification. Now stamped from the revision's own time. Found by the conformance suite on its first run. | fixed 0.5.0 |
| D-21 | drift | 2026-07-24 | **References live in two places and only one is checked.** The plane's `refs` table is populated from the promote payload; the catalog reads `references` out of `bundle.md` frontmatter. A caller can set them inconsistently and nothing notices, because C-6 only ever sees the frontmatter. Either promote should parse references from the document, or the payload field should go. | open | fixed 0.6.2 |
| D-22 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **The revise path writes no Session Log entry.** C-13.2 requires one on any bundle whose `last_updated` moves past `created`, and C-5.1 requires prior entries be retained. A member editing through the browser can therefore produce a bundle the gate refuses, with a message about the Session Log that will mean nothing to them. The editor should append an entry on save. | open | fixed 0.6.1 |
| D-23 | defect | 2026-07-24 | **The revise path had never worked.** The save handler read `lease.result.baseSha`; the lease returns `base`. So every revision sent `base: undefined`, the store correctly refused it as a stale write, and the page reported the raw code `CAS_STALE` because its friendly message matched on `STALE`. It also overwrote `created` with the save time, destroying when the bundle was actually created, and never moved `last_updated` in the document the catalog reads. Found while fixing D-22, which was one of four things wrong in the same twenty lines. | fixed 0.6.1 |
| D-24 | defect | 2026-07-24 | **A comment broke the served page, for the second time.** setup.mjs is one template literal, so an unescaped backtick terminates it and a dollar-brace starts an interpolation. A comment quoting two field names in backticks made the module unparseable. The 0.3.8 hang was the same class. Both were found by accident; `hygiene.test.mjs` now scans the template for unescaped backticks, counts interpolations, loads the module, and parses the script it serves. | guarded 0.6.1 |
| D-25 | design | 2026-07-24 | **`promote` writes a whole image, so a partial writer deletes what it does not mention.** This is correct for a content-addressed store with a single writer, and it is a trap: the monitor's first tick supplied only `bundle.md` and removed the provenance register from every bundle it touched, taking the monitoring baseline with it. Fixed at the call site, and the image now carries a blob's size so callers CAN pass the rest back, but the sharp edge is still there for the next writer. Options: a patch-style promote that merges, or a refusal when a promotion drops a file that the previous revision had and the writer did not name. The second is probably right, since silent deletion has no legitimate use. | open | fixed 0.13.1 |
| D-26 | design | 2026-07-24 | **A whole-store conformance pass pays one round trip per bundle.** Measured: 63s locally for 20,000 bundles, about 2,060s on the deployed plane sequentially, roughly 97% of it fixed per-call latency. The catalog is a pure function over an injected filesystem and the images already live inside the Durable Object, so the pass belongs inside the object: one call in, a findings summary out. Store and checks are not the constraint; the shape of the API is. | open | fixed 0.12.0 |
| D-27 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **`list` returns every bundle and is linear.** 81ms at 5,000, 434ms at 20,000, roughly 2s at 100,000. Fine now, and it returns everything because nothing has needed less. Pagination or a narrower projection is the answer, and the benchmark above says when. | open | fixed 0.14.0 |
| D-28 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **The Conversion Plan's prediction table is not in the corpus.** Four documents reference "Conversion Plan step 6" and its prediction table; the plan itself is not in the repository, so the benchmark records actuals with nothing to compare them against. Either the plan joins docs/architecture or the references should stop implying a document a reader can find. | ACCEPTED · open; probe 1 (RETRIEVAL-PROBE.md) recorded actuals with no prediction table to check against, as this gap predicts |
| D-29 | defect | 2026-07-24 | **The browser's revise path deleted every file but bundle.md.** Identical to the monitor's first tick, in a different place: editing a bundle with a captured document removed its provenance register and every capture. Found by closing D-25, which refused the write instead of obeying it. Both call sites now carry files forward. | fixed 0.13.1 |
| D-31 | design | 2026-07-25 | **The `public` token class was an attractive nuisance and is retired.** A credential handed to the public is not a credential: to be public it must be widely distributed, and once distributed it bounds nothing. It granted `selftest` and `publishedlist`, was never issued by the installer (which creates exactly boot, member, and probe), and was bound on no instance, yet its existence is what invited `op=index` onto its list and produced D-30. The published surface is protected structurally by the `classes: null` ops that read only the published projection. When a public directory of published material is wanted, it is an unauthenticated op on that projection, like `verify`, never a token class. | fixed 0.14.2, held by test/fence.test.mjs |
| D-30 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **`op=index` served working-corpus metadata to the public token class.** The control plane's own header says the public class is published-scope reads only, and its note on `verify` justifies that op being unauthenticated precisely because it answers from the published projection. `op=index` reads the `bundles` table, so it met neither condition, and a public-class credential received every bundle's id, title, current state, last-updated time, and image hash. A title names what a group is investigating and the state says how far along it is, both before there is anything to answer, which is the disclosure the two-bucket fence exists to prevent. No caller depended on the grant. Found while settling the retrieval design, since the same fence governs search. | fixed 0.14.1, held by test/fence.test.mjs |
| D-19 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **Migrated manifest entries carry the migration's wall clock** as their `created`, not the original promotion time, so C-12.1 reports 18 bundles as having a live bundle.md older than a superseded version. D-17 fixed the stamping code; these rows predate it. Cured by re-migration on 0.5.1 rather than by in-place repair. | M6 · open |
| D-20 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **The gate needs byte-complete images.** C-12.2 checks history-snapshot presence with `files.has` rather than the elided-aware helper, so a tier-scoped read fails it even when the bytes exist in R2. Correct for a byte-complete gate, but it means gating a capture-heavy bundle pulls its history blobs: 148MB for the whole record. Measure in S-8 and consider whether R2 `head` can satisfy presence without a fetch. | S-8 | fixed 0.6.0, catalog 1.16.5 |
| D-18 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **The conformance suite asserts only the C-12 family.** Other families are counted and printed but not failed, because the intake path owes D-7. Tighten to "zero findings" when S-3 lands. | M0 · S-3 |
| D-16 | gap | 2026-07-24 | **The cover field must be labelled as a label**, not a name, wherever it appears. A field that reads as "identity" invites an administrator to type a legal name, which is the whole exposure the cover-and-handle split exists to avoid. Naming it is the cheapest part of the obligation and the only part the software can enforce. | open | fixed 0.14.0 |

| D-32 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **The shipped retrieval path is 3.5x slower than the probe it inherited its numbers from.** MEASURED, 2026-07-25, by `npm run bench:retrieval` (test/retrieval-scale.mjs), which loads a corpus through the real `promote` and drives the real `op=search`. At 20,000 bundles the shipped path runs 5ms to 163ms; probe 2 recorded nothing above ~46ms. The gap is real work the probe did not do: a 26-column provenance projection per hit, the viewer gate, bm25 with snippets, and a facet pass. Two structural causes were found and fixed in 0.17.0, halving the worst shape from 305ms: the gate was a CTE intersected into every statement, which cost a full table scan per statement, and is now a WHERE predicate on rows already selected; and the facet pass ran one statement per field, rebuilding the same scope six times, and is now batched. What remains is inherent to the contract rather than accidental, so closing this further means a covering index for the provenance columns, or counting facets from one scan in JS instead of a GROUP BY per field. Bob's decision, 2026-07-25: ship in the measured condition and stop quoting the probe's numbers as if they described this code. | M5 · measured 0.17.0; further optimisation open |
| D-33 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **The id tiebreak is required by argument, not by a failing test.** Every sort compiles to `ORDER BY <field> <dir>, bundle_id ASC` because without a total order a row can appear on two pages or on none. `test/search.test.mjs` builds 600 bundles that all carry one value on the sort field and pages the whole set. Removing the tiebreak from the compiler and re-running it STILL PASSES: at that size SQLite returns tied rows in a deterministic order. So the order is deterministic but undeclared, which is the real hazard, since the delivery order is a property of the plan and the plan changes with corpus size, with an added index, and with an engine upgrade. The guard that actually holds is at compile time, in `test/query.test.mjs`, which asserts every sortable field in both directions ends in a unique column. Demonstrating the runtime failure needs a corpus large enough to spill the sorter, which belongs with D-32 and not in a suite. | M0 · open |
| D-34 | design | 2026-07-25 | **A server-side selection has no lifecycle and no owner.** Step 5 of the retrieval build order is not in 0.16.0. `mode=ids` returns the whole set in the page's order, which satisfies "select-all is a distinct operation", but the snapshot-by-handle construct is absent and with it every resource question it raises. A Worker holds no connection, so the plane cannot observe a closed tab and can only require proof of life: ownership by session so a logout or a revocation collects, a TTL refreshed on read, a Durable Object alarm to sweep, and caps by BYTES before count, since a 20,000-id selection is 400 to 800KB in the same SQLite that holds the record. Above the byte cap, store the query plus the SHA-256 of the ordered id list rather than the ids: drift detection stays exact and storage becomes O(1), at the cost of being able to say a large selection drifted without saying which rows moved. Note also that a selection is the first thing in an append-only store that is legitimately collectable, and that exception needs writing into doctrine or a later session will read the sweep as a violation. | fixed 0.17.0 |
| D-35 | design | 2026-07-25 | **Drift in a selection must be detected and must not be absorbed.** When the corpus moves under a selection, five things can have happened: an item was revised, an item was purged, an item stopped matching the query, a new bundle started matching, or the viewer's visibility changed. The first two are one join if `bundle_sha` is stored per item; the next two are one re-run of the stored query; the last is the gate applied at action time, which is obligatory anyway. Auto-updating the set is rejected: a selection records an operator's INTENT, and an action landing on rows the operator never saw is an accountability failure in a record whose whole purpose is attribution. The monitor complicates this honestly, since mechanical revisions will drift a long-lived selection with no human involved, so drift should be CLASSIFIED using the `writer` and `operation` already on the manifest rather than absorbed. | fixed 0.17.0, held by test/selection.test.mjs |

| D-36 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **workerd's SQL limits are orders of magnitude below SQLite's documented defaults, and a bench found them rather than the suite.** Two ceilings, both binary-searched through the real code path on 2026-07-25: a statement may bind about **100 variables** (SQLite's default is 32,766) and a compound SELECT may have **five terms** (SQLite's default is 500). The first broke a chunked id list at 400 per chunk; the second broke a six-arm facet statement AND, more seriously, would have broken the query compiler itself, since six metadata filters is one ordinary pass over a filter sidebar and compiles to six INTERSECT arms. Both are now guarded, with the id chunk at 64 and compound chains nesting through subqueries at 4 per compound, and `test/query.test.mjs` asserts the nesting across four query shapes. The open item is the CLASS: these limits are undocumented, they are not the platform's published behaviour, and any new statement shape can meet another one. The lesson is the one that keeps recurring in this repository, which is that a bench that runs the real path at real size finds what a suite at small scale cannot. | M5 · guarded 0.17.0; the class is open |
| D-37 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **The 300-second selection keep-alive.** CLOSED by Bob's decision, 2026-07-25, on the grounds that a selection timeout is not a failure mode but a term of the contract, the same way an idle logout is: intrinsic to the relationship rather than an error, and its consequences the client's to decide. The client may re-select against a possibly different record set, tell the user the window closed, or warn as expiry approaches and offer to hold. The contract is already expressed on the wire and needs no new machinery: `op=select` returns `expires` and `ttlSeconds`, `op=selection` returns a refreshed `expires`, and `NO_SUCH_SELECTION` IS the timeout signal. `op=selection` is non-mutating and defaults to weight `report`, so a client polls it both to hold the lease and to learn what drifted, which is what a UI wants to show anyway. Reclamation is alarm-driven: `#armSweep` on creation, `alarm()` sweeping and re-arming while any selection remains, self-terminating when the table empties. 300 seconds stands. Three claims made against this item during the 0.21.1 session were WRONG and are recorded so they are not made again: that the sweep was opportunistic-only and needed an alarm (it has one), that expired and unknown needed distinguishing server-side (the client knows its own creation time and TTL and can distinguish locally), and that no way existed for a client to say it was still present (`op=selection` is exactly that). | closed by decision |
| D-38 | design | 2026-07-25 | **The number of Information records a Project can cite is bounded by INLINE_MAX, not by SELECTION_MAX_ITEMS, and the two were set independently.** Citing writes one frontmatter entry per cited record into a single `bundle.md`, measured at 83 bytes per edge at a 25-character bundle id. The enumeration cap is 10,000 and the inline file limit is 1MB, so a MAXIMUM LEGAL SELECTION originally produced a 1,070,846-byte document and was refused by `promote` with `OVERSIZE_INLINE`: an error about inline byte storage, in answer to an operator who selected a legal number of records and pressed cite. Found by `test/cite-scale.mjs`, not by the suite. Two changes in 0.18.0 make it survivable rather than resolved: the Session Log entry is bounded at 20 named ids plus a count (it had listed every id, costing a further 24 bytes per edge), which took an edge from 107 to 84 bytes and lets a maximum 10,000 selection fit at 841KB; and `CITATION_TOO_LARGE` now refuses before anything is written, naming the real limit and the room remaining. The ceiling is now reachable only CUMULATIVELY, and is: at 12,000 edges a further 2,000 refuses and leaves the document byte-identical. `bundle.md` cannot spill to R2 to escape this, because the gate compares it byte-wise against history (C-5, C-12) and `schema.mjs` keeps every file the gate reads inline by rule. Bob's decision, 2026-07-25: leave the ceiling where it is. The tested ceiling is far above any plausible group, lowering the selection cap would penalise selections that are not for citing, and there may be Cloudflare configuration raising these limits that has not been explored. Revisit when a real corpus approaches it. | ACCEPTED · open, by decision |
| D-39 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **Every op returns a worker exception on a POST with an empty body.** `store.mjs`'s `fetch` calls `await req.json()` unconditionally when the method is POST, so an empty body throws before any op is dispatched and the caller sees Cloudflare error 1101 rather than a BIO refusal. Verified on the deployed 0.18.0 against `stats`, `index`, `dangling`, `searchfields` and `selectionlist`, so it is general and pre-dates the citing work; it was found while exercising `op=cite` live. Harmless to correctness, since every op that matters is reachable by GET or by POST with `{}`, but it turns a client bug into an opaque platform error instead of a named reason. One-line fix, deliberately not folded into the 0.18.0 release. RESOLVED 2026-07-31 (REC-3): the guard already existed at the Durable Object (store.mjs `fetch`, commit 6ac72d0a — an empty POST body is `null`, a present-but-invalid one is refused `BAD_JSON` by name), so an empty POST already returns a named JSON reason rather than a 1101; the debt stayed open only because NO suite locked it in. `test/empty-body.test.mjs` now drives an empty POST to the five debt-named ops (stats/index/dangling/searchfields/selectionlist) and to mutating ops (promote→NO_BODY etc.), with the negative control RUN (break the guard → the five ops return a non-JSON 500, 8 fail; restored → 10 pass). | resolved 2026-07-31, REC-3 |
| D-40 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **Test and bench fixtures write `criticality: notable`, which is not a legal value.** The catalog's enum is `crucial` or `supporting` (C-2.7), so every Information bundle these fixtures build is non-conformant, and a conformance assertion run over one would fail for a reason unrelated to what it was testing. Found on the deployed 0.19.0 by auditing the scratch namespace after a live check, not by any suite, because the suites conformance-check the PROJECT they cite into and never the Information they cite. Fixed in the fixtures written this session (`cite`, `edges`, `cite-scale`, `limits-probe`). NOT fixed in `retrieval-scale.mjs`, `search.test.mjs` and `selection.test.mjs`, where `notable` is deliberately used as facet and filter test DATA (`q=criticality:notable` is a bench shape measuring the dominant-value case) and changing it would rewrite assertions to fix a fixture rather than a defect. The real risk is a future session copying one of those fixtures as a template. | M0 · open, narrowed |
| D-41 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **The Section 4 governance write path had never run against a deployed instance.** CLOSED 2026-07-25 on the deployed 0.21.1, once Bob issued a throwaway ADMIN_TOKEN. 27 of 27 assertions passed live: consensus refusing an addition past the second and naming who must still endorse, the last endorsement issuing the invitation, one removal vote falling short with the tally reported, the target refused their own vote, a second vote carrying with deciders and reason recorded, `IMPOSSIBLE_AT_TWO` at two administrators, `ROOT_OF_TRUST` refusing to remove the founder, `ADMIN_REQUIRES_VOTE` refusing a direct revocation, and the capability rules. So 0.20.0 carries no D-43-shaped hole. The record was untouched throughout: 30 bundles, 137 files, 239 history rows, audit 30 clean. NOTE: the verification ran against the `bio` store rather than `scratch`, because D-44 made the member lifecycle uncompletable in `scratch`. Two probe member rows and some admin_votes rows remain in the biosmoke7 roster as a result; the RECORD is unaffected and the instance is disposable. | closed |
| D-44 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **Unauthenticated ops ignored the `store` parameter, making an invitation created in `scratch` unredeemable.** `enroll` and `invitelook` are `classes: null` and the control plane pinned every such op to `idFromName("bio")`. So `op=memberadd&store=scratch` answered `ok` and handed over a token that could never be redeemed, because enrolment always reached `bio`. A silent dead end, and it made the scratch namespace useless for rehearsing the member surface, which is exactly what an operator would want to do before touching a live roster. Pinning is CORRECT for `claim` and `login`, since an instance has one identity and there is nothing to claim in a scratch namespace, so the fix scopes only the invitation ops: the token is the authority and exists in exactly one store, so naming a store grants an unauthenticated caller nothing. Fixed 0.21.2, asserted in `fence.test.mjs`. Found against the deployed plane while closing D-41, not by 1280 green assertions. | fixed 0.21.2 |
| D-42 | gap | 2026-07-25 | **Burner-URL invitations are specified but only half built.** Section 6 wants a URL carrying a one-time code that is SPENT ON USE and afterwards resolves to nothing, carrying no record of what it formerly addressed, so a leaked or archived link is inert and reveals neither the group nor the invitee. What exists is an invite code the administrator hands over, reached through a URL fragment (the D-14 fix), and the code is correctly cleared at enrolment so it cannot be replayed. What is missing is the URL itself being the credential and resolving to nothing afterwards rather than to an enrolment form that says the invite is spent. Unblocked. | M7 · open |
| D-43 | defect | 2026-07-25 | **A `classes: null` op tested only at the Durable Object is untested where it is used.** `op=invitelook` shipped in 0.21.0 with a ReferenceError in its control-plane branch, so every live call answered a Cloudflare 1101 while 1276 assertions passed: the burner suite drove the store directly, as every store-level suite does, and for an unauthenticated op the control plane is the ONLY route a real caller has. Fixed in 0.21.1. THE CLASS IS NOW CLOSED TOO: `fence.test.mjs` reads the `classes: null` set out of the module and calls every one of them through the control plane with input that should produce a structured refusal, asserting only that a BIO answer comes back rather than a worker exception or `unknown op`. Reachability, not success. The probe catches a thrown parse, because a worker exception returns a non-JSON body and a verifier that dies on the failure it exists to detect reports nothing. Reading the set from the module means an op added later cannot pass by not being listed. | closed 0.22.1 |

## How to use this file

Reference items by ID in commit messages and in session notes. When a step in
`PLAN.md` resolves an item, change its status to the commit that did it rather
than deleting the row: the history of what was wrong is worth as much as the
record of what was fixed, and a ledger that only shows current problems teaches
nobody anything.

Items marked `accepted` are known, judged tolerable, and carry the reasoning in
the row. They are not resolved and should be re-read when circumstances change.
| D-54 | gap | 2026-07-29 | **The installer does not check or enforce the Workers plan.** BIO now depends on platform limits that differ by plan, and a group installing on Workers Free gets a working instance that behaves differently from every description of it: 50 external subrequests per invocation instead of 10,000, 10ms of CPU per request instead of 30s, 10 minutes of browser time per DAY instead of 10 hours per month. All of that is survivable (the plane calibrates its own ceiling and captures across ticks) and none of it is visible to the person installing. The installer must detect the plan, state plainly what capture will and will not do on it, and record the answer where the instance can report it. Enforcement is the open question: REFUSING to install on Free would contradict the ruling that Workers Paid is an optimisation and never a requirement, so the likely shape is inform-and-record rather than refuse. Wrangler's `limits.subrequests` is also unset in this repo and should be set explicitly once a plan is known, because the default moved from 1,000 to 10,000 in February 2026 and a default that moves is not a decision. | M2 · open **CHANGED IN CHARACTER 2026-08-04 by DEC-42: the installer no longer merely DETECTS the plan — it REQUIRES Workers Paid, VERIFIES it, and refuses to complete honestly rather than installing something quietly degraded.** That refusal IS this row's fix now: the D-106 failure this guards against (a group installing today getting something quietly different from every description of it) is closed by refusing, not by warning. Free is no longer a supported configuration. |
| D-55 | design | 2026-07-29 | **A rendered capture may contain content whose authority is not the hosting site, and `capture.authority` holds exactly one value.** Bob's ruling: if content produced by a third-party script is ever evidence, it must be recorded as produced by that third party and not by the hosting site. The provenance document has one `authority` per capture, so a rendered document carrying an advertiser's, an analytics vendor's, or a CDN-hosted widget's output cannot currently attribute it. Attribution has to be per-origin and sub-document, which is a shape the register has never held: `references[]` targets a bundle and nothing finer, the same granularity problem D-53 is blocked on. Deciding this is a precondition for treating any rendered capture as evidence, not a follow-on. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · BLOCKS D-64 · open, blocks CLIENT-RENDERED.md |
| D-56 | unverified | 2026-07-29 | **CPU headroom: MEASURED 2026-07-29, and the worry was unfounded on the numbers available. HELD OPEN DELIBERATELY as a watch item with no task attached.** The entry asked whether capture was near the free plan's documented 10ms CPU ceiling. Measured on biosmoke7 (Workers Free) at 0.44.0: `op=cpuprobe` completed 20 steps of 2,000,000 reference iterations, 40,000,000 in total, and the isolate was killed during step 21 with HTTP 503 `error code: 1102`. Forty million modular multiplications is not 10ms of anything, so whatever is enforced on this account is not the documented figure. Real captures are nowhere near it: the heaviest measured, a news front page, does 49 compute calls over 16.97MB of hashing and parsing. **A SEPARATE AND MORE IMPORTANT FINDING came out of trying: a Worker CANNOT TIME ITSELF.** Cloudflare freezes `Date.now()` during synchronous execution as a timing-attack defence, so the first meter reported zero for every segment of every real capture. Any millisecond figure reported from inside a Worker is a fabrication. Consumption is therefore counted in WORK (calls, bytes) and the ceiling in REFERENCE ITERATIONS; see `src/cpu.mjs`. **WHY THIS STAYS OPEN.** A CPU overrun terminates the isolate: there is no catchable error, no chance to record anything, and the caller sees an opaque 1102. Unlike the subrequest ceiling, this failure mode can never announce itself, so a future scope of work can begin silently overrunning it and the only symptom will be requests that stop working. Nothing is asked of any session by this row. It exists so that an unexplained 1102, or a capture that fails only on large inputs, is read as a CPU overrun on the first guess instead of the fifth. If work volume per capture ever grows by an order of magnitude, re-run `op=cpuprobe` and compare. | WATCH, no task · open as a watch item, no task |
| D-57 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **`resolveLinks` describes a self-reference as a target that CHANGED, and the UI prints that sentence to a member verbatim.** Found on live data during the U8 exercise. A paginated index links to itself (every Legistar calendar does), so the store holds a capture of the target whose `first_retrieved` and `last_retrieved` both equal the source document's own retrieval instant. The bracket arm needs `observations > 1` and does not fire; the `before && after` arm then matches THE SAME ROW on both sides and returns `undetermined` with the basis "the target changed somewhere between the captures bracketing this document's retrieval", plus a detail naming one capture hash twice: `bracketing captures differ: fc7f4bb39281 last seen 2026-07-30T00:18:02Z, fc7f4bb39281 first seen 2026-07-30T00:18:02Z`. Nothing changed and the two captures are one capture. The verdict value is defensible; the BASIS is false, and because the UI shows the plane's basis in the plane's own words rather than paraphrasing it, a member reads a fabricated claim about a source. The fix belongs in the plane, not the viewer: `resolveLinks` should recognise `pick.capture_sha === sourceCapture` and report a self-reference, which is a fourth basis rather than a fourth verdict. The UI already counts self-references apart from connections (see below), so the count is right while the sentence is wrong. | M3 · open |
| D-58 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **`captured_locators` and `links` are written ONLY when `op=acquire` is called with `subresources:true`, so an ordinary capture files no address and can never be a link target.** Found while trying to establish a `contemporaneous` verdict live: a plain `op=acquire` of a page that other captures link to left the verdict at `undetermined` because nothing had recorded that the store now held that address. Both `recordcapturedlocator` and `recordlinks` sit inside the subresource branch in `index.mjs` (the block guarded by reading the primary back out for the parser), which means the address index reflects only the subset of captures that asked for support material. Consequences: a link to a plainly-captured document resolves as `offsite` when the record demonstrably holds it; the reverse index (`op=links&address=`) under-reports what points at a document; and no verdict can ever be established for a target captured the ordinary way. `recordcapturedlocator` needs nothing from the parser and belongs beside the register write, unconditionally. The links write legitimately needs the parse and can stay where it is. RESOLVED 2026-07-30: moved to the capture body, unconditional, with `stLim` hoisted. Regression test drives `op=acquire` and asserts both directions (a PDF and a plainly-captured page resolve as `linked` naming their real hashes; an address the record genuinely lacks is still `offsite`). Negative control confirms five assertions fail against the unfixed code. | resolved 2026-07-30 |
| D-59 | unverified | 2026-07-30 | **`contemporaneous` has never been observed on real data, and the strongest verdict arm may be unreachable for the sources BIO actually captures.** The arm requires one set of bytes observed on both sides of a document's retrieval, `observations > 1` on a single `captured_locators` row. Measured 2026-07-30 on `oakland.legistar.com`: two captures of `Legislation.aspx` twelve minutes apart produced two DIFFERENT hashes (`7f1083d74f30`, `8691808a30ab`), because ASP.NET pages carry per-response viewstate and timestamps. A page whose bytes differ on every request can never produce an `observations > 1` row, so identical-byte bracketing cannot fire for it regardless of whether the content changed. That makes `undetermined` not merely the common case but the ONLY reachable verdict for a large class of municipal sources, which is honest and is also weaker than the design assumed. The other establishing routes named in LINK-FIDELITY.md (a timestamp token over the target's hash, a third-party archive, monitoring across the interval with no change detected) do not depend on byte identity and are now the load-bearing ones rather than the fallbacks. Worth measuring across more hosts before deciding whether the bracket arm earns its complexity. | M3 · open |
| D-60 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A capture has no STABLE DIGEST, so change detection, duplicate detection and contemporaneity all run on a hash that moves for reasons that are not changes.** MEASURED 2026-07-30, two fetches of `oakland.legistar.com/Calendar.aspx` three seconds apart: identical length, **114,177 differing bytes, 31% of the document**, every differing byte inside exactly two hidden fields, `__VIEWSTATE` (115,096 bytes) and `__EVENTVALIDATION` (876 bytes). Normalise those two and the remaining **252,948 bytes, 68.6%, are byte-identical**. Nothing about the calendar changed. ASP.NET reserialises its control tree and reissues its anti-forgery list on every response, and municipal publishing runs on this class of software. Three separate mechanisms are wrong because of it: **monitoring** reports a change on every tick and therefore reports nothing; **duplicate detection** does not fire on re-captures of the pages that get re-captured most, so the record grows a second bundle for one document while C-18.3 and `op=audit` both stay silent because the hashes genuinely differ; and **contemporaneity's strongest arm** can never fire (see D-59). The fix is one thing: beside its raw identity hash, a capture carries a derived digest computed with known-volatile regions normalised to a placeholder. Identity stays raw and raw bytes are never rewritten; comparison uses the stable digest. Volatile regions are RECORDED with their field names, offsets and lengths, and the fact that two fetches differed only there is itself a dated observation worth keeping. Classification is per-family, heuristic, and reclassifiable exactly as chrome detection is, discovered by measurement rather than declared. A volatile region is never treated as evidence. Design written into LINK-FIDELITY.md. **PARTLY DONE 2026-07-30:** the volatile machinery is NOT at `civicos-ui/volatile.mjs` (that path does not exist; the row was wrong). It lives in `docprofile/index.mjs` (the `digests` and `compare` exports) with per-family handlers in `docprofile/handlers/` (`aspnet-webforms.mjs` is the Legistar/viewstate case), validated both directions against two live captures of one Legistar page. The PLANE has not adopted it: monitoring, `op=audit`'s duplicate sweep, and `resolveLinks`' bracket arm all still compare raw hashes, so the plane should import `digests`/`compare` rather than grow a second copy. **ADOPTION FEASIBILITY MEASURED 2026-07-30:** docprofile is 37KB of pure JS, zero external deps, zero node builtins, only internal cross-imports. Bundled through the plane's own esbuild target it tree-shakes to **5.3KB** against 2.4MB of headroom under the 3MB Free limit, so the bundle-size concern that applies to unpdf (D-91) does NOT apply here. docprofile sits at repo root OUTSIDE `bio-plane/`; the import path from `src/` is `../../docprofile/index.mjs`, and because `build` inlines all imports into one self-contained `dist/bio-plane.bundled.mjs`, the deployed artifact and the installer are unaffected by the cross-directory location as long as `docprofile/` is present at BUILD time (it is, in a clean checkout). Adoption is a build task, not an investigation. | M3 · open — **PARTLY STALE, corrected 2026-08-06: the PLANE HAS ADOPTED docprofile** (`bio-plane/src/index.mjs:70` imports `identify, doctypeFor, profileRecord, digests, CONFIDENCE, readText` from `docprofile/registry.mjs`), so "the PLANE has not adopted it" is no longer true as written. **CHECKED 2026-08-06 (sub-session). THE ROW IS ONE-THIRD DISCHARGED, and my own 2026-08-06 correction above was half-right — it is true of the WRITE path only.** `compare()` is **never called anywhere in `bio-plane/src/`**; the only caller in the repo outside docprofile itself is the browser (`app.html:13676`). The plane calls `identify()` and `digests()` in exactly one place, `op=acquire` (`index.mjs:3251`, `:3314`). Site by site: **(a) MONITORING — STILL RAW.** `index.mjs:3834-3838` digests the fetched bytes with `crypto.subtle` and compares `seen === baseline` against `provenance.json`'s recorded sha. No digest, no compare. On a Legistar page this reports `modified` every tick, which is this row's original measurement, undischarged. **(b) AUDIT'S DUPLICATE DETECTION — DISCHARGED.** `checkRegisterIntegrity` (`bio-checks.mjs:3553-3558`) buckets on `docs[i].profile.digests.evidentiary` when `determined === true`, beside the raw arm, firing only when ≥2 different raw shas share a digest. **So this row's claim that the duplicate sweep compares raw hashes is STALE, and `DOCUMENT-PROFILES.md:309-310` repeats the stale claim** — delegated to FRAMEWORK in `CLAIMS.md`. But note the scope: it is INTRA-BUNDLE, and no cross-bundle sweep exists at all. **(c) `resolveLinks`' CONTEMPORANEITY BRACKET — STILL RAW.** `store.mjs:14859-14873` brackets over `captured_locators` rows whose identity is raw `capture_sha`, so on a viewstate-churning page every fetch mints a fresh row with `observations = 1`, `bracket` is always null, and the verdict degrades to undetermined. **This is D-59's "strongest arm can never fire", still true.** So what remains on this row is (a) and (c) |
| D-61 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **`op=lease` cannot be taken by a machine credential, so no unattended writer can revise a bundle.** Found live: `leases.actor` is NOT NULL and is stamped from the session, so a member TOKEN (as opposed to a member session) fails with `SQLITE_CONSTRAINT_NOTNULL` on `leases.actor`. The revision path in the UI takes a lease before promoting, which means the capture-refill path is session-only. That collides with Bob's ruling that captures are autonomous jobs a member can walk away from: if the daemon finishes a capture the member started, it cannot write the completed manifest back, because it cannot take a lease. Two candidate shapes, and the choice is a judgment: either the lease grows a machine actor identity (`class:member`, `daemon`) so unattended writers are named rather than anonymous, or the refill path skips the lease and relies on `promote`'s CAS on `base`, which is the actual integrity mechanism and which was used to work around this today. The lease is a courtesy lock against two members editing at once; the CAS is what prevents a lost update. Whichever way it goes, the daemon needs to be able to complete work it was asked to do. **CLOSED 2026-07-31 (REC-2, e425b24):** option (a) — a named machine actor `token:<class>`, server-stamped and unforgeable, no schema change; the store refuses an anonymous lease by name, and promote's CAS on `base` (the real integrity mechanism) is untouched. | M1 · CLOSED (REC-2) |
| D-62 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **The installer's `mdFor` omits `content_hash` even when a document is attached, so any bundle it writes can never be released.** Found live on the first bundle U8 wrote. C-2.7 makes a well-formed `content_hash` an ENTRY REQUIREMENT for `verified`, and the release flow reads the frontmatter and nothing else, so a bundle carrying a perfectly good capture is refused at `collected -> verified` for a field the writing surface never wrote. It also makes the bundle unfindable through the search layer's `hash:` facet, which reads that same field, which in turn defeats the duplicate detection described in D-60. FIXED in civicos-ui's ported `mdFor` (writes `content_hash: sha256:<primary>` whenever a document is present, absent for typed intake where inventing one would be a lie) and the live bundle was repaired by revision. **NOT fixed in `bio-plane/src/setup.mjs`**, which is the installer's own copy and the one a new group gets, so every bundle created through the installer wizard with a captured document has the same hole. RESOLVED 2026-07-31 (REC-3): `bio-plane/src/setup.mjs` `mdFor` now emits `content_hash: sha256:<primary capture sha>` whenever a document is attached (gained an `src` param + one caller update), absent for typed intake, mirroring the civicos-ui port. Verified via `test/setup-honesty.test.mjs`: the served SETUP_HTML carries the emission, and checkBundle (the authority) confirms a document bundle WITH content_hash clears the C-2.7 "verified state requires a well-formed content_hash" finding that WITHOUT it refuses release; negative control RUN (drop the line → 3 fail). | resolved 2026-07-31, REC-3 |
| D-63 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **Only three stacks have been measured, and every unmeasured one is a handler waiting for evidence.** `docprofile/` now has a structure for recognising a document's stack and kind and deciding what is evidentiary, what is furniture and what is per-render machinery, and it is validated against ASP.NET WebForms (Legistar), WordPress (Oaklandside) and a client-rendered app (OpenGov). Everything else in Oakland's orbit falls to the conservative handler, which is safe and noisy: Drupal, plain static HTML, Granicus, CivicPlus, NextRequest, Squarespace, and the county's own sites. The work per stack is small and specific: fetch the same page twice, diff, attribute the differences, decide whether the document has an identifiable boundary, and write the handler with the measurement in its comments. The noise the conservative handler makes on an unmeasured source is the signal that the source needs a handler, so this row is a standing invitation rather than a blocker. Do not add a rule without a measurement: a careless rule hides a real change, which is the only failure mode here that matters. | M2 · open, ongoing |
| D-64 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **A client-rendered page is recognised as a shell and still cannot be captured as evidence.** `docprofile`'s `client_rendered` handler detects the case structurally (a body with no links and no prose) and carries a member-facing warning that what was collected is the frame and not the figures. That is the honest interim and not the requirement. The standing ruling is that JS-rendered content IS the content and must be captured at the SAME GRADE as the rest of the document, because the render happened in the site's own execution environment at capture time while the HTML/CSS rendition is rendered later in the reader's. That needs the Browser Rendering path, which is available on Workers Free at 10 minutes a day. Until it lands, `oaklandca.opengov.com` and every portal like it can be captured and cannot be cited, and the record should say so on the document page rather than only at capture time. Blocked behind D-55 (per-origin authority for a rendered capture) for anything carrying third-party script output. | M2 · BLOCKED on D-55 · open |
| D-65 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **Monitoring contracts exist and nothing consumes them.** `docprofile/monitoring.mjs` decides, per document kind, whether to watch substance, membership, or nothing at all, and diffs an index's entries into removed / altered / added with removal and alteration as events and addition as routine. Validated against real Legistar rows. The PLANE's watcher does not call any of it: monitoring still compares raw hashes on every kind of document alike, so a delisted meeting is invisible and a reissued viewstate reads as a change. Two further pieces belong with the adoption. The daemon's per-bundle monitor FREQUENCY should follow the document kind, because a delisting is time-sensitive and a record rarely moves, and the current one-frequency-fits-all is what makes the noise expensive. And a confirmation needs somewhere to land: "all 18 entries still present and unchanged" is dated first-party evidence that nothing was withdrawn, which is the primary contemporaneity route's raw material, and today it would be computed and discarded. | M3 · open |
| D-66 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **Only one content type is written, and the others are named but unmeasured.** `docprofile/doctypes/` has the layered structure and `meeting_calendar`, written entirely from a page that was fetched, diffed and read. Bob named the others directly and each needs the same treatment before it is written: a regulation or ordinance (what is an amendment versus a renumbering), a staff directory (a title change may matter, a phone extension may not, and departure is the significant one), an agenda (an item added or withdrawn before a meeting, and the relationship to the minutes that follow), meeting minutes (attendance, votes, and continuances), and a budget or dataset. The generic fallback reports change without describing it, which is noisy and honest, and the noise is the prompt. DO NOT write a type from what a document probably looks like: a content type invented rather than measured reassures people about things it has not understood, which is the one failure mode that matters here. | M2 · open, ongoing |
| D-67 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **Connections are emitted and nothing stores, relates or shows them.** `meeting_calendar.connections()` produces referential and temporal edges from a real calendar (measured: 39 referential, 11 temporal from one page), including the valuable form, an absence with a due date, such as minutes not published three weeks after a meeting. Nothing consumes them. Three pieces are missing. STORAGE: the plane has `links_to` in `REL_VOCAB` for source-asserted links, and these are SYSTEM-asserted inferences about meaning and sequence, which is a different `asserted_by` and probably a different vocabulary. AGEING: a temporal absence with an `expected_by` needs something to notice when the date passes, which is monitoring's job and not the parser's. PRESENTATION: Bob was explicit that the two kinds are understood and processed differently by people and their assistants and must be presented differently in the UI, and the document page currently has one link surface built for source-asserted links only. | M4 · STORAGE DONE (FW-8, fe81904): connections now PERSIST (connections table, op=connect/connections). Relating/showing (ageing + presentation) remain — slice B + Steps 6/8. |

| D-72 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Connections have no GRADE, so a case cannot show which of its links are the weak ones.** RULED by Bob: BIO's contribution should "improve the grade of connections overall". Framework §8.1 defines the scheme on the model of capture grade: A, the source's own identifier with both ends captured; B, an identifier the source uses matched exactly at both ends; C, correspondence such as a name or a date proximity, plausible and never presented as established; D, asserted with no captured basis, recorded as testimony with an author. Nothing implements it. Two distinctions must survive implementation, because both are easy to lose: grade is NOT credibility, since a Grade D connection from a member who was in the room may be the most valuable thing in a case and is labelled by author rather than discounted; and grade is not `asserted_by`, since the author says who claims it while the grade says what would be needed to check it. The value is that grade is IMPROVABLE, and raising it is work a machine does well and a person does slowly. | M4 · CLOSED for connections (FW-8, fe81904): every connection carries the §8.1 grade = weaker of its two ends; established derives from it structurally. |
| D-73 | design | 2026-07-30 | **The connection table models a pair and the domain needs a chain.** RULED by Bob: the meeting-agenda-minutes sequence is ONE form of connected data, and the system must support many types of happenings and progressions, his example being need, budget request, budget approval, RFP, responses, award, signed contract. Framework §8.2 generalises the table into progressions with stages carrying `after`, cardinality, interval and required-ness. The construct that falls out and did not exist before is the MISSING PREDECESSOR: a later stage present with no earlier one. It is usually sharper than a missing successor, because late minutes are an administrative lapse while an award with no solicitation is a question about how public money was committed, and it is epistemically stronger, since a missing successor may simply not have happened yet while a missing predecessor either exists somewhere or never existed and both answers are worth having. Also needed: exception documents that lawfully discharge a skipped stage, so the question becomes "where is the document saying this may be missing" rather than "why is this missing"; and junction checks as data, which is where a case actually gets made. | M4 · CHAIN DONE (FW-8 rule+2-node, FW-9 f000854 N-stage instances with weakest-grade inheritance). That is precisely where the most consequential progressions live. The lever is that institutions DO reuse certain identifiers across systems, and finding which ones is empirical work exactly like measuring a host stack: a contract or purchase order number, a project or CIP number, a resolution or ordinance number, an APN, a fund or account code. Each identifier found in two systems converts an entire progression from Grade C to Grade B. | D-75 | design | 2026-07-30 | **The content framework and the object catalogue have been developed independently and have never been connected.** Framework §12, added at Bob's direction that the system must support goals, objectives and aspirations: the record already models intent as `project` (which carries an `objective` field, states `forming → investigating → matured → closed`), `focus` and `problem` (open questions and obstacles), and `action` (steps). The framework was written through eleven sections without touching any of it. Two object types genuinely do not exist and are needed: ASPIRATION, a standing commitment that never closes and sets priority, and GOAL, a bounded pursuit that eventually does. Conflating the three is the ordinary failure: an aspiration written as an objective is never finished and demoralises, and an objective written as an aspiration is never checked and quietly abandons itself. | M4 · open |

Three things follow and each is worth the work on its own. Progress becomes derived: "41 of 58 at Grade B, 12 Grade C for want of a shared identifier, 5 with no solicitation and no exception document" is computed, so nobody has to be trusted about how it is going. The gaps become the WORK LIST, with the 12 naming exactly what measurement would raise them and the 5 being records requests with the request already specified. And an AI assistant working the objective becomes auditable in the same terms as anyone else, since everything it produces carries provenance and grade. | M4 · open |
| D-77 | doctrine | 2026-07-30 | **Goal-directed work is where a case turns into a brief, and nothing guards against it.** Now invariant 7 of the framework: a goal may direct what is SOUGHT but must never filter what is recorded, retained or shown about what was found, and a finding that cuts against a goal must be surfaced at least as prominently as one that supports it. This is the invariant most likely to be violated BY ACCIDENT, because helpfulness looks exactly like it from the inside: an assistant pursuing an objective, ranking findings by relevance to that objective, is one short step from an assistant that quietly does not mention the inconvenient one. `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md` is the doctrine this connects to and neither is implemented. | D-78 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **Both bundle writers hardcode `surfaced_by: human`, so an assistant cannot honestly surface a focus.** RULED by Bob: an assistant may open a focus unattended, because that support is central to what BIO should offer and a focus is informative and advisory rather than committing. RESOLVED 2026-07-31 (REC-3): rather than trust a caller-asserted literal, `surfaced_by` is now SERVER-STAMPED at the trust boundary in `bio-plane/src/index.mjs` op=promote, exactly where author/owner/by/viewer are already stamped — a focus CREATED by a machine credential (viaSession false) records `agent`, one by a member's session `human`, the caller's value overwritten and the bundle.md sha recomputed. Stamped on the creation act; a revision carries the document's value forward. `test/surfaced-by.test.mjs`: a member-surfaced focus records human, an agent-surfaced one agent (the hardcoded `human` does not survive); negative control RUN (force the stamp to human → 2 fail). The DISPLAY half is D-82, still open. | resolved 2026-07-31, REC-3 |

AGE RATHER THAN VANISH: a machine-surfaced focus nobody has acted on after some interval moves to `deferred` with the reason recorded, never silently disappearing, because a finding that disappears is indistinguishable from one that was never made and that rule does not relax because the finder was a machine. Neither the aggregation key nor the ageing job exists. | M4 · open |
| D-80 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Contact between aspirations is not detected, and contradiction is a thing to FIND rather than prevent.** RULED by Bob: contradicting aspirations are welcomed, because we may not realise that they contradict and because we learn from trying to achieve aspirations whether they are achieved or not. What remains to build is detection of CONTACT, not of contradiction, because judging whether two prose commitments contradict is not something this framework can establish and inventing that verdict would violate invariant 5. Contact IS establishable: two aspirations directing work at the same entities, touching the same progressions, or ordering the same queue differently. What counts as contact is named in framework §12.1 and not specified, and the surface that shows it does not exist. | M4 · open |
| D-81 | design | 2026-07-30 | **An aspiration accumulates learning and has nowhere to put it.** Follows from Bob's ruling that we learn from pursuing aspirations whether or not they are achieved. Three properties are the whole value and each is easy to lose. A DEAD END IS KEPT, since "we assumed the fund code would appear in the procurement portal and it does not" is the kind of thing every member relearns individually and nobody writes down, and it is exactly what makes the next member's work cheaper. It is NOT A PROGRESS BAR, since an aspiration has no completion; objectives beneath it carry satisfaction conditions and derived progress, the aspiration itself does not. And it SURVIVES ABANDONMENT: retiring an aspiration after two years should be an act that captures what it taught rather than one that archives a folder. | M4 · open |

| D-83 | design | 2026-07-30 | **The bias doctrine's SUBJECT REGISTRY and the framework's entity axis are the same construct, and are on course to be built twice.** `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md` safeguard 4 requires a subject registry: a bundle of entries for sources, institutions, offices and movements, each with aliases, plus declared relations between them (`proxy_for`, `member_of`, `overlaps`), each justified and citable.
