| D-84 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **`object_type: bias` does not exist in the check catalogue, so a bias bundle cannot be written.** The catalogue carries information, focus, project, action and problem. `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md` specifies bias sets as bundles of `object_type: bias` precisely so they inherit append-only history, member-authored transitions, convergent promotion and conformance checks without inventing governance. The doctrine is three days old, fully worked, and unimplementable at the first step. Small: a type, its states, its heading set, and the statement-anatomy checks (kind in the closed set of three, subject referencing the registry, justification required, citation required for kind=pattern, and the malformedness refusal). **SCOPE ADDED 2026-08-04 by CONDUCT, enacting DEC-54 (Bob) — whoever closes this row must build to these four, and they are here rather than only in the register because a design recorded where the decision was made reaches only the person who already knows it.** (a) An INHALED external policy **SPLITS** into bars (`required_strength`) and bias statements (scrutiny / inference / pattern): one document contains both, and conflating them breaks the gate/disclose distinction DEC-20 rests on. (b) **The UNENFORCEABLE RESIDUE is a first-class published output, not a log line** — a case saying "held to AP's standards" must ALSO say which of those standards this system does not check. The evidence is in `research/SEARCH-COMPLETENESS.md`: the extractable rules are the countable ones, and in four of five documented verification failures **the countable rules were formally satisfied while the uncountable properties failed** — so enforcing the extractable half silently would deliver enforcement of precisely the part that does not protect, wearing the authority of the whole policy. (c) **INHALE MEANS PROPOSE FOR ADOPTION, NEVER INSTALL** — adoption is an authored, attributed act (DEC-46, D-90, D-82), because otherwise adopting a policy LAUNDERS a standard: "we follow BBC standards" with nobody in the group having authored anything, which is the never-prefill violation wearing a compliance badge. (d) **The adopted policy is PINNED — source, retrieval date, content hash — and a published case names the VERSION it was held to.** An external policy moves; a case published under it must stay checkable after it moves. That is DEC-12's edition pattern and D-183's calibration pattern at a third altitude, and it is what makes REGRADE meaningful across organisations: two lenses, both pinned, both re-runnable. | M4 · open |
| D-85 | design | 2026-07-30 | **An assistant-surfaced focus must carry the bias manifest in force when it was surfaced, and nothing does.** Follows from two facts that met this session. Bob ruled an assistant may open a focus unattended; the bias doctrine says any BIO work done under bias carries the fully declared bias as part of that work's evidentiary record. The assistant is doing BIO work, the effective statement set shaped what it scrutinised and which inferences it could draw, and unlike a member it will not remember. Without the manifest a focus surfaced under one lens is indistinguishable later from one surfaced under another, and bias debt cannot be computed against it. This also answers a question the doctrine leaves open: an assistant has no bias of its own to declare, it has the effective set it was run under plus the objective it was pursuing, and recording both is the whole of its obligation. | M4 · open |
| D-86 | design | 2026-07-30 | **Bias debt and temporal expectations are the same shape and should share one mechanism.** Bias debt: the lens changed, so this analysis owes a re-run, and it may not advance its workproduct_state or be ratified until the debt is settled; tracking continuous, settlement batchable. Temporal expectations from framework §8.2: this progression stage is due and has not arrived. Both are an obligation with a clock, attached to an object, blocking a state transition, settleable in batches. Two schedulers would be two sets of bugs about the same thing. Step 7 of the CONSTRUCTS plan is where the ageing mechanism gets built and is the moment to decide it once. **PARTLY SETTLED 2026-07-31 (REC-8, Step 7): the TEMPORAL half is built and the mechanism is decided — an `overdue-scan` consumer on REC-1's reconciling DO alarm detects an overdue required successor (derived on read against an injectable clock, no stored flag), surfacing an `overdue_successor` proposal. The bias-debt half is DEFERRED and rides the SAME consumer shape (an obligation-with-a-clock, batch-settleable) with a different producer — a bias-debt sweep would register beside `overdue-scan` and inherit the reconcile/self-termination, so the "one mechanism, not two" ruling holds. What remains open under D-86 is building that bias-debt producer.** | M4 · open |
| D-87 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A pattern statement's evidence is cited once and never revisited, so a bias can outlive its justification unremarked.** Bob's reframing: a side-effect of this framework's work should be that evidentiary comments accrue to bias records, measuring the extent to which the bias is JUSTIFIED. The doctrine requires a `pattern` statement to cite evidence and refuses to let one leave draft without a citation; what it lacks is TIME. A statement declared in March citing three instances is a different object by August, either because those three became forty or because they stayed three while sixty went the other way, and nothing notices either. This is the INVERSE of bias debt: bias debt says the lens changed so the analysis owes a re-run, this says the evidence changed so the lens owes a re-examination. The second is the one nobody builds because it is uncomfortable, and a group may keep a statement whose grounds have evaporated provided it says why, but the decay must not go unremarked, since a bias whose grounds have quietly evaporated is undeclared in the way that matters. | M4 · open |
| D-88 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A bias statement should be able to carry a MEASURABLE FORM, and it is the same construct as an objective's satisfaction condition.** Framework §13.1. Most pattern statements are not mechanically measurable and must stay analysis; some are, and those should say so, with the measure expressed in the framework's own vocabulary. Three properties keep it honest and each is easy to lose. The measure NEVER EDITS the statement: text and justification stay a member's authored act and the standing measure is a derived dated attachment, because a machine that could rewrite doctrine would be worse than the problem it solves. The scope is REGISTRY-DEFINED and never hand-picked, since a cherry-picked denominator would let a group manufacture authority for a bias, which is the distortion the malformedness rule fights arriving by the back door. And it is reported WHICHEVER WAY IT CUTS, which is the case where invariant 7 actually costs something, because the value of the feature is entirely in the measures that refute. Build it once with §12's satisfaction-condition evaluator. | M4 · open |
| D-89 | doctrine | 2026-07-30 | **Bias must never shape what is captured or monitored, only how conclusions are weighed.** Framework §13.1 settles a question v0.8 asked badly. A pattern statement's evidence comes from monitoring; if monitoring configuration were itself shaped by a lens, a measure computed over that collection would prove only that the bias had been thorough. The fix is to forbid the coupling rather than document it: a scrutiny statement must not cause the system to collect less from a source, or more. This is invariant 7 applied to the bias doctrine itself, and it is what keeps the whole of §13.1 from being circular. Nothing enforces it today because neither bias binding nor per-source monitoring configuration exists, which makes now the cheap moment to write the constraint down rather than discover it later. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open |
| D-90 | doctrine | 2026-07-30 | **Measure decay is loud and never blocking, which is the general rule that derived things inform and authored acts bind.** RULED by Bob, by pointing at the principle already written: the measure never edits the statement, so a measure that may not edit a statement certainly may not block work resting on one. Now invariant 8 of the framework, and it runs through more than bias: an assistant's proposal, a connection grade, a recogniser's confidence all report and none decides. The asymmetry with bias debt is exact rather than arbitrary. Bias debt blocks because the GROUP changed the lens by an authored act and the block is what makes that amendment mean something; a decayed measure means the WORLD changed and nobody decided anything, and an institution improving its behaviour must not be able to freeze a group's in-flight work. The path from decay to a block runs through a person: the measure reports, a member amends or retires the statement, that amendment is authored, and it generates bias debt in the ordinary way. A group that reads a collapsed measure and keeps the statement anyway has done something legitimate and recorded. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open |
| D-91 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **A PDF is captured as bytes only: no content, no links, no structure, so the document class this project exists for cannot participate in the citation graph.** RULED by Bob: content should be identified and processed in other document types exactly as it is in HTML. Today `op=acquire` hits the `NOT_HTML` guard at `index.mjs` and a PDF yields a hash and nothing else. Agenda packets, staff reports, budget exhibits and ACFRs are PDFs, and an agenda PDF linking to a dozen item PDFs is precisely the graph CAPTURE exists to build. Mapping onto machinery that already exists: `/Annots` with `/Subtype /Link` and a `/URI` action are the `<a href>` equivalent; named destinations and `/Dest` are the `anchor` partition; embedded files are `intra`; page number plus annotation rectangle is the element reference. Link annotations are cheap and need no font handling; Workers has `DecompressionStream("deflate")` natively so FlateDecode costs no bundled code. Text extraction is the expensive half (glyph-to-Unicode through embedded CMaps) and Bob is right that libraries solve it: `unpdf` is a serverless pdf.js build targeting Workers. The real constraint is bundle size against the 3MB Free worker limit and CPU against the measured ceiling, both MEASURABLE rather than arguable, and an earlier claim here that it was "expensive and failure-prone" was a guess and is withdrawn. DECIDED: CAPTURE extracts structure and hands FRAMEWORK a parsed object tree; `docprofile` decides what the content is, because content identification is FRAMEWORK's subject regardless of container. **PHASE 1 LANDED 2026-07-31** (area CONTENT-PDF, commit 0b1c6ce): `bio-plane/src/pdfstructure.mjs` extracts the outbound-link graph — `/Annots` link annotations classified into the SAME anchor/intra/deferred/refused partitions HTML uses (wrappers byte-identical to subresources.mjs, asserted; plus a first-class `undetermined`), resolving classic objects, FlateDecode, and object streams, and reading a page's `/Rect` + index as the element reference. 46-assertion suite with a run negative control; producer-side shape registered as I2 (provisional) in INTERFACES.md. NOT DONE: text extraction (glyph→Unicode via `unpdf`) is phase 2 and gated on measuring bundle size vs the 3MB limit and CPU vs the ceiling; and live-verify awaits `op=pdfstructure`, a recorded DELEGATION to CAPTURE. **OP WIRED 2026-07-31** (CAP-1, commit 2ab62f4): `op=pdfstructure` serves the structure over the `op=capture` byte path, read-only, same auth. **PHASE-2 MEASURED → GO 2026-07-31** (CPDF-1, MEASUREMENTS.md): `unpdf` bundles to ~2.9MB raw / 0.71MB gzip — FITS the 3MB Free limit with 2.29MB gzip headroom; node-proxy extraction ~0.7–0.9 ms/page. Text extraction (CPDF-2) now in progress. STILL OPEN: unpdf/pdf.js wants the whole document in memory — exactly what I1's range reads avoid for large PDFs — so the authoritative large-PDF Worker-CPU/memory ceiling needs a deployed probe; oversized PDFs are recorded text-undetermined. | M2 · open; phase-1 + op wiring landed, phase-2 GO, text extraction in progress |

| D-98 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **There is no task or inbox mechanism, so the authority-determination ruling has nowhere to land.** RULED: undetermined authority becomes a task for the project manager, falling back to a group admin where a project has no manager, forwardable to a member better placed to attest. Every user has a todo list behaving like an inbox, and Bob has flagged that its transport and UI surface might later BE email. Consequences: field bounds must be enforced at WRITE time rather than at render time, because an email renders in a client we do not control where a plausible instruction is what phishing looks like; and the record must be transport-agnostic. Build on the gathering queue's shape, reusing C-18.5's grammar bounds and the quoted-data exporter, since the F5 threat is identical. `member_expertise` already supplies routing. Same underlying gap as D-52, which records that BIO has no notification channel at all. GROUNDWORK 2026-07-30: docs/development/INBOX-GRAMMAR.md fixes the field grammar and routing as a CONTRACT, deriving every bound from settled precedent (C-18.5 field-for-field, the RULED routing order, member_expertise's real columns), so this is now a build task rather than a design task. RULED 2026-07-30: an undetermined capture creates a task automatically at capture, through a PRODUCER/CONSUMER QUEUE. The capture path only enqueues (carrying refers_to, F5-bounded subject, timestamp); a separate consumer holds the sole write path to the inbox, applies routing and the C-19.1 grammar, and dedups idempotently on (refers_to, kind). This stops the daemon credential's blast radius at the queue boundary: a leaked capture token can enqueue noise but cannot write a task, set an assignee, or forge history. Transport (Cloudflare Queue vs durable table drained on schedule) is the consumer's choice at build; table-as-queue is the default for staying in the DO and the audit model. Contract in INBOX-GRAMMAR.md updated. RESOLVED 2026-07-31, 0.49.0. Built against INBOX-GRAMMAR.md as a BUILD task: C-19.1 added to `bio-checks` as a sibling of C-18.5, `refers_to` reusing the exported BUNDLE_ID_RE and the locators reusing `isPublicHttpsLocator` rather than restating either, exported for the write path exactly as `checkGatheringGrammar` is, and run at the gate beside it. Catalog 1.16.5 to 1.17.0; `ratify.test.mjs` CORRECTED rather than loosened to a pattern, because the point of that assertion is that a ratification records WHICH catalog judged it. The producer/consumer split is enforced structurally: the capture path reaches only `task_queue`, keyed on (kind, capture_sha) so a re-capture loop dedups against itself, and there is deliberately NO control-plane op for enqueue at all, which is tighter than the contract asked. `taskdrain` is the sole writer. `actor` on every member action is stamped server-side. THREE PLACES THE CONTRACT WAS UNDERSPECIFIED, resolved in the direction its own typing demanded rather than by redesign: (1) `refers_to` is typed as a bundle id but AT CAPTURE NO BUNDLE EXISTS, since op=acquire returns a document that op=promote later files, so the queue event carries a capture_sha and the consumer resolves it through the register; an event whose capture is never promoted WAITS rather than inventing a subject. (2) 'the referred bundle's project' is meaningless for an Information bundle, which is what these captures are, so a project that CITES the bundle supplies the manager, and with no manager and no active admin the task is honestly `unassigned` rather than addressed to a phantom. (3) the dedup is on LIVE tasks, open OR forwarded, not open alone: a forwarded task is still somebody's work and excluding it would let a re-capture spawn a second task for a subject already in flight, which is the flood the dedup exists to prevent. `test/inbox.test.mjs`, 71 assertions, grammar tested both ways with TASK fixtures. A NEGATIVE CONTROL EARNED ITS KEEP: neutering the write-path grammar check left all 67 original assertions passing, because every task the consumer builds from ordinary input is well-formed by construction, so nothing actually proved the write gate fired; an assertion driving it with a capture registered against a non-canonical bundle id was added and the control now fails 3. Live-verified on biosmoke7 0.49.0 in the SCRATCH namespace end to end: a real undetermined acquire enqueued one event and wrote no task, a drain reported it waiting while unfiled, a promote made it resolvable, the next drain created the task, and the task read back off the live instance is CLEAN under the catalog that shipped with it. NOT DONE: nothing drains on a schedule, so a task appears only when op=taskdrain is called. See D-109. | resolved 2026-07-31, 0.49.0 |

| D-102 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **The installer wizard binds no INSTANCE_NAME, so every wizard-installed instance identifies as "instance unnamed" in its user-agent.** Found live: biosmoke7's agent said `instance unnamed` through 0.46.0 and 0.47.0 because the binding never existed; grep confirms `newgroup/src` never writes it. The instance component exists so a third party can throttle one operator instead of a provider, and "unnamed" defeats that. The deploy path is FIXED (deploy.mjs binds INSTANCE_NAME from the slug, pushed to biosmoke7, verified live in the provenance chain); the WIZARD path is not, which is the one a new group gets, the same split as D-62. Admission is unaffected: the 2026-07-30 ladder measured instance as droppable. RULED 2026-07-30: the installer wizard must PROMPT the operator for an installation name at setup, and that name becomes the INSTANCE_NAME component of the UA string. This is not just binding a slug the deploy already knows; it is asking a new group what to call themselves, so a third party (e.g. Akamai, IA) can throttle or allowlist one operator by name. The name needs the same write-time bounds as any UA-bound field, since it ends up in a header a counterparty parses. Until the wizard is updated, the reference instance biosmoke7 is bound as 'development' (deploy.mjs binds it; the live agent already carries whatever the last deploy set, currently biosmoke7 as the slug). SIMPLIFIED AND RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (Bob): the instance name IS the worker name. The wizard already collects it as `slug` (validated by slugOk, used for the script and the subdomain), so there was never a prompt to add: it just had to BIND INSTANCE_NAME from the slug it already holds. Done in newgroup/src/index.mjs on BOTH paths, install and update; binding it on update is what retro-names every copy installed before this existed, which are advertising 'unnamed' today, with no operator action. wizard suite +2, 92 passing. deploy.mjs already binds the same way, so plane and wizard now agree and there is ONE source of truth. CONSEQUENCE NEEDING BOB: the reference instance was hand-bound to 'development' earlier this session, which under this rule is a second name that DRIFTS: the next deploy.mjs run overwrites it with the slug 'biosmoke7'. Either the worker is renamed, or the instance is simply called biosmoke7, or an explicit override is added and the one-source-of-truth rule is knowingly relaxed. Not decided here. RULED and DONE 2026-07-30: the interim 'development' override is REMOVED and the reference instance is bound to its worker name, biosmoke7, verified live in the provenance chain. The rule is uniform with no exceptions: instance name IS worker name, on the plane and in the wizard, for this instance and every one that follows. There is no second name and nothing to drift. | resolved 2026-07-30 |
| D-103 | gap | 2026-07-30 | **The governor's state surface is Durable-Object-internal; no control-plane op exposes it.** governorstate and governorconfig exist as store routes for the plane's own use and for suites, but an operator or the UI cannot ask "which hosts are held and why" or set a per-host appetite without a deploy. Small: an OPS entry each, admin/member read for state, admin write for config. Deliberately not folded into 0.47.0, which was already a schema release. RESOLVED 2026-07-30, staged as 0.48.0 (signed 6946361…, NOT yet deployed, awaiting a grant): op=governorstate (admin/member/probe, read) reports which hosts are held and why, so a member watching a capture stall sees the governor is the reason rather than a broken source; op=governorconfig (admin/probe, mutating) sets a host's appetite and REQUIRES a host so no fat-fingered global change is possible, with an omitted appetite resetting that host to the instance default. governorconfig is a null NEEDS entry (bounded by SESSION_OPS.admin like the roster ops, not a section-5 capability), which the capability completeness test confirms. | D-104 | design | 2026-07-30 | **A governor-refused monitor tick and a source-unreachable monitor tick look the same to the fallback logic that does not exist yet, and they must not.** When the per-host governor holds a monitor tick (D-95), `op=monitor` returns HTTP 429 `HOST_COOLING_OFF` and writes no tick: honest, because the check did not run. But the archive-fallback ruling shipping next (AUTHORITY-AND-TRUST.md: an alternative source counts as a re-fetch for monitoring after three consecutive failures or fourteen days) counts CONSECUTIVE FAILURES, and a governor refusal is OUR OWN politeness declining, not the source being unreachable. If the fallback counts a governed refusal as a failure, sustained self-throttling would trip the archive fetch: we would fetch from IA because WE paced ourselves, which is backwards and also loads IA for no reason. The distinction is exactly the one the user-agent measurement already taught for a different mechanism: unreachable is a property of a host AND an egress, and "we chose not to ask" is neither. FIX WHEN THE FALLBACK IS BUILT: a governed refusal must not increment the source-failure counter; only an outcome the SOURCE produced (a real 4xx/5xx from the origin, a network failure reaching it) counts toward the threshold. Nothing is wrong in the shipped code, because nothing consumes the counter yet; this exists so the counter is built to exclude governed refusals from the first line rather than discovered to include them after a spurious fallback. RESOLVED 2026-07-31, 0.50.0, and built BEFORE the fallback exactly as this item asked, so the exclusion is designed in rather than discovered after a spurious fallback. New table `source_reachability`, keyed on the normalised DOCUMENT address rather than a host, because one page can be gone while the rest of a site answers. The outcome vocabulary is CLOSED (`success`, `source_refused`, `fetch_failed`, `governed`) on the reasoning that the entire value of the table is telling kinds of not-getting-the-bytes apart, and a free string would let a caller collapse that distinction by accident; `failure` is deliberately not a word it accepts. All four sites in the acquire path are wired: the governor's refusal, the network throw, a non-ok origin response, and success. A governed refusal is counted in its OWN column and moves neither `consecutive_failures` nor the staleness clock nor even `attempts`, because the source was never asked. It is counted rather than dropped so the exclusion can be audited: a number excluded from a decision must stay visible, or a reader cannot tell a source nobody could reach from a source nobody asked, and the verdict's `basis` string says which case it is in words. Bob's ruling 2026-07-31 is the reason this matters more than it looks: the governor is meant to keep traffic low enough that being banned is not a concern, so governed refusals are expected to be COMMON, and a counter that mistook them for source failures would fire the fallback hardest exactly when we were being most polite. One design question the ruling left open and this answers explicitly: the fourteen-day arm measures the length of the CURRENT FAILING RUN, not time since last success, so a document nobody has asked about in a fortnight is not eligible while a document that has been failing since a fortnight ago is; thirty days of nothing but governed refusals is asserted not to age a document into eligibility. `test/reachability.test.mjs`, 34 assertions, every read time-pinned through a `now` parameter so the suite tests the rule and not the day it runs. Negative control: letting governed fall through to the failure path breaks 17 of 34. Live-verified through the real op=acquire path on 0.50.0 for both a 200 and an origin 404. | resolved 2026-07-31, 0.50.0 |
| D-105 | unverified | 2026-07-30 | **The Wayback CDX claims the archive-fallback design rests on could not be checked from this session's egress, so they remain a vendor description rather than a measurement.** ARCHIVE-FALLBACK.md asserts the CDX record shape (SURT urlkey, 14-digit timestamp, original, mime, status, base32 SHA-1 digest, length), the `id_` raw-bytes replay suffix, `warc/revisit` dedup records as dated identical-bytes observations, and the 24-to-60/min rate ceilings. `web.archive.org` returned `hostname_blocked` from the Anthropic container egress on 2026-07-30, so none of it was verified here. The plane's OWN egress is Cloudflare and CAN reach IA (it reaches oaklandca.gov), so the archive session should measure these through the deployed plane before building on them, exactly as the host-stack and user-agent facts were measured rather than assumed. Treat every number and field shape in ARCHIVE-FALLBACK.md as unconfirmed until then. MEASURED AND CLOSED 2026-07-31 through the deployed plane at 0.49.0, store=scratch, three requests to archive.org in total. The container block was reconfirmed first (`Blocked by egress policy`), so the plane's egress is the one that was instrumented, which is also the one that will run the fallback. Record shape CONFIRMED field for field: SURT urlkey, 14-digit timestamp, original, mimetype, statuscode, base32 SHA-1 digest, length; and the `id_` suffix returns raw bytes (HTTP 200, 32,564 bytes, text/html, no redirect). Two things came free that the design wanted: the Memento headers (`Memento-Datetime`, `Link` with rel="original" and a timemap) are ALREADY on the replay response, so 'build to Memento not Wayback' is the lower-effort path rather than only the principled one, and `x-archive-src` names the source WARC. THREE CORRECTIONS, all now written into ARCHIVE-FALLBACK.md and MEASUREMENTS.md: (1) CDX `length` is the compressed WARC record size and NOT the body length, measured 6255 declared against 32,564 received, so it is neither a fixity nor a size check on our bytes; (2) a shared digest can mean an EMPTY BODY, since `3I42H3S6NNFQ2MSVX7XZKYAYSCX5QBYJ` is base32(SHA-1(empty)) and appeared twice in a five-row sample on two 301s, which constrains the best idea in the design: reading a `warc/revisit` as a dated identical-bytes observation holds ONLY for a real 200 with a non-empty body, and any contemporaneity claim built on archive digests must exclude the empty digest explicitly; (3) most rows are not 200, three of five sampled were usable and two were empty redirects, so a fallback taking the most recent row without filtering on statuscode and digest will record a redirect as the document. THE RATE FIGURES REMAIN UNVERIFIED ON PURPOSE and that half of this item stays open as D-111: establishing them requires provoking a 429, and the documented consequence of mishandling one is an hour-long block on Cloudflare's SHARED egress falling on unrelated people, which is not a cost this project gets to impose on strangers to satisfy its own curiosity. | measured 2026-07-31; rate figures remain unverified, see D-111 |
| D-106 | defect | 2026-07-30 | **The installer ships a plane thirteen releases behind the live one, so a new group installs 0.35.0.** `newgroup/src/release.mjs` embeds `RELEASE_VERSION = "0.35.0"` while the plane is at 0.48.0. Everything this session shipped is therefore absent from a fresh install: the legible user-agent (D-94, and with it the ability to reach oaklandca.gov at all), the per-host governor (D-95), three-valued authority and the provenance chain (D-97), the observation-source split (D-96), and the governor's operator surface (D-103). A group installing today gets an instance that CANNOT capture from the City and identifies itself as `bio-acquire`, which is the exact failure this session spent three releases fixing. Found incidentally: `npm run build` regenerates the embedded blob, so the artifact showed as modified in the working tree while the declared version did not move. TWO THINGS TO FIX, and they are separable: (a) cut a new installer release embedding 0.48.0, and (b) the embed step should REFUSE when the embedded version does not match the plane's `package.json`, because a silently stale installer is the failure mode that let this drift thirteen releases without anyone noticing. (b) matters more than (a): (a) is a one-time catch-up and (b) is why it will not recur. Sovereign instances are the whole distribution model, so this is not cosmetic. RESOLVED 2026-07-31, and the mechanism was NOT a forgotten build step: `embed-release.mjs` read the embedded version out of `bio-plane/wrangler.jsonc`, and a tree-wide search found that field has exactly ONE reader, this script. `scripts/deploy.mjs` binds `VERSION` from its command line, so every release from 0.36.0 to 0.48.0 deployed correctly while the config literal sat at 0.35.0 untouched. Re-running the old embed today would still have produced 0.35.0, so (a) was not reachable without (b). Fixed: `package.json` is now the declared authority, `resolveVersion` refuses on ANY disagreement in either direction (a wrangler config ahead of package.json is the same drift mirrored, not a newer release), the refusal names both files and prints the exact edit, and it runs BEFORE the esbuild so a mismatch costs a second and leaves no stale artifact. `wrangler.jsonc` bumped to 0.48.0. TWO FURTHER STALE ARTIFACTS surfaced while fixing this, both consistent with the same pattern and both now corrected: `bio-plane/package-lock.json` declared 0.45.0 (three releases behind, self-healed by `npm install`, which means no clean install had been run in the plane tree since 0.45.0), and the CHECKED-IN `bio-plane/dist/bio-plane.bundled.mjs` hashed `ef23a492…`, matching neither 0.48.0 nor the signed asset. So the version lived in four places and three of them had rotted. Only `package.json` and the deploy command line were right, which is precisely why the live plane was correct while everything built from the tree was not. `package-lock.json` is deliberately NOT gated in hygiene: npm rewrites it on any install, so a check there would fail on a stale checkout rather than on a real defect. Two guards for one invariant, deliberately: the embed refusal cannot be bypassed, and a new check in `bio-plane/test/hygiene.test.mjs` fires cheaply at the moment a release bump creates the drift, in the thread that created it, rather than weeks later in whichever thread next builds an installer. `newgroup/test/embed.test.mjs`, 15 assertions, including a guard that resolves the REAL tree; negative control confirms hygiene fails when the drift is reintroduced. VERIFIED: the rebuilt plane bundle is byte-identical to the signed 0.48.0 asset (`69463617…`), so the installer's built-in copy and the repository copy are now the same bytes and both paths install the same plane; deployed installer read back from the account embeds 0.48.0, contains no occurrence of 0.35.0, and still carries `bindings: []`, the structural no-token guarantee. ONE CORRECTION TO THIS ITEM AS WRITTEN: the blast radius was overstated. `selectRelease` prefers a NEWER repository release over the built-in, and `release/RELEASE.json` has carried a correctly signed 0.48.0 throughout (verified this session against stock `ssh-keygen -Y verify`, with altered-bytes and wrong-namespace negative controls both rejecting), so an install with working network and a verifying signature was already getting 0.48.0. The built-in 0.35.0 landed only when the repository was unreachable or its copy failed integrity or signature. The defect was real and worth the priority it was given, but what it actually removed was the fallback, not the common path. | resolved 2026-07-31 |
| D-107 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **The installer has no scripted deploy, and that is part of why D-106 went unnoticed for thirteen releases.** `newgroup/DEPLOY.md` documents deployment as: open the dashboard, select all in the editor, delete, paste `dist/newgroup.bundled.mjs`, Deploy. A manual paste produces no record of what was deployed, no read-back verification, and no place for a version assertion to live, which is exactly the blind spot the plane does NOT have: `bio-plane/scripts/deploy.mjs` refuses to report success until it reads the script back from the account and hashes it against the signed asset. This session deployed the installer with `wrangler deploy` and verified by reading the script back through the API (version, absence of the old version, and `bindings: []`), which worked but is not written down anywhere. The installer deserves the plane's discipline: a script that deploys, reads back, and asserts both the embedded `RELEASE_VERSION` and the empty binding set, since the no-bindings property is a structural security guarantee that a hand-paste into the wrong Worker could silently break. Not urgent while installer releases are rare, but the cost of the manual path is now measured rather than assumed.  REINFORCED 2026-07-31 by a finding that makes this sharper than when it was written: a fresh checkout on Bob's machine was authenticated to a DIFFERENT Cloudflare account (`neologic`, `2f4cabcc...`) than the project's, because `wrangler` takes its identity from an OAuth session stored on the machine and a machine is often logged in for unrelated work. An installer deploy from that state would have SUCCEEDED and put the installer in the wrong account with no error to notice, which is precisely the class of silent wrong outcome the plane's own deploy path refuses to permit. PARTIAL FIX SHIPPED: both wrangler.jsonc files now pin `account_id`, so the repository rather than the machine decides where a Worker goes. `scripts/deploy.mjs` was never exposed, because it takes the account from CF_ACCT and talks to the REST API directly; the gap was always the installer's `wrangler deploy`. The rest of this item still stands, and the account discovery is an argument for it: the installer deserves the plane's discipline, meaning a script that deploys, reads the Worker back, and asserts the embedded RELEASE_VERSION, the empty binding set, AND the account it actually landed in. | M7 · open |
| D-108 | defect | 2026-07-31 | **`deploy.mjs` verifies the deployed BYTES, but a live Durable Object can keep serving the previous code after the verification passes.** Found immediately after deploying 0.49.0: `op=tasks` answered `unknown op: tasks` from the live instance while the deployed script demonstrably contained the op, and the same call succeeded minutes later with no redeploy in between. The error format is the DURABLE OBJECT's (`unknown op: <op>`), not the control plane's (`unknown op`), which locates it precisely: a fresh Worker isolate accepted the op and forwarded it to a DO still running the 0.48.0 route map. The deploy script's discipline is right about what it checks and silent about what it does not: it reads the script back and hashes it, which proves the BYTES landed, and `/version` is served by the Worker rather than the DO, so a post-deploy check of the version endpoint reports success while every DO-routed op still answers with the old map. That window is invisible to both checks. This is not the deploy script being wrong, it is the verification being narrower than the claim it appears to make. The fix is a post-deploy probe that reaches an op THROUGH the Durable Object and asserts the new surface answers, retrying briefly rather than once, the same fail-closed patience `deployed()` already applies to the upload. Until then, a session that verifies a release in the first seconds after deploy can conclude a shipped op is missing when it is merely not yet cycled, which is exactly the wrong conclusion to act on. CORRECTED AND WIDENED 2026-07-31 while deploying 0.52.0: the original diagnosis, a Durable Object lagging a fresh Worker isolate, was too narrow. The WORKER itself was stale too. Four calls seconds apart in one command got `/version` 0.51.0, two answers from the previous build, and one from the new one, so the rollout is PER-ISOLATE AND NOT ATOMIC and a verification in that window can receive a mix and reach opposite conclusions about the same property. This nearly produced a false security finding: a forged-locator probe appeared to show the new code honouring a caller-supplied replay locator, when the old code was answering. The requirement is therefore stronger than 'retry until the new op appears': a verification must CONFIRM IT IS TALKING TO THE NEW BUILD before believing any answer, including a failure. `/version` is the cheapest such confirmation, it was already available, and it was already answering wrong when first asked. Nothing here is a fault in deploy.mjs, which verified the bytes correctly every time; the whole gap is in what is done after it returns.  RESOLVED 2026-07-31, 0.53.0: `scripts/deploy.mjs` now waits for the instance to SERVE the version before reporting done, and says loudly when it does not. It is not a pass/fail on the deploy, because the deploy succeeded; it is a gate on believing anything measured afterwards. It earned its keep on its first real use: deploying 0.53.0 it printed ROLLOUT NOT CONFIRMED after 60s, which was correct, and the version appeared shortly after. On success it still says that only the WORKER is confirmed and Durable-Object-routed ops may lag, because that half is not something the deploy can check without an instance credential it deliberately does not hold. | resolved 2026-07-31, 0.53.0 |
| D-109 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **Nothing drains the task queue on a schedule, so an auto-created task appears only when somebody calls `op=taskdrain`.** D-98 shipped the producer, the consumer and the split; what it did not ship is a trigger. The RULED behaviour is that an undetermined capture creates a task AUTOMATICALLY at capture, and it now does exactly that up to the queue boundary, but the task itself waits for a drain that nothing issues. Draining from the capture path was considered and rejected: it would put the daemon credential back in the position of causing the inbox write, which is the coupling the producer/consumer split exists to break, even though the consumer would still be deciding every field. The natural home is a Durable Object alarm, the mechanism `selections` already uses (`#armSweep`, self-terminating when the table empties), armed on enqueue and re-armed while `task_queue` is non-empty. That keeps the trigger inside the DO and inside the audit model, and it costs an idle instance no timer. Until it exists the inbox is correct but not yet automatic, and the word 'automatically' in the ruling is not fully honoured. | M1 · open |
| D-110 | drift | 2026-07-31 | **`setup.mjs` still explains a refusal the plane no longer makes.** `acquireWhy` maps `NO_AUTHORITY` to "Say who issued the document." D-97 (0.47.0) made authority three-valued and REMOVED that refusal deliberately: a caller who cannot name the issuing party now leaves the capture honestly undetermined rather than being forced to invent an authority to get past the door, which was the false-precision failure the ruling names. The string is unreachable, so it costs nothing today, but it is a copy of a rule that was overturned sitting in the surface a member reads, and the next person to touch that file will read it as current. Small, and worth removing with the D-98 inbox surface when the UI grows one, since a member who sees an undetermined capture will want the task beside it. RESOLVED 2026-07-31 (REC-3): the stale mapping was deleted from `acquireWhy` in `bio-plane/src/setup.mjs`, and the dead reason's token now appears NOWHERE in the served surface (SETUP_HTML) — not in code, not even in the replacing comment, since carrying the name of an overturned rule into the page a member reads is the drift this row names. `test/setup-honesty.test.mjs` asserts SETUP_HTML no longer contains it while the kept refusals (BAD_LOCATOR) still are explained; negative control RUN (re-add the branch → 1 fail). | resolved 2026-07-31, REC-3 |
| D-111 | unverified | 2026-07-31 | **The Wayback rate ceilings are still third-party description and should stay that way until ordinary use tests them.** The 24-to-60 requests per minute figures, the 429 behaviour, and the claim that ignored 429s escalate to an hour-long firewall block are the remaining unverified half of D-105. They were NOT measured on 2026-07-31 and the decision not to measure them is the point of this entry rather than an omission from it: the only way to establish a wall is to hit it, and the documented consequence lands on Cloudflare's shared egress, which is to say on people who have never heard of this project and get no say. Our appetite stays the conservative 24/min because it is OURS to choose; their capacity gets discovered the way D-95 already discovers capacity, by a refusal arriving in the course of polite use and being recorded with its date and instrument. If a figure is ever needed sooner, the honest route is to ask the Internet Archive, not to probe them. Anyone tempted to close this by testing should read the outreach note at the foot of ARCHIVE-FALLBACK.md first. | NOT OURS TO MEASURE · open, and deliberately not measurable by us |
| D-112 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **The archive fallback's decision half shipped in 0.51.0; the capture half is composed by the CALLER rather than by the plane.** `op=archivelookup` applies the fence, governs the CDX fetch, parses, selects and returns `capture_with: {op: acquire, locator, via, documentAddress}`; `op=acquire` now honours `via: 'archive.org'` and the document-address/retrieval-locator split, files on the same `captured_locators` row as a direct capture, and grades the result C. So both halves exist and each is tested, but nothing composes them into one act, which has two consequences worth naming. First, the CDX evidence does not reach the stored provenance chain: `archivelookup` returns the second, unsigned hop and `acquire` does not receive it, so a capture filed this way today carries the one-hop direct chain with `via: archive.org` on the locator rather than the two-hop disclosed chain the ruling requires. Transitive trust is accepted WHERE DISCLOSED, and right now the disclosure is in the lookup response rather than in the record. Second, a caller could in principle call acquire with `via: archive.org` without having passed the eligibility fence at all. Neither is a hole an attacker gains from (the fence protects the Internet Archive from us, not the record from anyone, and grade C is the conservative direction), but the chain gap is a correctness matter and should close before anything files an archive capture into the real record. The fix is one op that runs the lookup and the capture together and threads the hop through, NOT a caller-supplied provenance parameter: a chain hop a caller can hand us is a chain hop a caller can invent. RESOLVED 2026-07-31, 0.52.0. Composed into ONE act rather than two: `op=acquire` with `via: 'archive.org'` and an `address` now runs the eligibility fence and the CDX lookup ITSELF, through the shared `archiveSelect` helper, and builds the second provenance hop from the CDX record that same call fetched. The stored `provenance_chain` is therefore two hops for an archive capture: ours, honest that we fetched the replay address rather than the publisher, and theirs, carrying the CDX evidence with `bound: false` and the reason it is unsigned. A caller supplies no hop, no replay locator, no document address and no grade; the only field written back onto the request body is the locator, and `documentAddress` no longer exists on a request body at all. Guarded at SOURCE in `hygiene.test.mjs` rather than only at runtime, because a runtime test can show one forged request was ignored while a source check shows the field cannot be read: five assertions that nothing reads a documentAddress, a hop, a chain or a grade off the body, and that the hop and address come from `archiveSelect`. Live on 0.52.0: a request carrying `locator: https://evil.example.com/x` and a forged `documentAddress` answers NOT_ELIGIBLE, the fence having run before anything was fetched, stable across three rounds. Authority is deliberately NOT auto-set to the Internet Archive: doing so would make an archive capture 'determined' and publishable without any member ever asserting who issued the document, when the publisher is reached by delegation and disclosed in the chain rather than asserted by us. | resolved 2026-07-31, 0.52.0 |
| D-113 | defect | 2026-07-31 | **`op=purge` reported scope ALL while leaving the tables added since it was written.** Found during close-out: a scratch purge cleared five bundles and answered `scope: "ALL"`, and an open task pointing at one of the purged bundles survived it. That task then referred to a bundle that no longer existed, which C-19.1 refuses as unresolvable, so the purge had produced exactly the inconsistent state the checker exists to catch. A purge that reports ALL and leaves rows behind is worse than one reporting a narrower scope, because the caller believes the store is empty. FIXED in 0.53.0: the whole-store arm now takes `tasks`, `task_queue` and `source_reachability`, `stats()` reports all three so the removal counts can be proved rather than asserted, and the per-bundle arm deliberately touches none of them for the same reason it leaves selections alone. Verified live: the second sweep removed 1 task and 3 reachability rows and left scratch genuinely empty. THE STANDING HAZARD, which is the part worth carrying forward: this list is maintained by hand and there is nothing that fails when a new derived table is added and not added here. D-98's tables were three releases old before anyone noticed. A hygiene-style check that every CREATE TABLE in schema.mjs is either named in purge or explicitly exempted would close the class rather than this instance. | M0 · open as a class; the instance is fixed |
| D-114 | design | 2026-07-31 | **C-18.9 may be gating publication on the wrong field, and the fence was NOT changed because loosening it is irreversible.** Bob, 2026-07-31: "A capture can be published if there are no undetermined authority links in the provenance. So recording that the capture was done through IA is proper, even if the content authority is still undetermined." That distinguishes two things this codebase currently treats as one. The PROVENANCE authority is who served each hop and is always known: we know we fetched it, and we know the Internet Archive served us. The CONTENT authority is who ISSUED the document and is frequently undetermined. C-18.9 as shipped refuses a bundle at or past verified when any provenance document carries `authority_state: 'undetermined'`, which is the CONTENT axis, so on the reading above it is gating on the wrong one. WHAT WAS DONE IN 0.54.0: only the part that was unambiguous and cheap, namely recording `capture.authority: 'Internet Archive'` on an archive capture, which was Bob's actual correction and removes the objection that doing so would smuggle a document into publishability. WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT DONE: C-18.9 was left exactly as it is. Publication is the one act in this system that cannot be undone, because a published hash answers forever by design, so a fence around it is the worst possible place to apply a session's reading of one sentence. If the reading is right, the correct change is to gate on the provenance chain having no undetermined attestor and to let content-undetermined material publish WITH its undetermined state stated, which is a real doctrine shift and belongs in AUTHORITY-AND-TRUST.md over Bob's signature rather than in a commit message. NEEDS BOB.  RESOLVED 2026-07-31, 0.55.0, catalog 1.18.0, after Bob delegated the determination: "You do what you know to be right." The fence now sits on the PROVENANCE chain rather than the content axis. Refused at or past verified: a captured document with no provenance chain, any hop naming no attestor, content authority undetermined AND silent (no dated basis), and authority_state 'determined' with no authority named. Content-undetermined material WITH a dated basis may now be published. The reasoning is the project's own and is written into AUTHORITY-AND-TRUST.md: refusing publication on a missing attribution recreates at the publication gate the pressure to invent an authority that D-97 removed at the intake gate, and moving that pressure later makes it worse rather than better, because by then a member has done the work and wants it out. What a published hash claims is bytes, address, date and route, not that the document is authentic municipal record. The conformance suite was CORRECTED rather than exempted, both ways, and now asserts that a two-hop archive chain passes, which is the point of disclosure. Ratification remains a member's signed act and an undetermined capture still raises an inbox task, so the question still reaches a person; it simply no longer stops the record saying what it honestly holds while the question is open. | resolved 2026-07-31, 0.55.0 |
| D-115 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **The installer installs ONE Worker, and the topology now has a fleet.** Bob's 2026-07-31 decision moves heavy, dependency-laden functions into dedicated single-purpose Workers the plane calls over service bindings, `pdf-worker` first. `newgroup` installs the plane and nothing else, so a group installing after the fleet lands gets an instance whose PDFs silently do less than every description of it — which is D-106's failure exactly, a quietly different install that nobody notices because nothing errors. The installer must install the fleet, bind the services, and verify each member's version on read-back, and the wizard must say what a member being absent costs. | M7 · open |
| D-116 | design | 2026-07-31 | **Version authority must span the fleet, or D-106's drift class returns multiplied.** A version lived in four places and had rotted in three; the fix made `bio-plane/package.json` the single declared authority with `resolveVersion` refusing on ANY disagreement in either direction. A fleet member is a fifth, sixth and seventh place for a version to live, each with its own deploy and its own rollout, and a plane calling a stale sibling over a service binding is a NEW face of D-108: the plane can verify itself current while the member still answers from the previous build, invisibly to both. Needs the authority rule extended to every member and a post-deploy probe that establishes which build ANSWERED on the far side of the binding, not just on this one. | M7 · open |
