| D-118 | unverified | 2026-07-31 | **Service bindings and multi-script accounts on Workers Free are UNMEASURED, and the tiering rests on them.** Workers Paid is an optimisation and never a requirement (RULED), and the sovereign installer puts instances into other groups' accounts, most of which will be Free. Whether a Free account can run a second script, whether service bindings are available on it, and what they cost against the request and CPU budgets are all currently assumptions — the same category as the subrequest ceiling before it was calibrated and the Wayback rate figures that stay third-party. If a Free instance cannot reach `pdf-worker`, Tier 1 is not an optimisation of PDF text extraction, it is the ONLY PDF text extraction such an instance has, which changes Tier 1 from a sizing exercise into a floor. Measure through the plane's own egress before building the Tier 2 path, exactly as D-105 was measured rather than believed. CLOSED 2026-07-31 (CPDF-7), measured on the project's own account (`MEASUREMENTS.md`, the CPDF-7 section): the account is on Workers FREE (the API refused `limits.cpu_ms` with "not supported for the Free plan"), and on that Free plan a SECOND script deploys, a SERVICE BINDING between two Workers deploys and RESOLVES, and a cross-Worker call executes end-to-end for ~1 ms wall-clock / one subrequest (25 binding calls in one Free invocation succeeded). So the conditional does NOT fire: `pdf-worker` (CPDF-6) is viable on Free and central, and Tier 1 (CPDF-4) is not forced to be the floor by any binding limitation. NARROWED, not carried: the one residual Free concern is pdf.js against the 10 ms Worker-CPU/invocation ceiling (measured non-raisable here) — a DIFFERENT axis that already lives as CPDF-1's gated Worker-CPU follow-on and constrains an in-plane Tier 1 equally, so it is not a differentiator. | M2 · closed 2026-07-31 (CPDF-7); the residual CPU-ceiling question stays with CPDF-1's follow-on |
| D-119 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **A reused subresource is never verified against its source, and a ratified bundle can carry one silently.** Asset reuse (0.38.0 onward) spends zero subrequests on furniture the record already holds, which is what makes the 50-request ceiling survivable, and the manifest honestly records `fetched_this_capture: false`. What does not exist is the other half: nothing detects post-hoc that a reused asset later changed, and nothing re-fetches reused parts when a bundle is ratified — the moment its claim changes from working material to evidence. The reuse path's own manifest note TELLS a reader that a ratified capture must re-fetch, and nothing does, so the note describes an obligation the code does not honour. DECIDED 2026-07-31 by session BOB under Bob's delegation, after Bob confirmed the adjacent ruling (a reused asset IS allowed in evidence) and did not recall the mandatory-re-fetch one, which had been recorded once by a session and contradicted by its own document's footer: (a) post-hoc detection from `site_assets` is unconditional and free; (b) at ratification the re-fetch is mandatory as an ATTEMPT AND A RECORD, never as agreement, so `confirmed`/`changed`/`unavailable` all ratify and only silence is forbidden — which is what keeps a source that has gone dark ratifiable, the circumstance the record exists for; (c) a PLAIN GET rather than `If-None-Match`, reversing the conditional-GET suggestion in `CAPTURE-SCALING.md`, because both cost one subrequest and a 304 is the origin's assertion where a hash is our own evidence; (d) a fourth outcome `not_attempted`, recorded with its reason, for parts the invocation budget could not reach, because a ratification that quietly checked 40 of 60 parts claims more than it verified. Full reasoning in `CAPTURE-SCALING.md` item 6. | M2 · queued CAP-4 |
| D-121 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **Spreadsheets, word-processing and presentation documents are captured as bytes only, and the format dispatch cannot absorb them.** Bob's direction: BIO must inspect these as it inspects HTML and PDF, cleanly. Research in `OFFICE-FORMATS.md`. The shape of the finding: `.docx`/`.xlsx`/`.pptx` are ONE container (OOXML = ZIP of XML parts) with three part-maps, and `.odt`/`.ods`/`.odp` are the same shape — so this is not three parsers. MEASURED 2026-07-31 in workerd via miniflare: `DecompressionStream` supports `deflate-raw` and round-trips it (480 raw / 29 compressed / intact), so the container is readable in the plane with ZERO dependency, exactly as `FlateDecode` made PDF phase 1 dependency-free. Outbound links live uniformly in `_rels/*.rels` with `TargetMode="External"` and map onto I2's existing partitions unchanged; embedded files are `intra`. Text is EASIER than PDF, not harder — XML text nodes, no glyph/CMap problem, so there is no Tier 2 and no fleet member for office text. Two element references are BETTER than PDF's page+rect: `Sheet1!B14` and slide+shape are stable and human-meaningful, the first time the record can cite finer than a document without inventing an anchor scheme. THE ARCHITECTURAL POINT: today's dispatch is already TWO mechanisms that do not scale — a hardcoded `HTML_CT` array guarding the acquire-time subresource branch (everything else refused `NOT_HTML`) and a separate read-time `op=pdfstructure`. Three more formats makes five special cases across two mechanisms. The framework already specifies the fix and has never exercised it: §4's uniform recogniser plus a registry per axis, with FORMAT named outright as a candidate axis. So the work is stand up the FORMAT registry and MOVE HTML AND PDF ONTO IT, then add formats as registry entries. | M2 · open |
| D-124 | design | 2026-07-31 | (renumbered from a colliding D-122 by CONDUCT 2026-07-31) **Office formats carry latent evidence HTML and PDF do not, and the same artefacts carry personal data the publisher did not knowingly publish.** From `OFFICE-FORMATS.md`. The evidentiary half is substantial and is why this is more than parity: an XLSX stores the FORMULA (`<f>`) beside its cached value (`<v>`), and for accountability work the derivation is frequently the finding — a PDF or printed copy destroys it permanently; DOCX tracked changes and comments record who changed what and what a reviewer said, which a published PDF is designed to remove; PPTX speaker notes are routinely more candid than the slide; hidden rows, columns and sheets are first-class findings invisible in every rendered form; and `docProps/core.xml` carries creator, `lastModifiedBy`, revision count and instants. The risk is the same artefacts: `lastModifiedBy` NAMES A MEMBER OF STAFF, a tracked change attributes an edit to a person, a comment may be candid about a named individual — none knowingly published, all inside a document the body did knowingly publish. CAPTURING is not in question (the record holds what was served and stripping bytes would break the hash and the premise). SURFACING is a different act with effects on people outside the project, which is the D-77 / invariant-7 neighbourhood and Bob's to rule. RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-5), and the ruling INVERTS the framing: these are public documents, there is no reason to redact anything from a public record, and who edited a document and when IS evidence — it demonstrates the actions of people and departments. So this is not a disclosure risk to be managed but an EVIDENCE SOURCE to be extracted: creator, lastModifiedBy, revision count and instants, tracked changes with author/date/superseded wording, comments with author and date, speaker notes, hidden sheets — structured, projected, indexed and searchable like any other content. The recommendation this row originally carried ("present and not promoted", kept off indexes and facets) was self-defeating: an analyst can only decide what matters if they can find it, and the patterns that make this material valuable are only visible ACROSS documents. Scope is public records; restricted material is D-124. | M2 · open, ruled |
| D-123 | gap | 2026-07-31 | **I2's element reference is PDF-shaped and office formats need a per-container form, which is `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md`'s first real use.** I2 records `source: {page, rect} | null`. A spreadsheet's reference is `{sheet, cell}` and a deck's is `{slide, shape}`. That is a change to a shape FRAMEWORK builds against, so it goes through the change protocol — and `INTERFACE-CHANGES.md` DOES NOT EXIST, deliberately, because `PARALLELISM.md` says writing it before it is needed gets it wrong. This is the moment it is needed, and a session should expect to write the protocol file as part of the work rather than discovering halfway that there is nowhere to file the proposal. CONCRETELY, today's shape is `source: { page: 3, rect: [x0,y0,x1,y1] } | null`; a sheet needs `{ sheet: "Sheet1", cell: "B14" }` and a slide `{ slide: 7, shape: 3 }`. **AND IT IS NOT SAFELY ADDITIVE, which the first reading of this got wrong:** a consumer written as `if (source) usePage(source.page)` does not break loudly on a sheet-cell source, it reads `undefined` and carries on — so the change REQUIRES consumers to discriminate on a kind tag, which is precisely the "shape another area builds against" case the protocol exists for. A genuine fork the proposal must settle: whether the canonical reference is a STRUCTURED union or a per-container STRING (`"Sheet1!B14"`, `"slide7#shape3"`, `"p7#rect(...)"`) with structured fields beside it. The string has a real argument — `Sheet1!B14` is already the form a person recognises, and the citation surface will want to show it — while the structure is easier to validate. ONE WRINKLE WORTH KNOWING BEFORE PROPOSING: FRAMEWORK owns I2, is dormant, and has never consumed it, so nothing breaks TODAY; the whole reason to use the protocol is that registering an interface means not changing it casually later. CONDUCT answers on FRAMEWORK's behalf IN WRITING (protocol step 3), and that answer must be recorded as CONDUCT answering for a dormant area rather than as FRAMEWORK agreeing — the distinction matters when FRAMEWORK wakes and disagrees. AND A REAL RISK OF DECIDING EARLY: M4 brings readings and the entity axis (D-71, D-83), and an element reference may want to BE the same construct as an entity locator rather than a sibling of it. RESOLVED 2026-08-01 as a PROPOSAL, not a unilateral edit, after Bob delegated it as plumbing. THE FACTS CHANGED WHILE IT WAS BEING REASONED, which is why the answer is not the one drafted an hour earlier: I2 went from PROVISIONAL-and-unconsumed to 1.0.0 STABLE, confirmed by its owner FRAMEWORK (FW-1, framework-agent-2, 2026-07-31). A draft decision that it was provisional and unconsumed, so producer-side revision rather than protocol, was obsolete before it was written -- and this row's original claim, that this is INTERFACE-CHANGES.md's first use, turns out RIGHT for a different reason than it gave. So the protocol applies, INTERFACE-CHANGES.md is written (it deliberately did not exist), and the shape is filed as IC-1 PROPOSED rather than edited into a stable interface by a non-owner: `source` becomes a tagged union with a REQUIRED `kind` discriminator and a REQUIRED human-readable `ref`, with `pdf-page` keeping `page`/`rect` byte-for-byte. Not safely additive, and that is exactly why it is a protocol case: `if (source) usePage(source.page)` reads undefined on a sheet-cell source and carries on. Pure-string was rejected (pushes per-container parsing onto every consumer, where silent misreads live) and pure-structure was rejected (forces every display surface to rebuild the human form). D-121 steps 1-2 proceed meanwhile; neither emits a `source`. | M2 - PROPOSED as IC-1, awaiting FRAMEWORK | M2 · open |
| D-124 | design | 2026-08-01 | **DEC-5 settles public records and deliberately does not settle restricted material.** Bob's ruling — surface everything, internal metadata included, because who edited a document and when is evidence — was given for PUBLIC DOCUMENTS, and he scoped it himself: "there may be other situations where they're not and there are restrictions on what should be on the record or redacted." Three classes are visibly outside it and none is answered: a public-records-request response carrying STATUTORY redactions (where the redaction is itself a fact about the body, and re-deriving redacted content from document metadata would defeat a legal protection the record has no standing to override); MEMBER-ORIGIN or confidential-source material, which C-18.1's custody block already treats differently from a public fetch; and material a body published in error and withdrew, where holding it is settled but surfacing its authorship is not. Recorded now, with a TRIGGER rather than a date, so nobody over-applies DEC-5 to a class it never covered and nobody re-raises it before there is anything to reason about. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · deferred — trigger: the first capture that is NOT a straightforward public publication (a records-request response, member-origin material, or a withdrawn document) |
| D-125 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **A member has no notification preferences, and DEC-10's ruling requires them.** RULED 2026-08-01: an overdue progression stage notifies the member who AUTHORED the connection between that instance and a Focus or Project, and the notification must offer three things -- remind me again at a further increment overdue, stop notifying me about this one, stop notifying me about that GROUP. The plane has no state for any of it: `tasks` carries `open`/`forwarded`/`resolved` and nothing per-member and nothing with a snooze clock. Needed: a per-member preference keyed on the thing muted, where the mute key is expressible on the axes the CONNECTION was made on (progression type, body, or Focus/Project) so a member mutes what they mean rather than a bucket the system chose; a snooze whose increment is THE STAGE'S OWN DECLARED INTERVAL rather than a new constant, since the expectation already carries the number and a second one would drift from it. **THE HAZARD, and it is the one to build against first: MUTING IS PERSONAL, DISMISSING IS A RECORD ACT, and they must never be one control.** If 'stop notifying me' also dismisses the finding, one member's inbox hygiene erases a finding for the whole group -- which is the silent disappearance the discovery-loop doctrine forbids (D-79, Step 8b). The finding stands; only that member's notification stops. NOTE the channel itself already exists: D-98's task inbox is the in-app notification channel, so D-52 ('there is no notification channel in this system at all', raised for export notification) is substantially answered by the same mechanism -- one channel, three consumers, which is the D-86 lesson applied rather than a second scheduler's worth of bugs. AMENDED 2026-08-01 (DEC-10 amendment): notifications AGGREGATE PER CASE, so the preference state is needed at TWO levels, not one -- per item and per (member, Focus/Project) group. The group entry is STANDING and ACCUMULATING rather than a stream, which removes any digest-cadence machinery: one live entry per (member, case) while it holds unhandled events, re-notifying only on a snooze increment or when something new lands after the member last looked. AND THE GROUP MUTE CARRIES ITS OWN HAZARD: it must be scoped to the KINDS PRESENT WHEN IT WAS MADE, so a genuinely new kind of event on that case surfaces again -- otherwise 'mute this case' is a permanent blindfold, which is the same silent-disappearance failure as conflating mute with dismiss, one level up. Grouping itself needs no new stored relation: `tasks.refers_to` names a bundle and the case comes from the citation edge that already exists, so the group is DERIVED from the authored connection rather than duplicated beside it. | M4 - open; the inbox half M8 |
| D-126 | design | 2026-08-01 | **About thirty notification generators exist or are designed, with no catalogue, no classes and no item contract.** Inventoried 2026-08-01 in `NOTIFICATIONS.md` at Bob's direction: clock-driven (overdue successor, temporal expectation, bias debt, recheck sweeps, fallback eligibility, capture-session TTL), data-flow driven (undetermined authority, source modified/removed, governed-vs-unreachable, duplicates, link verdicts, post-hoc reuse divergence, completed walked-away captures, partial captures, text-undetermined, client-rendered shells), governance (endorsements, expertise confirmations, the doorbell, export notification, owner rescue, spent invitations) and analysis (assistant-surfaced focuses, missing predecessors, improvable connection grades, satisfaction gaps, measure decay). THE SIZE IS THE ARGUMENT: thirty ad-hoc sites means thirty invented wordings, severities and button sets, and the drift is guaranteed rather than likely. This is the lesson `CONSTRUCTS.md` item 5 already recorded — ad hoc event strings with no registry, 'the same lesson the check catalogue already taught the plane' — so kinds take stable `N-` ids beside the `C-` ids, and this closes that half of D-68. CLASSES ARE THREE, NOT FOUR SEVERITIES: FINDING (true about the world/record, may become evidence), OBLIGATION (a named person must act for the record to proceed), CONDITION (a fact about our own machinery). A severity ladder was rejected because it encodes how LOUD rather than what it MEANS and rots in one direction — everything becomes a warning; warning/error are severity, which is orthogonal and mostly derivable. FINDING and CONDITION must not merge, or our plumbing dilutes the record's findings and the standing rule that a technical complication is never surfaced as a choice is broken. NEEDED: the catalogue with ids, the item contract (summary, detail, BASIS, producer-published options — never a surface-side map, which is the `op=searchfields` drift), and the `per-item` application weight beside `refuse`/`report`, where a retained item carries its reason. | M4 - open; the surface half M8 |
| D-127 | design | 2026-08-01 | **CASE-MAKING is undesigned, and it is what the whole system is for.** Bob, 2026-08-01: "the CivicOS system is about developing grounded, justified, and meaningful analysis and conclusions. These are built upon evidence and findings from that evidence. We haven't dove into that process of case making yet. But it will certainly involve organizing and building upon this substrate and their derivatives." Everything built to date is SUBSTRATE for this: capture produces bytes and provenance, content produces structure, the framework produces meaning, retrieval finds things, and findings accumulate — and nothing composes them into a justified conclusion a reader can check. The pieces that exist and are unconnected to it: `project` carries an `objective` and a state machine; `focus` holds an open question; citations link Information into a Project with `cites`/`corroborates`; the published surface (UI-PLAN U12) describes a case file as a NARRATIVE with print as first-class, and names the sewer case as the proof piece — but that is a RENDERING of a case, not the process of making one. Missing entirely: how a finding is adopted into an argument, how competing or contradicting findings are held together (D-80 rules that contradiction is a thing to FIND rather than prevent), how a conclusion states what it rests on and what would falsify it, how the grade of a case is derived from the weakest link in its chain (D-72's connection grades are the input and nothing consumes them), and how invariant 7 is enforced in practice — a finding that cuts against the case must take the same path as one that supports it. This is not a milestone gap to be filled quickly; it is the layer the roadmap calls ANALYSIS and it deserves its own design pass with Bob, not a session's guess. **RESTATED BY BOB 2026-08-01 AS THE SYSTEM'S RAISON D'ETRE, which makes it the most important open item in the repository:** CivicOS is about telling a story, or equivalently making a case — capturing the data, rigorously extracting meaning, understanding the legal/regulatory/policy framework, making connections, and figuring out how {information, money, responsibility, relationships, decisions, power} is SUPPOSED to flow and how it ACTUALLY does, where it works and where it does not; and a meta-level above all of that, answering questions, making a case, telling a story and TAKING ACTION to affect the living civic system. "There's nothing about CivicOS and how it works that's more important than supporting users traverse that winding path of questioning, exploring, discovering, documenting, and impacting the civic system." **A STRUCTURAL CONSEQUENCE IT EXPOSES IMMEDIATELY: the milestone ladder has no rung for the thing the system is for.** M0-M8 are substrate and surfaces — running unattended, every document class as evidence, knowing and connecting what it holds, search, leaving, installing, reaching. Not one is "a member can make a case." Read against this statement the ladder is upside down: case-making is not M9 appended after the substrate, it is what the other eight SERVE, and the ladder should say so. Fixing that belongs INSIDE the design pass rather than before it, because the rung's shape is what the pass produces. **And note the fifth verb, which no milestone and no construct touches at all: IMPACTING.** Questioning, exploring, discovering and documenting all have substrate; taking action to affect the civic system has none. **THE PASS IS OPEN as `architecture/BIO_Case_Making_v0_1.md`** (2026-08-01), recording Bob's framing as he gives it plus the observations it raises; nothing in it is settled and nothing builds from it yet. FOUR THINGS ALREADY SURFACED THERE. (a) The constructs CONFLATE WORK WITH OUTPUT while the substrate already draws that line: focus / project / action describe work, a case file is an output, and the two-bucket fence plus ratification is exactly that distinction load-bearing in the plane and unexpressed in the member vocabulary. (b) **A PREVIOUS CLAIM HERE THAT IMPACTING HAS NO SUBSTRATE WAS WRONG and is corrected: `action` IS it** — states planned/active/awaiting_response/resolved/abandoned, headings including `## Correspondence`, fields `action_kind`, `risk_tier`, `counterparty`. Unmistakably outward-facing and in the catalogue throughout. What is missing is not the object but its CONNECTIONS: nothing links an action to the findings that justified it, the case it advances, or the outcome it produced, so the record can hold 'we wrote to the city' and cannot say 'because of these findings, and here is what came back'. The loop from evidence to action to CONSEQUENCE is unbuilt, and the consequence half is itself new evidence about how the system responds — D-128's declared-versus-actual flow measured on our own intervention. (c) Audience difference is probably RENDERING rather than four case types, and the sharp form of it is CLAIM STRENGTH: a journalist can publish 'records suggest', a lawyer needs 'the record establishes', so a case carries claims with derived strength and a rendering selects what clears the audience's threshold and says what it excluded. That gives D-72's unconsumed connection grades a consumer and gives invariant 7 a MECHANISM rather than an exhortation. (d) **~~The government-administrator archetype INVERTS the threat model~~ — STRUCK 2026-08-01, and the row's own correction is recorded here because this is the row a case-making session reads first.** Bob ruled the same day that BIO's doctrine contains nothing resembling "stick it to the man", the objective is BETTER GOVERNMENT, and all stakeholders are assumed to want better outcomes in the public interest — bad actors being real and identified BY EVIDENCE rather than assumed by role. So an administrator is a stakeholder like any other and the question was malformed. THE THREAT MODEL IS SYMMETRIC AND ITS MORE DANGEROUS HALF IS OURS: someone may damage a publisher, and someone may claim far beyond what the evidence supports — and almost every hard-won rule in this repository defends against the second. What remains open is narrower: in an administrator's instance, what does the two-bucket fence protect and from whom? Full reasoning in `BIO_Case_Making_v0_1.md` section 4. The stale claim was — every doctrinal assumption here is outsider-looking-in, and for an administrator the subject and the user are the same institution, so the adversarial fence protects nothing while the impact available is larger. Raised, not answered; it is doctrine. | DOCTRINE (Bob) · open — the design pass, and it is the TOP of the ladder |
| D-128 | design | 2026-08-01 | **The analytic product of progressions is the DELTA between declared and observed flow, and nothing names it as such.** Bob, 2026-08-01: "Obligations are the flows (edges) that go on in a living civic system. Understanding those flows, evolving that understanding, and differentiating between how things are supposed to flow and how they really flow (and the implications of those differences)." This corrects how the OBLIGATION class was first written in NOTIFICATIONS.md — as "a named person must act for the record to proceed", which is a MEMBER TASK and conflated two objects. The CIVIC obligation (the body's declared duty: minutes within N days, an award follows a solicitation) is the primary thing and is a model of how the institution is SUPPOSED to work; the member task is downstream plumbing that frequently arises BECAUSE a civic obligation was observed unmet. Naming them one thing models our inbox and calls it the institution. WHAT FOLLOWS: a progression definition is the DECLARED flow, its instances are the OBSERVED flow, and the divergence has three shapes — missing predecessor (D-73's sharper case), overdue successor (DEC-10), and out-of-order stage (named here for the first time; nothing models it). The delta is not a by-product of progressions, it is the reason to have them, and it needs somewhere to live: a flow model per institution that EVOLVES as understanding does, since a group's picture of how a body is supposed to work is itself a claim that gets refined and should carry its own basis. D-74's shared-identifier measurement is what makes cross-system flows observable at better than Grade C. | M4 · open |
| D-129 | design | 2026-08-01 | **`undetermined` conflates two different claims: WE DO NOT KNOW, and THERE IS POSITIVELY NONE.** Found by the practice survey (`PRACTICE-SURVEY.md`): Wikidata distinguishes `somevalue` (unknown) from `novalue` (positively no value) as separate ASSERTED statements, and BIO blurs them under one word. The conflation is real in our own code, not theoretical — a scanned PDF with no text layer is `novalue` (there is no text to extract, permanently, a property of the document) while a PDF over the Worker envelope is `somevalue` (there IS text and we did not get it, a limitation of our run that a later run could lift). Both currently surface as text-undetermined with the difference only in a reason string. The same split is latent elsewhere: an authority nobody can establish versus a document that genuinely has no issuing authority; a link whose verdict cannot be established versus one established to have no relationship. WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND TIDINESS: the standing rule is that `undetermined` is first-class and must be STATED, and its value to a reader is that it says what we do not know — but 'we could not read this' and 'there is nothing here to read' are different claims about the world, and a member deciding whether to re-capture, escalate or move on needs to tell them apart. One is worth retrying; the other never will be. Cheapest correct shape is probably a second field beside the reason rather than a new vocabulary, but that is a design call for whoever takes it. | M2 - open **WIDENED 2026-08-04 by the archival-systems survey (`STORE-AS-CACHE.md`): the useful state set is LARGER than two, and `partial` is the state BIO most obviously lacks.** Software Heritage stores crawl outcomes explicitly as `OriginVisitStatus.status` in {`created`, `not_found`, `ongoing`, `failed`, `partial`, `full`} — so *we went to this origin at time T and it was not there* is stored DATA, never an inference from missing rows (docs.softwareheritage.org/devel/apidoc/swh.storage.interface.html). Memento (RFC 7089) goes further and makes an archived 404 a CITABLE Memento, MANDATING that a replayed 4XX/5XX carry the same status code, with CDX indexing it in `statuscode` beside the 200s. **`partial` is not hypothetical for us: it is CPDF-5's measured Tier-1-at-88% case and every over-envelope document**, both of which today collapse into the same `undetermined` as a document we never read at all. The governing finding of that survey bears directly on this row — *systems built as ARCHIVES record absence; systems built as CACHES treat absence as a transient failure to be retried* — so this row is load-bearing for the read-through-cache framing rather than tidiness. **SHARPENED AGAIN 2026-08-04 by the caching-systems survey: the principled set is FOUR states with GRADED RETENTION, and there is an AUTHORITY rule BIO already believes in another vocabulary.** RFC 2308 (*Negative Caching of DNS Queries*) separates: the name does not exist (NXDOMAIN, SOA-derived retention, *"one to three hours… work well"*, over a day *"problematic"*); exists-but-not-this-record (NODATA); **we asked and could not tell (SERVFAIL — MUST NOT be cached longer than five minutes)**; and the server was unreachable (same five-minute bound). So: `NEVER_LOOKED` / `LOOKED_ABSENT` / `LOOKED_INDETERMINATE` / `PRESENT`, plus SWH's `partial`. **An indeterminate result must never inherit a definitive lifetime** — CloudFront's error floor is 10 s, Google Cloud CDN publishes 120 s for 404/410/451 and 60 s for 405/501. HTTP encodes the same confidence split more weakly and has NO wire representation for *we have not looked*, which is exactly this row's conflation. **AND RFC 2308 §5 IS THIS PROJECT'S OWN DOCTRINE, ARRIVED AT INDEPENDENTLY IN 1998: *"Negative responses without SOA records SHOULD NOT be cached"*** — the SOA is the proof of AUTHORITY that makes a negative answer a FINDING rather than an absence of finding, so an absence with no authority attached is not recordable at all. That is *an equality that costs nothing to produce is not evidence*, applied to absence. |

| D-131 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **A raw NUL byte in `store.mjs` makes the repo's largest source file INVISIBLE to the shell's `grep`, silently.** Found while verifying a research pass. `bio-plane/src/store.mjs:3833` builds a composite dedup key `sk + <NUL> + cs` and writes the separator as a RAW BYTE (offset 230,100) rather than the escape `\u0000`. The intent is sound (NUL cannot occur in either component) and the runtime value is identical; the damage is entirely in tooling. `grep` in this environment wraps ugrep with `-I` (skip binary), one NUL makes the whole file binary, and every grep against the store core returns **exit 1, no output, no warning** — a false negative indistinguishable from 'no matches'. Confirmed by differential: the shell's `grep -c alarm` returns nothing while `/usr/bin/grep -c` and `command grep -c` both return 45. THIS IS THE PROJECT'S FAVOURITE DEFECT CLASS, third instance: `node --check` does not catch it, every test passes, and a verification tool silently stops verifying (siblings D-24, D-101 — a stray backtick terminating a template literal, a balanced pair defeating `node --check`). It matters because sessions verify claims about `store.mjs` with grep constantly: the instrument said the code was not there and the code was there, nearly causing a CORRECT research finding to be discarded. FIX: write the separator as the escape, and add a hygiene assertion that no file under `bio-plane/src/` contains a raw control byte outside tab/newline/CR — same shape as the existing backtick guard, closing the class. | M0 - closed 2026-08-03 (REC-27) — the separator at store.mjs:3833 is now the `\u0000` ESCAPE, value-identical, with a comment stating why; plain `grep -c "CREATE TABLE" bio-plane/src/store.mjs` returns 9 WITHOUT `-a`; and the class is closed as the row asked: hygiene.test.mjs scans every file under `bio-plane/src/` for a raw control byte outside tab/LF/CR, the same shape as the backtick guards. |

THE LESSON IS ABOUT THE CHECK, NOT THE TABLES: closing a class by parsing one file closes it only for that file, and the check's own success made the second location invisible. The fix is to enumerate tables from the RUNTIME schema rather than from a source literal. | M0 - closed 2026-08-03 (REC-27) — hygiene.test.mjs now parses store.mjs's CREATE TABLE literals as well as schema.mjs's (the name anchored to the opening paren / USING, so the prose-minted phantom table `does` in this row's own enumeration cannot recur; the eighth table it missed was the FTS5 virtual `bundles_fts`) and reports **52 of 52 tables covered by purge or a stated exemption**. Of the eight: `bundles_fts`/`selections`/`selection_items` were already purged; `project_participants` and `project_owner_votes` (keyed on project_id, a bundle id) added to purge in BOTH arms with stats/removed counters and behavioural assertions in projects.test.mjs; `member_expertise` (roster identity, like members), `admin_votes` (append-only governance record so an ejection stays auditable) and `export_log` (an export can never happen silently, and a purge must not erase the evidence one did) EXEMPT with those reasons stated in the allowlist. Negative control run: removing the project_participants DELETEs fails hygiene NAMING it (51 of 52) and fails 4 behavioural assertions. |
| D-138 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The UI's state-machine drift guard does not read the authority it claims to check against.** VERIFIED: `civicos-ui/check-semantics.mjs` reads `app.html` and never reads `bio-plane/checks/bio-checks.mjs` (0 occurrences). The UI comment at `app.html:1690-1693` states that this file enforces agreement with the catalogue. It does not — it binds the UI's static copies to each other. So the focus state machine exists in at least three places (the catalogue, the store, the UI) and the check that is supposed to stop them drifting compares two copies while leaving the SOURCE unchecked. A drift in the catalogue — the authority — passes silently. This is the same shape as D-137 one layer up: a guard that appears to close a class and closes only the part it happens to read, with its own green result standing in for the coverage it does not have. | M8 - open |
| D-139 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **Nothing publishes what may be DONE to an object, so every act surface must keep a forbidden surface-side map.** VERIFIED: no `affordances` op and no `options` publication exist in `bio-plane/src/index.mjs`; `op=whoami` publishes capabilities and `op=searchfields` publishes the query vocabulary, and there it stops. The seven `action_kind` values live only in the check catalogue. The interaction constructs make producer-published options a HARD constraint precisely because a surface-side map drifts — and the UI's hand-composed query syntax, drifting from `op=searchfields` which exists to prevent exactly that, is the measured proof. So the ACT construct cannot generalise past one hand-built instance until an op answers: for THIS object in THIS state, what acts exist, at what rung, needing what capability, and what will each refuse. Precedent for the shape is `searchfields`; precedent for producer-declared actions rendered by a surface is GitHub's Checks API (`PRACTICE-SURVEY.md`). This is small and it unblocks the entire M8 surface programme. | M8 - open |
| D-140 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **The queue's two organising axes are not data.** VERIFIED against `CREATE TABLE tasks` in `schema.mjs`: the columns are id, kind, refers_to, capture_sha, subject_text, subject_desc, locators, assignee, assignee_role, status, created, resolved_at, history. There is **no `class`** (FINDING / OBLIGATION / CONDITION, which decides what handling MEANS and whether it is personal or a record act) and **no `case`** (the Focus/Project grouping key Bob ruled on, which is both the relevance filter and the aggregation key). There is also no `queue_state` for the per-member mute, snooze and last-seen that D-125 requires. So the queue as designed — grouped by case, typed by class, with two-level preferences — cannot be built from `tasks` as it stands, and a surface would have to derive class from `kind` by a hardcoded map, which is D-139's forbidden shape wearing different clothes. Note the case key is derivable from the citation edge rather than needing duplication, but it must be RETURNED by the op that lists the queue. | M8 - open |
| D-141 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The UI rebuilds the project-visibility leak client-side, enumerating every project's projection.** VERIFIED at `civicos-ui/app.html:749-768`: `reverseRefs` calls `loadRecord()` (`op=list`), filters to every `focus`/`problem`/`project`, then calls `getProjection()` on EACH and parses `fm_json` to index their `references`. Its own comment says so — 'walk the projections of every focus and project'. So on top of D-135 (the server never stamps a viewer onto `list`/`projection`), the client actively ENUMERATES what Membership v2 section 7.9 says an uninvited member must not see: not the project's existence, name, references or participants. The two defects compound rather than duplicate — D-135 is the gate that does not fire, this is the caller that walks the whole set anyway, and fixing only the server would leave a client that asks for exactly the thing the server is now refusing. Note the intent is benign and stated ('at the record's scale this is a handful of reads'), which is what makes it the ordinary shape of this failure: a reasonable optimisation at 30 bundles that is a disclosure at 300 and a fence breach at any size. | M7 - open |
| D-142 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **Search silently degrades to a title-substring scan over an unfiltered list, and looks identical when it does.** VERIFIED at `civicos-ui/app.html:4013-4018`: if `op=search` returns no recognisable row shape, the surface falls back to `loadRecord()` — `op=list` — and filters client-side on `title`/`bundle_id` substring, with the comment 'so search always answers'. Three things are wrong and the third is the worst. (1) It bypasses the D-15 viewer gate, since `op=list` carries no viewer stamp (D-135). (2) It answers a WEAKER QUESTION: no full text, no frontmatter, no facets, no relevance — a member searching for a phrase inside a document gets nothing and is shown a confident empty state. (3) **A member cannot tell which engine answered.** The result list, the count line and the empty state are byte-identical either way, so a silent degradation presents as a finished search — which is the 'an outcome that costs nothing to produce is not evidence' family arriving in the UI: a result set that looks like a search result and is not one. A surface that cannot answer must SAY it cannot answer; `undetermined` is first-class here too. | M8 - open |
| D-143 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **A member of the public can verify a hash and list ids, and cannot READ a published case — the surface UI-PLAN calls 'the reason the rest exists' has no data path.** VERIFIED. The unauthenticated (`classes: null`) ops are exactly: `publishedmanifest`, `bootstrap`, `claim`, `login`, `enroll`, `invitelook`, `verify`, `knock`. **Not one of them returns a published bundle's bytes or prose.** And `publishedList` in `store.mjs` selects `bundle_id, bundle_sha, ratified_at, attestor_member, gate_version` — **no title** — which is why the published view renders a bundle id as its heading. So the public surface can today answer 'does this hash match' and 'what ids exist' and cannot answer 'what does it say'. U12 describes the case file as a narrative a member of the public reads start to finish, with print first-class and the sewer case as the proof piece, and names it as the reason the rest of the system exists; it is the one rung with no reachable data beneath it. NEEDED: a `classes: null` op serving a published bundle's prose and its registered bytes from the PUBLISHED bucket only (never the working corpus — the two-bucket fence is what makes an unauthenticated read safe), and a title on the published projection. Note the fence makes this SAFER to build than it looks: that bucket has never held unratified material, which is the same reasoning that already justifies `verify` being unauthenticated. | M6 - open |
| D-144 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **Re-ratifying a bundle DESTROYS the previous attestation — the record can still serve an old published hash and can no longer say who attested it.** VERIFIED at `bio-plane/src/store.mjs:5934-5940`: `published_bundles` is keyed on `bundle_id` alone and the write is `ON CONFLICT(bundle_id) DO UPDATE SET bundle_sha, ratified_at, attestor_key, attestor_member, gate_version, sig_armored` — every field replaced. **ONE CORRECTION TO HOW THIS WAS FIRST REPORTED, and it changes the fix rather than the severity.** The system's headline promise HOLDS: `published_shas` is keyed `(sha256, bundle_id, path)` and is genuinely append-only, so a hash once published stays answerable forever and `op=verify` on an old hash still works — the comment at 5941 saying exactly that is true. What is destroyed is the ATTESTATION PROVENANCE of the earlier publication: who signed it, when, with which key, under which catalogue version. So the record can answer 'yes, these bytes were published' and cannot answer 'by whom, and under what rules' — in a system whose entire product is that claims carry their author, and about the one act it calls irreversible. THE FIX IS NOT IDEMPOTENCE, which is what the first reading would have suggested: re-ratification is legitimate (a bundle revised and re-published SHOULD carry a new attestation). The fix is to make `published_bundles` accumulate like `published_shas` already does — key it `(bundle_id, ratified_at)` or chain each attestation to the one it supersedes — so the history of who stood behind which version is kept rather than replaced. Note a storyboard (`SB-OUTPUT` S16) designs a screen promising exactly that accumulation and cites `published_shas` as its justification without reading the row above it. | M6 - open |
| D-145 | design | 2026-08-01 | **Bundle ids are per-instance sequential, so nothing addressed by id survives leaving the instance that minted it.** VERIFIED: `allocId` (`store.mjs:4401`) mints per-instance sequence numbers, so every sovereign instance independently produces `INFO-2026-0001`. Hashes are global and ids are not. This is a hole under M6's whole promise — a record that can be mirrored, verified and outlived by tooling that already exists — because everything the record says ABOUT a document is addressed by id: `references[].target`, `refs`, `inquiry_basis`, `elevated_into`, every citation edge. An export can be verified byte for byte and its internal graph cannot be re-pointed at anything outside itself, and two instances citing each other would collide on the first id either had ever minted. The design pass asked what GRADE a cross-instance citation carries (`AUDIENCES` row 17) and nobody asked whether it can be ADDRESSED at all. Candidate shapes, none free: an instance-scoped prefix (cheap, changes every id and this record is append-only), a UUID beside the human id (no migration, two identifiers for one thing), or citation by content hash with the id as a comment — which is the shape the record's own doctrine already prefers, since identity is canonical and the address is a comment. | M6 - open |
| D-146 | gap | 2026-08-01 | **Six UI surfaces READ the entity/connection/progression layer and nothing can WRITE it — nine mutating ops landed with no caller anywhere, so the surfaces render empty by construction.** VERIFIED. The UI calls `entity`, `entitybyalias`, `connections`, `concerns`, `resolutions` and `captureprogressions` — all reads. Of the eleven mutating ops in that family, only `taskresolve` and `connect` have any caller: **`entitycreate`, `entityalias`, `relationdeclare`, `resolve`, `resolvetestify`, `progressiondefine`, `thread` and `discharge` have no caller in `civicos-ui/app.html` OR `bio-plane/src/setup.mjs`.** So an entire milestone's write path is unreachable while its read path is shipped and rendering. THIS CORRECTS AN EARLIER CLAIM OF MINE IN THE SHARPEST DIRECTION. I recorded that the A-D grades and the entity axis 'have no consumer until the claim layer exists', and used that to argue the claim layer should be built first so they would acquire one. The literal claim is false and the truth is worse: **they have consumers and no producers.** Six surfaces already depend on this layer and display nothing, and no amount of building above it changes that. The ordering consequence is real: the entity WRITE surface is not a later enrichment, it is what makes six shipped surfaces non-empty AND what produces the earned grades an inquiry's basis legs need (`resolutions` being the A/B/C source). Build the claim layer and the entity write surface together, not one behind the other. | M4 - open |
| D-147 | design | 2026-08-01 | **The `action` object models a records request as one round trip, and the real lifecycle has at least four decision points with their own clocks and outcomes.** `action` runs `planned -> active -> awaiting_response -> resolved / abandoned`, so `awaiting_response` collapses everything between asking and getting. The externally-sourced lifecycle (see `PROBLEM-DOMAIN.md`) has distinct stages that each produce a different fact about the body: the FEE ESTIMATE, the FEE-WAIVER decision, PARTIAL PRODUCTION, the ADMINISTRATIVE APPEAL, and litigation. Each has its own clock and its own outcome vocabulary, and collapsing them loses precisely what an accountability record wants. Federal appeal adjudication averaged 96.91 days in FY2024 [DOJ OIP, government's own statistics] and appeal outcomes vary by an order of magnitude between agencies — complete reversal 9.3% at State, 17.6% at DOJ, 31.4% at DHS [computed from the three agencies' own FY2024 annual reports] — so **a design must not assume a single reversal rate or a single response shape**. This is D-128's declared-versus-actual flow with real numbers attached: the declared flow is the statutory timeline, the actual flow is what these figures describe, and the delta is the finding. | M4 - open |
| D-148 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A fee quote is EVIDENCE about a public body, and the design treats it as an administrative obstacle.** Sourced examples: Colorado's Secretary of State quoted **$2.1M** (69,736 projected labor hours) for election contracts; Texas DCJ quoted **$1,132,024.30** for records the state PREA office supplied for **$551.39** — **a 2,000x spread for identical records**; Nevada DETR quoted $650,000, then **dropped a $1,083 charge entirely and released the records free once challenged**, which is direct evidence the quote was not cost-based; DoD quoted **~$660 million** [MuckRock survey Dec 2023; Las Vegas Review-Journal Sept 2023; Techdirt — press and NGO sources, labelled as such]. Against that, the federal fee system collects **0.34-0.38% of what FOIA costs to run**, about **$1.54 per request**, and the State Department collected **$0.00** in FY2024 [government's own reported statistics] — **fees are a deterrent instrument, not a cost-recovery one**, and the record should be able to say so with its own captured evidence. Consequence for the design: a fee quote should be a first-class captured artifact with a comparison basis, not correspondence prose. Two identical requests priced 2,000x apart is exactly the shape BIO exists to make checkable, and today it would be a paragraph in a `## Correspondence` heading. | M4 - open |
| D-149 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **The design is jurisdiction-blind, and BIO's own jurisdiction differs from federal FOIA on every axis that matters.** Nothing in the record models WHICH access regime an `action` runs under, and the assumptions differ sharply. Federal: fee-shifting is discretionary ('the court MAY assess... substantially prevailed', 5 USC 552(a)(4)(E)), search and review fees are chargeable by requester class, and plaintiffs substantially prevailed in only **112 of 1,672 lawsuits, 6.7%** [GAO-16-667, the government auditing itself]. **California, which is Oakland and therefore BIO's actual regime**: fee-shifting is MANDATORY ('the court SHALL award court costs and reasonable attorney's fees to the requester', Gov. Code 7923.115(a)); fees are **direct duplication cost only** with no search fee and no review fee (7922.530(a)); and **redaction time is not billable at all** — *National Lawyers Guild v. City of Hayward* (2020) 9 Cal.5th 488 threw out a $2,938.58 invoice for 40.2 hours of bodycam redaction, holding that 'extraction' means retrieving responsive data, not omitting unwanted data. So in California 'it will take us 200 hours to redact this' cannot lawfully become a bill — and a design carrying federal assumptions would model a cost that does not exist here and miss a violation when one is charged. A pending 2026 California bill would loosen the fee rules [CalMatters, Mar 2026]; flag as pending, not law. | M4 - open |
| D-150 | design | 2026-08-01 | **The completeness claim is VALIDATED by the strongest external evidence in the study — and the same evidence says an authored field is necessary and not sufficient.** From `PROBLEM-DOMAIN.md`: concealing exculpatory evidence appears in **44% of exonerations involving official misconduct**; Nifong scoped a report to exclude; Fujitsu edited out references to known defects; South Yorkshire amended 116 statements. **Every retained fact was true in every one of those cases** — which is precisely the failure the completeness claim targets and the reason a case is not merely a published finding. That is the clearest validation any BIO construct has received from outside the repo. BUT THE SAME SOURCES CARRY A CORRECTION: in both the SBI and Rolling Stone reviews the catch was **a second person with standing**, not a form field — and in each the person who found the gap was overruled inside the hierarchy. So the authored exclusion statement is necessary and insufficient, and the design should say so rather than let the field stand in for the check. What that implies is not settled here: candidates are a second-member acknowledgement on the exclusion statement before publication, or making the statement the thing a reviewer is specifically asked to attack rather than one field among many. Do not build the field and call the problem solved. | M10 - open · REC-14 (disposition corrected 2026-08-01 by session BOB: this row was written `M6` when the ladder had no rung above M8, and M6 is *the record can be left, mirrored and outlived* — nothing to do with a completeness claim at publication. M10 now exists and is where publication lives.) |

| D-151 | defect | 2026-08-01 | **A machine credential can RESOLVE an unassigned task, so an obligation can be closed with no member act — and the code's own comments say it cannot.** VERIFIED this turn in the source. `Store.#refuseNotYours` (`bio-plane/src/store.mjs:6943-6946`) returns `null` — allow — the moment `row.assignee === "unassigned"`, BEFORE it examines who is calling; and `taskforward` / `taskresolve` both carry `"probe"` in their `classes` (`bio-plane/src/index.mjs:271-272`). So `op=taskresolve` driven by a machine credential closes the task and writes a history entry reading `actor: "token:probe"`. The fence's own comment states the guarantee it makes — *"a daemon cannot close somebody's work"* — and that is exactly true and exactly insufficient: **a daemon cannot close somebody's work; it can close NOBODY'S work, and closing is the act.** Two comments in the same codebase already forbid what the code permits: `taskResolve`'s own header calls it *"Also a member action"*, and `index.mjs:602-604` calls both verbs *"MEMBER actions performed by a PERSON through their session"*. This is the same class as D-137 and D-138 — a guard whose comment describes a stronger property than its code enforces, so a later reader takes the comment as the specification. THE FIX IS A PRECEDENT ALREADY IN THE FILE, not a new rule: `release` (`store.mjs:1857-1861`) refuses any author matching `/^token:/` with `MACHINE_CANNOT_RELEASE`. Refuse at the ACT, not at the fence, so the refusal does not depend on assignment state: `MACHINE_CANNOT_RESOLVE` and `MACHINE_CANNOT_FORWARD`, plus removing `"probe"` from the two ops' `classes` and correcting `#refuseNotYours`'s comment to state what it actually guarantees. The fence STAYS — it answers *is this THIS member's task* and the act refusal answers *is this a person at all*; the second is not derivable from the first, which is why the hole existed. `taskdrain` is deliberately untouched: it is the daemon's path and draining is not resolving. Lines up with REC-13's `MACHINE_CANNOT_CONCLUDE`, so the record ends with ONE rule in three places rather than three judgements — a machine may surface, route and prepare; a member authors, resolves and concludes. NEGATIVE CONTROL for the fixing item: remove the act-level refusal and a `token:probe` credential resolves an unassigned task while every existing task assertion still passes — which is the state today. Raised by DEC-7. | M8 · CLOSED 2026-08-03 (REC-28): act-level refusals by shape before the row is read; probe declassed with the ACL limit stated; fence kept; NC re-run by CONDUCT. |

| D-152 | design | 2026-08-01 | **Image-only PDFs must be OCR'd and investigated, and OCR text must never be indistinguishable from text the publisher wrote.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-4), overturning the recommendation that scanned PDFs stay honestly unreadable. Bob: *"Some PDFs will be released in the public record as image only. The textual contents of these images need to be extracted and investigated for meaningful content."* The refused argument was a volume argument — ~14% of a 14-document sample, skewing to design and scan artifacts — and coverage is not a volume question: a class the record can capture and never read is a hole, and it does not have to be large, only non-empty. The class is also not randomly assigned; scanned-only is what a signed order, an exhibit, a handwritten annotation and a deliberately-imaged release all look like. *"Investigated for meaningful content"* puts OCR output on the READING path (FW-15's L2→L3 wire), not merely in a text field, or the class is captured, readable and still ungraphed. **THE PROVENANCE RULE IS STRUCTURAL AND IS THE THING THAT COULD TURN THIS CAPABILITY INTO AN OVERCLAIMING DEFECT:** extracted text carries `text_source: 'layer'`; OCR carries `text_source: 'ocr'` with engine, version and per-region confidence, and they are distinguishable everywhere the text is shown, cited or indexed. A basis leg resting on OCR'd text carries its IMAGE REGION (page + rect) so a reader checks the claim against the pixels rather than against our transcription — the same discipline as answering by hash. OCR never raises a capture grade, and a low-confidence region reads `undetermined` rather than a best guess, which is the mojibake rule one layer up. **MEASUREMENT GATES DESIGN:** whether OCR runs in-plane at all is unknown — a WASM engine's bundle size against the Worker limit and its CPU against the isolate ceiling (D-36, D-56) — with the pdf-worker fleet member (I6) the first fallback and an external service the second. Nothing is designed before that number exists. | M2 - open · CPDF-9 (measure), CPDF-10 (build) |

| D-153 | design | 2026-08-01 | **`research/AUDIENCES.md` H4 and H6 are not hazards to be avoided; they are the required workflow — corrected by external evidence, and the pass is NOT edited.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-13). AUDIENCES §7 runs `RATIFY -> CASE -> rendering -> action -> consequence` and §9 lists the pre-publication release of material among its HAZARDS — H4 (*the low-threshold rendering that escapes*) and H6 (*the embargo*). The practice literature says the ordering is inverted, and the sources are professional standards rather than opinion: the SPJ code (*"diligently seek out subjects of news stories to give them the opportunity to respond to allegations of wrongdoing"*); GAGAS/Yellow Book, where obtaining the views of responsible officials is a REPORTING REQUIREMENT and GAO's own protocols give an agency **7 to 30 calendar days** on a draft report, expect a single position including the rationale for any disagreement, and print the response beside the finding; and the Columbia Journalism School review of Rolling Stone's *A Rape on Campus*, which identified a comment request made **without specifics** as the central failure. So an unratified rendering carrying specific claims must be able to leave the building, addressed to the subject, under a deadline, before anything is ratified — which is exactly AUDIENCES row 14's *"delivery with no bucket"*, and the outside evidence says that row is the MAIN PATH for any case that names anybody rather than an edge case. **THE PASS IS DELIBERATELY NOT EDITED** — a research pass is a record of what it concluded, and correcting it in place would rewrite history and hide that the evidence arrived later and overturned it; same treatment `RECONCILED.md` gave the storyboard files, with the correction living in a live surface and a pointer going on the pass. What Bob's ruling adds beyond the sources: the GATE is not the contact but the group's DECLARED, JUSTIFIED POSITION on it, carried inside the case as declared bias — which is what makes a tipping-off refusal legitimate and visible instead of quiet. | M10 - open · REC-14, REC-24, UI-17 |

| D-154 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A HUNCH is temporary declared bias with a temporarily-high connection grade, and it is bias debt that must clear before publication.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-15), and it resolves the D1(a)-versus-D1(b) fork by showing it was a false one: an authored connection grade and an earned one are not rivals, they are two phases of the same object. During `open` a member may author a connection grade high enough to make otherwise disconnected evidence traversable — *"the investigator thinks this is true"* — and at `published` no hunch may remain, so every leg is earned or is honest testimony. **THE GATE ALREADY EXISTED AND WAS BUILT FOR SOMETHING ELSE:** `BIO_Declared_Bias_v0_1.md`'s bias-debt section already ends *"a work product carrying bias debt cannot advance its workproduct_state or be ratified for publication until the debt is settled"*, written for a bias statement CHANGING and leaving old analysis owed a re-run. Registering a hunch as what it is reaches that rule with no new machinery. It also settles what CLEARING means without a further ruling: debt is *cleared by re-running the evaluation under the current set*, so the test is neither "delete the leg" nor "leave it in unrated" — **the case must still hold when the hunch is removed from the set**. `grade_source` gains `hunch` beside `resolution` and `testimony`; it is the ONLY authored grade permitted above D, permitted precisely because it cannot survive publication; it carries an author and a date; and it is **visible as a hunch on every surface from the moment it is made**, because the failure mode this construct invites is a hunch quietly ageing into a fact. A hunch is NOT an `undetermined` leg and must never be composed as one — R1 suspends an axis when a grade is ABSENT, while a hunch grade is present and asserted, and treating it as undetermined would destroy the traversability the ruling exists to preserve. `op=publishpreflight` refuses `UNCLEARED_HUNCH` naming every leg, before any signature exists. Manifest registration needs `object_type: bias` (D-84) and is sequenced behind it; the leg-level source and the refusal ship with the claim layer where they bite. | M9 - open · REC-11, REC-12, REC-15, REC-18, UI-11, UI-12 |

| D-155 | design | 2026-08-01 | **A project declares the evidentiary strength its work requires, and the published case carries that declaration beside the strength it reached.** RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-17). `BIO_Content_Framework_v0_10.md` §12 has recorded since v0.1 that a claim *"needs a standard of proof attached and that is doctrine rather than architecture — the next design conversation"*; this is that conversation. The question was what makes an unsupported case harder to state, and both options this project generated put the standard in the wrong place: a global floor would refuse honest weak findings, and no floor leaves nothing to fall short of. **The standard belongs to the GROUP and to a particular piece of their work** — a project convened to refer something to an auditor needs a different bar from one convened to decide whether a thing is worth looking at, and both are legitimate, so a single system-wide answer was always going to be wrong for one of them. **This is the PROJECT axis and it is not the AUDIENCE axis**, which is why it does not contradict `research/AUDIENCES.md` §5's ruling that per-audience relaxation is *"a structural prior by role — the same defect as a suspicion flag, with the sign reversed"* and that a threshold belongs on a rendering and never on ratification. A project-declared bar is authored by the group, before the work, about its own intentions; nobody's standard is set by who they are. That file is not edited (see D-153's reasoning); its ruling stands and is about a different axis. SHAPE: `project` frontmatter gains `required_strength{capture, connection}` — **a PAIR per R2**, because a scalar would re-collapse the two axes in the one field a reader is most likely to quote. C-2.9 already requires a non-empty `objective` on the same object, so the check has a precedent beside it. `op=publishpreflight` refuses `BELOW_PROJECT_STRENGTH` naming the axis; the escape is amending the project's declared bar, which is an authored, dated, on-the-record act visible in the published case — you can lower your own bar, you cannot do it quietly. A project with no declared strength gates nothing and the case SAYS so rather than rendering a blank, since an absent bar is not a bar of zero. An inquiry outside any project has no bar and inherits none. | M10 - open · REC-14, REC-15, UI-18 |

| D-156 | design | 2026-08-02 | **"Audience" names two different things in this project, and they have different machinery attached.** Named by Bob 2026-08-02 (DEC-17 addendum): in requirements gathering the word refers to **user types** — the people who use BIO; when referring to a published case it means **the readers and consumers of that case** — elements of the public who use the information, analysis and conclusions it contains. VERIFIED in the corpus: `research/AUDIENCES.md` enumerates *"eight audiences"* mixing both senses in one list — an administrator or a lawyer READING A PUBLISHED CASE is the second sense, while *"the publisher's own future members"* and the primary archetype (*a member of a community accountability group*, from `JOURNEY-PRIMARY.md`) are BIO USERS and are the first; the word `archetype` occurs twice in the entire file. **THE CONFLATION IS WHY THAT FILE'S §5 READS AS A WARNING RATHER THAN A DEFINITION.** Its hard constraint — *"a per-audience relaxation is a structural prior by role… if administrators need a lower threshold, that is a threshold on a RENDERING, and it must never be a threshold on RATIFICATION"* — is correct and unchanged, but it had to be written defensively because "administrator" sat in a list mixing a user type with a case reader, which makes a per-role gate look like a natural extension of the list. Separated, the rule is definitional: **renderings and thresholds vary by AUDIENCE; capabilities, journeys and surfaces vary by USER TYPE; neither ever reaches ratification.** DISAMBIGUATION ADOPTED: `AUDIENCE` is reserved for readers/consumers of a published case; the requirements sense is `USER TYPE`, or `ARCHETYPE` where a document already uses that word. This is the D-68 / D-8 vocabulary-drift class caught BEFORE it reached the code — no surface, table or op names either concept today, so the correction costs documents and no migration, which is the whole reason to pay it now. The affected documents are `research/AUDIENCES.md`, `research/JOURNEY-PRIMARY.md`, `UI-PLAN.md` and `research/CAPABILITIES.md`; the research passes take a pointer rather than an edit (D-153's reasoning). **AMENDED 2026-08-02 by Bob, and the correction is larger than the vocabulary split:** the USER TYPE list is not one archetype. Bob: the community-accountability-group member is the PRIMARY archetype, but user types also include *"journalists, lawyers, city administrators, an oversight body, and any other role that involves collecting evidence, investigating, and any other group interested in improving government using the capabilities and processes of the CivicOS."* **The consequence D-156 did not see: the same person can be BOTH.** A lawyer may USE an instance to build a case and may also READ someone else's published case — the two senses are not two populations of people, they are two RELATIONSHIPS to a case, and which one applies depends on the case, not on the person. That is why the machinery differs (capabilities and surfaces versus renderings and thresholds) and why neither ever reaches ratification. | M10 - open · a documentation pass, no code |
| D-157 | defect | 2026-08-02 | **`op=memberlist` hands the cover↔handle PAIRING to ordinary members and to `MEMBER_TOKEN`, and §3 says only administrators see them together.** MEASURED 2026-08-02 (session BOB, `MEASUREMENTS.md`): an ordinary member's session (`administer: false`) and the shared `MEMBER_TOKEN` machine credential each receive `handle = cover` for every member on the LIVE `bio` store, byte-identical to the `ADMIN_TOKEN` view. `BIO_Membership_Architecture_v1.md` §3 and `v2` §3 are identical and unambiguous: *"Pairing. Only administrators see cover and handle together."* The split is the anti-deanonymisation mechanism — `schema.mjs` on `members.cover`: *"the cover-and-handle split exists precisely so that a roster seized or subpoenaed does not deanonymise the group."* **The source contradicts itself in three places:** `index.mjs:407` grants `classes: ["admin","member","probe"]`, the comment eight lines below it says *"All admin-only: memberlist pairs cover with handle and only administrators see those together (section 3)"*, and `store.mjs:5810` says *"Every op that reaches this is admin-only at the control plane."* Neither comment is true. **`PROBE_TOKEN` is NOT exposed** — `scopeFor` (`index.mjs:862`) confines probe class to the `scratch` store, a different DO with its own member table; that half was measured before it was claimed. **The fix is a projection, not a refusal:** §3 also says *"Members and the public see handles"*, so a member legitimately needs the handle roster (project participant lists, authorship) and must not receive `cover`. `test/members.test.mjs:192` asserts *"machine member token reads the roster"* and must be CORRECTED rather than exempted (`CLAUDE.md`), with a comment saying why the old assertion was wrong; the negative control is a non-admin caller receiving a `cover` field at all. | M7 · CLOSED 2026-08-03 (REC-29): the projection withholds cover from every non-administrator, server-stamped, fail-closed; members.test.mjs corrected; NC re-run by CONDUCT. |
| D-158 | defect | 2026-08-02 | **A signing key registered for a member who never ENROLLED reads `active` on `op=signerlist` and is refused by `op=ratify`.** MEASURED 2026-08-02 (session BOB, `MEASUREMENTS.md`, real `ssh-keygen` signatures through the real ratify path). `signerList()` (`store.mjs:5884`) reads the `signers` table alone; `gateFacts()` (`store.mjs:5920`) joins `members` and requires `s.status='active' AND m.status='active'`. The REVOCATION path agrees only because `memberSet` (`store.mjs:5857-5861`) cascades — it deletes the member's sessions and sets their signers to `revoked`. There is no cascade for the other direction: a member is `status='invited'` until enrolment (`store.mjs:5731`) and `signerAdd` checks only that the member EXISTS (`store.mjs:5874`), so an admin may register a key for someone who has never enrolled and `signerlist` reports it `active` while a signature from it returns `SIG_UNKNOWN_KEY`. **Not a blocker for RECONCILED §4 Q11**, which is settled YES on this measurement: the surface's *"you hold no active signing key"* is never a false alarm, because a key `signerlist` does not show active is one `ratify` will not accept. It is wrong only in the optimistic direction. **Two candidate fixes, and the second is preferable:** make `signerList()` join `members` so one predicate serves both, or make `signerAdd` refuse a member who has not enrolled — the join is the smaller change but leaves a row that means nothing; refusing at write keeps the table honest and is the shape `signerAdd`'s existing NO_SUCH_MEMBER check already reaches for. Whichever ships, the other view must be asserted against it. | M10 - open · REC-15/UI-17 pre-flight; scoped in the 2026-08-02 BOB INBOX entry |
| D-159 | design | 2026-08-02 | **An ungraded leg now costs a conclusion nothing, which makes leaving one ungraded the cheapest way to keep an inconvenient leg out of the reckoning.** Created by DEC-18: an ungraded leg is INERT — it does not contribute, does not floor and does not unrate — so a member holding a leg that cuts against their conclusion can decline to grade it and the conclusion stands on its load-bearing legs. This is a laundering path in the OPPOSITE direction from the one R1 closed, and it is named here rather than left for a build session to discover. **Three defences already stand and a fourth should not be invented before use shows one is needed:** every ungraded leg is NAMED, on the case and in the index row (DEC-18's plural clause); the completeness statement is authored, unprefilled and must say what the case leaves out; and invariant 7 makes a leg that cuts against travel the same path as one that supports. **What would settle it is USE, not argument** — whether real groups leave cuts-against legs ungraded is observable once M9/M10 ship, and inventing a counterweight now would be the same mistake as a global strength floor (§4 Q2). **RE-READ 2026-08-08 by PL-14 / IS-7 against a machine shaping legs at volume (the re-reading QUEUE IS-7 asked for), OUTCOME: THE RULE IS UNCHANGED AND NO FOURTH DEFENCE IS OWED — but ONE THING DID CHANGE and it is not the rule.** (a) **THE MACHINE HOLDS NO LEVER, and that is now enforced rather than intended.** D-159's hazard is a CHOICE — a member declining to grade a leg that cuts against them. A machine has no such choice: §5 rules that *"the grades arrive from the record"* and PL-14 enforces it at the READ, taking every effective grade from `earnedBasisRegistry` and leaving a leg ungraded only where the record earned nothing. So volume does not multiply a decision nobody can make, and the composed-at-volume case is not a wider version of this debt. (b) **BUT THE FIRST DEFENCE DEGRADES WITH VOLUME WHILE THE ARITHMETIC DOES NOT.** *"Every ungraded leg is NAMED"* is a defence only while somebody reads the names; one ungraded leg among four is seen, forty among two hundred in a machine-composed reading is a list nobody reads. The rule holds; its enforcement by a human reader thins. (c) **AND A SIBLING LEVER NOW EXISTS THAT D-159 COULD NOT HAVE NAMED:** under DEC-32's OR-max a leg can be put in a BRANCH OF ITS OWN, where it neither floors nor unrates — visibly present and contributing nothing, which is a stronger form of the same move. That is DEC-32's own named hazard with its own three containments (authored sufficiency, the compound falsifier, the reader), so it is a sibling and not an extension. **WHAT THE RE-READING CHANGES IS THE OBSERVABLE, not the rule:** this row says to watch whether groups leave cuts-against legs ungraded; against a machine the fact worth measuring is the RATIO AND ITS PROVENANCE — how many of a reading's legs are ungraded, and whether each is ungraded because THE RECORD EARNED NOTHING or because A MEMBER DID NOT GRADE IT. Those are one fact today and will not be. **PL-14 makes that measurement cheap rather than taking it:** `op=versionstrength` publishes `ungraded[]` with a per-leg reason distinguishing the two, so when M10 runs with a real group the number this row waits for can be read off the answer instead of reconstructed. RAISED, not acted on — the disposition below is unchanged. | M10 - open · WATCH, no task — re-raise when M10 has run with a real group |

| D-196 | design | 2026-08-04 | **The completeness statement is PROSE with nothing behind it, which the search-completeness research identifies as the exact claim the field considers worthless.** MEASURED: `completenessFields` (bio-checks.mjs) carries `statement` (authored prose about what the case does NOT cover), `subject_justification`, and `excluded` rows of (target, description, reason). **It records NOTHING about what was SEARCHED**, and `excluded` can only hold DELIBERATE omissions — things someone knew of and left out — so there is no representation at all for what nobody knew existed. The literature's verdict on unbacked completeness claims is Blair & Maron (1985): attorneys stipulated they must reach 75% recall and sincerely believed they had; measured recall was ~20%, after iteration and refinement. **THE FIX IS ALREADY DESIGNED UNDER ANOTHER NAME.** `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` argues for an append-only OBSERVATION LOG — every look, including the ones returning nothing — on provenance grounds. That is also, exactly, the disclosed-process half of a defensible completeness claim: *we searched these sources, at these levels, on these dates, on these leads, and this came back, including the empty results*. That is Rule 26(g)'s *reasonable inquiry* made checkable. **The two designs are ONE design** and neither works alone: an observation log nobody publishes is bookkeeping, and a completeness statement with nothing behind it is Blair & Maron. What must NOT be built: a recall-like number (the denominator is unknowable at BIO's prevalence and Goodhart is invoked by name in the literature) or elusion testing (its inventors have asked the profession to discontinue it; it overstated recall by 16-36 points in live litigation, and its failure mode is assessing one's own work). See `research/SEARCH-COMPLETENESS.md`. | M10 - open · the completeness statement gains a search record from the observation log |
| D-195 | design | 2026-08-04 | **DEC-32's OR-arithmetic takes the MAXIMUM over branches, which is only sound if the branches are INDEPENDENT — and nothing in the design tests independence, which three separate research strands identify as the exact failure that defeats every professional methodology.** DEC-32 (ruled 2026-08-04) sets strength as MIN over AND-related legs and MAX over OR-related branches, with *independently sufficient* an AUTHORED member judgment. **The research says an authored judgment is precisely what fails here, because correlation is invisible from where the author stands.** THREE INDEPENDENT DEMONSTRATIONS: (1) **The New York Times' Iraq WMD post-mortem** — reporters had defector testimony PLUS senior administration confirmation, two sources from different institutional categories, and the editors' note records that the accounts were *"often eagerly confirmed by United States officials"* who had themselves been fed by the same INC/Chalabi pipeline. Multiplicity was real; independence was not. (2) **Steve Buttry's Farragut case (Verification Handbook, p.15)** — twelve team members plus the opposing star and coach, fourteen honest non-colluding eyewitnesses, all consistent, all WRONG the same way; game video showed one foul where all fourteen remembered three or four. His conclusion: source count is not evidence when a shared narrative can generate correlated error. (3) **The Berkeley Protocol names CIRCULAR REPORTING as a hazard (para.187) and supplies no test for it**; the Verification Handbook prescribes triangulation while its own lead case study shows triangulation failing; Bellingcat's peer review is same-institution; ICIJ's journalists all work one corpus. **THE CONSEQUENCE FOR BIO IS EXACT: two OR-branches whose legs trace to captures sharing an origin are not two grounds, and taking the max publishes a strength the finding does not have — the Judith Miller error with arithmetic behind it.** THE FIX BIO IS UNUSUALLY WELL PLACED TO BUILD, and no newsroom could: provenance is CONTENT-ADDRESSED and every capture records its origin and chain, so the system can DERIVE whether two branches share an upstream source and surface it. Derived informs, authored binds (D-90) — the system flags *these two grounds share a source* and the member decides; it must not silently compute independence, and must not refuse the act. Also record what the member asserted: *independently sufficient* should mean independent in ORIGIN, not merely in reasoning, and the entry's own anti-gaming argument (visibility to a reader) only works if the shared origin is visible too. | M9 · open — pair with REC-12's strength derivation; DEC-32's arithmetic is unchanged, the INDEPENDENCE CHECK is what is missing |

So the frontier is entirely DERIVED, and a derived frontier inherits the corpus's blind spots exactly — the machine analogue of a reporter who only reads clippings. **THE CONSEQUENCE THAT MAKES THIS EVIDENTIARY RATHER THAN ERGONOMIC:** RFC 2308's rule (see `STORE-AS-CACHE.md`) is that a negative answer with no proof of AUTHORITY attached is not recordable at all — the SOA is what makes *it does not exist* a finding rather than a shrug. **A member's authored lead IS that authority.** *We went looking for the document this tip described, across these sources, on this date, and there is none* is a finding with a name behind it; the same empty result with no lead behind it is silence that cost nothing to produce. | D-191 | design | 2026-08-04 | **A capture assembled from reused subresources is a COMPOSITE with a temporal spread that the record does not state, so it may depict a moment that never existed.** From the archival-systems survey (`STORE-AS-CACHE.md`): Ainsworth, Nelson & Van de Sompel measured that embedded resources in an archived page routinely carry datetimes *"up to several years in the future or past"* relative to the embedding page, and the follow-up study is titled *"Only One Out of Five Archived Web Pages Existed as Presented"* (arxiv.org/abs/1402.0928; HT '15). **BIO builds exactly this shape on purpose**: `CAPTURE-SCALING.md`'s reuse policy fetches evidence and reuses furniture, and the manifest records `reused_from` and `reused_fetched_at` PER PART — so the data to compute the spread exists and nothing computes or states it. CAP-4's re-fetch at ratification NARROWS the spread and does not MEASURE it. The honest shape is the one the record uses everywhere else: state the fact rather than fix it — a composite capture carries its temporal extent (earliest and latest part fetch instant) and a reader weighs it, exactly as they weigh a grade.
