| D-192 | defect | 2026-08-04 | **Integrity of stored bytes is not integrity of what a reader SEES, and archived-page replay is a documented attack surface.** Lerner, Kohno & Roesner, *Rewriting History: Changing the Archived Web from the Present* (ACM CCS 2017, adalerner.com/lerner-rewritingHistory-CCS17.pdf): four demonstrated attacks that alter a reader's view of an archived page — text, images, styling and behaviour — by injecting or abusing script that runs at VIEW time, and the paper's own summary is that *"attackers do not need to compromise the archives in order to compromise users' views of a stored page."* BIO stores captured HTML and renders it, and the whole product is trustworthiness of the record, so a reader shown an altered rendition of bytes we hold intact is the defect class this project cares about most — the hash stays correct while the claim the reader forms is wrong. NOT YET ASSESSED against our own viewer: what BIO renders, with what sandboxing, and whether any captured script executes at view time is UNMEASURED and is the first step. NEEDS: the harness disposes of its miniflare sandbox in a `finally`, the suites dispose of their own, and the battery sweeps ORPHANS older than the current run at start; the OCR probes' npm installs are separately bounded. Measured three modes × three runs: succeed-and-exit leaked 3/3, THROW leaked 0/3 (miniflare's exit hook is still armed when a suite never reaches dispose), dispose+250 ms settle leaked 0/3. So a `finally` would have fixed nothing, and 23,263 directories in days is the record of a battery **working**. The battery now ASSERTS it, from a temp directory of its own that it hands to every child, so the count is attributable and a concurrent un-fixed checkout cannot redden the run. Verified: battery green twice, 95/95, 5172 assertions, 0 residue each; NC (remove the exit disposal) → 84 sandboxes leaked and the run fails while all 95 suites still report green. RESIDUAL, deliberately left: the OCR/PDF probes are out of scope by the item, and `tier1-coverage-probe.mjs`'s persistent `$TMPDIR` cache is why they must stay out. **CORRECTED 2026-08-08 by M0-10 (D-235), and the correction is about the INSTRUMENT rather than the fix: the hygiene assertion this row installed certified that a suite IMPORTS `sandbox.mjs`, never that its files LAND inside the `$TMPDIR` the import redirects.** Two suites imported it and wrote to hardcoded `/tmp` paths anyway, so both passed while leaking outside the fence — and because `battery.mjs` counts residue only INSIDE `$TMPDIR`, the leak was not under-reported but invisible (`0 directories, 0 sandboxes` printed over 12 MB accumulating in `/tmp/mfp` since 2026-07-31). The containment half is now asserted; see D-235. **THE FENCE ITSELF IS SOUND AND IS NOW MEASURED UNDER GENUINE CONCURRENCY** — the question M0-13 explicitly could not settle: 12 truly overlapping processes, distinct sandbox each, no cross-contamination, 0 left after exit, with an ARMED probe that reports DOES NOT HOLD when the sandbox is shared. |

Silent re-grading in either direction is the defect. **THIS GENERALISES BEYOND OCR and is the reason to build it once**: Tier-2 pdf.js has a pinned version that will move, an AI post-processor is a second derivation, an external service retrains under an unchanged name (DEC-35's own argument against Textract) — three engines with one problem, the D-164 lesson applied before rather than after. | D-199 | design | 2026-08-04 | **The `ai` credential class and its declared task scope — DESIGN AHEAD, recorded here by CONDUCT enacting DEC-55 (Bob, 2026-08-04), because nothing reaches the plane today and a scope kept only in the decision register reaches only the person who already knows it.** Whoever builds the assistant (DEC-27) builds to these five. (1) **Mint the `ai` class in `classify()` on the DEC-37 precedent**, with a DECLARED TASK SCOPE enforced at the gate in `scopeFor`'s shape. (2) **The task scope is declared IN THE RECORD, never in a settings row** — DEC-17's reasoning transplanted: a settings row *"would be a way to change the standard with nothing to read afterwards"*, and what an AI credential may reach is exactly the thing that must be amendable only as an authored, dated, on-the-record act. (3) **Minting an AI token is a MEMBER act, never an AI act.** If an agent can request a broader token, the scoping is theatre — the never-prefill pattern applied to capability rather than to text. (4) **The record names the token IDENTITY and the PRINCIPAL behind it** — an organisation-scoped key acts for the group with nobody individual behind it; a member-scoped key is attributable. Both are legitimate, they carry different accountability, so an act must say which; never the token's value, which `tokens.mjs` already denylists on publication. (5) **THE EXISTING MACHINE FENCES GENERALISE TO THE NEW CLASS BY CONSTRUCTION — AND ONLY BECAUSE OF REC-46, WHICH LANDED THE SAME DAY.** Until then `store.mjs` answered *"is this a machine"* for itself ELEVEN times in two hand-typed shapes while the catalog answered it a third way with a word list that knew nothing of the prefix, and REC-45 had measured the cost: the gate accepted `asserted_by: token:member`. REC-46 collapsed them into one predicate over the `token:<class>` stamp, so **a token stamping `token:ai` is caught by every `MACHINE_CANNOT_*` refusal automatically**. Asked a day earlier this would have meant editing eleven sites and probably missing one. **The negative control is therefore cheap and MANDATORY, and ships WITH the class rather than after it:** mint an `ai`-class credential and assert that `MACHINE_CANNOT_PUBLISH`, `_CONCLUDE`, `_RELEASE`, `_REOPEN`, `_CORRESPOND`, `_MOVE_ACTION` and D-151's resolve/forward refusals each fire BY NAME — and that removing the predicate makes them all pass. **SEQUENCE THE CHECK ROLE FIRST**: DEC-24 calls it *"the role most worth building"* because the threat model is self-directed overclaiming, and it is also the safest, since a role that only reads and raises needs the narrowest scope of the four (EXTRACT, PURSUE, FIND, CHECK). | M9 · open — **NO LONGER ONLY WITH THE ASSISTANT (updated 2026-08-05, DEC-60).** The `ai` class now has a SECOND consumer and it is the first to hold a mutating op: the INVESTIGATIVE SESSION (`INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md`), whose scope reads across a project and writes only suggestions through one endpoint (IS-3/IS-4). **All five determinations above hold unchanged and IS-4 extends the scope vocabulary rather than revising it** — (2) in particular is what makes an investigative scope declarable in the record rather than in a settings row, and (5)'s negative control now has a second class of fence to fire against. DEC-24's rule still stands over all of it, and DEC-60 is its fullest expression rather than an exception: the machine proposes — now including the reasoned object itself — and the member authors by accepting. Nothing is owed before the assistant (DEC-27) OR before IS-1. Appended 2026-08-07, RESOLVED later the same day (session BOB, under Bob's tactical delegation — `IS-SWEEP-2026-08-07.md` §4b item 7): **CHECK is the investigative session's first DEPLOYED MODE** — the session run with its stated objective against an existing conclusion IS the CHECK role, so the enacted CHECK-first instruction is satisfied by one build, not a separate prior one. Milestone token corrected M4→M9 2026-08-07 per MILESTONES.md's M9 placement note (finding F8) |

| D-212 | design | 2026-08-05 | **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-05 by the VERSIONS design — the question this row asked turned out not to be a record question at all.** The row went through three drafts and each was wrong in an instructive way, so the history is kept rather than deleted. **Draft 1** said claims must be recordable as agreeing or disagreeing along a range, the hazard being a single collapsed number. Bob refuted the premise with the case the row was built on — an award that skipped competitive bidding plus an emergency exemption: *"I see two pieces that when taken together tell the story of what happened and why - perhaps justifiably, perhaps not."* They do not conflict, and whether either is RELEVANT depends on what the inquiry asks. **Draft 2** replaced that with three stored objects — support, undercut, rebuttal — where an undercut kills the inference from a leg to a claim while the leg stays true. Bob refuted that too, and more fundamentally: *"There may be evidence, even a single sentence of evidence, that supports and undercuts and rebuts a claim - all in the same sentence."* Roles are not types; one sentence plays all three at once against different claims. **THE ANSWER, and it makes the system smaller:** *"the only way to make sense of how the various legs of a claim come together is for an AI to be involved, for it to understand the facts in context and adjust the legs so that they properly tell the story and assign strength values that when calculated are supported by the evidence."* The calculation stays as simple as it is; the intelligence goes into how the legs are formed and weighted, not into relationships the record computes over. What the record holds is the legs, their strengths, and a written account of why they tell this story — carried by a VERSION (`INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §5, §6). **Nothing is owed on this row.** The lesson worth carrying: two attempts to solve a judgement problem with schema, both by a session doing structure where the question was understanding. | SUPERSEDED by DEC-60's versions design — no work owed; kept for the two refuted drafts |
| D-213 | design | 2026-08-05 | **ANSWERED 2026-08-06 by Bob: discovered evidence not related to the current inquiry is CAPTURED and recorded as an ACTIONABLE NOTIFICATION the member can act on later.** The problem, raised by DEC-60: the investigative session reads ALL of a project's inquiries for context but writes only to the SUBJECT one, so evidence it turns up bearing on inquiry B — the same vendor holding three other contracts, met while investigating whether one was competitively bid — had nowhere to go and was dropped by construction. The basis names both questions apart (`found_while_working` / `bears_on`), carries both principals through PL-4's one composer, and reports `basis_entry` as a MEASUREMENT over both basis projections — three-valued (`undetermined` / `absent` / `present`) so a reader can tell *no document holds these bytes yet* from *looked for and part of no case* from *a reading carries it*. `options[]` is real (document-grain acts on inquiry B) with the inquiry-grain gap DECLARED on the item as `options_grain` rather than hidden behind an empty array — D-222's grain problem, still open, now visible on the member-facing object instead of being a silence. **Two things the close condition did not predict, both recorded rather than absorbed:** (1) the mint fenced only CONDITION kinds, so PL-15 SWEPT the class — every item's kind is now checked against the catalogue whatever its class, split into C-31.2 `NO_SUCH_KIND` and C-31.3 `KIND_MISCLASSED` because unknown is not the same as misfiled; and (2) the mint ran AFTER the personal mute loop, so a FINDING minted under a kind the catalogue files as a CONDITION — the one misfiling `queueMute`'s write fence accepts — could be muted away with the mint blind to it. The mint now runs first. Both found by RUNNING the control, not by reading. **D-194/D-184 remain open beside it** (the same gap for a member's OWN leads) and are unchanged by this |
| D-214 | design | 2026-08-05 | **ANSWERED THE DAY IT WAS RAISED — Bob, 2026-08-05: `rejected` is BOTH an act AND a state of a suggested leg, and BOTH `considering` and `rejected` are REVERSIBLE.** Raised by DEC-60 and closed in the same session; kept as a row because IS-1 builds to it and the reasoning must reach whoever writes the state machine. What this settles, in the terms the builder needs: (1) the leg REMAINS A SUGGESTION throughout — `rejected` is a state the suggested leg is in, not a separate object and not a deletion, so a rejected leg is still readable, still shows its basis, and still counts toward nothing; (2) the REJECTION ACT is recorded in its own right, which is what makes a PATTERN over rejections queryable — and that pattern is where DEC-60's third argument lives, since a member who rejects every suggestion running against their thesis is visible only if the acts persist; (3) **REVERSIBILITY answers the question that made this hard** — a rejected leg that new evidence would revive can simply be un-rejected, so nothing needs a terminal state and nothing is lost by rejecting early. Note for IS-1: reversibility means the four states are NOT a one-way ladder, so the transition table must be authored rather than assumed, and each reversal is itself a member act that is recorded (an un-rejection is evidence about the member's attention exactly as the rejection was). | M9 · answered 2026-08-05 (DEC-60, Bob) — IS-1 UNBLOCKED on this axis; the row closes when IS-1 lands with the transition table and its negative control |
| D-215 | design | 2026-08-05 | **Two mechanisms proposed inside DEC-60's design and NOT ruled.** *(Rewritten 2026-08-05 in plain terms at Bob's instruction — the first draft called (1) a "congeniality self-report", a phrase invented in the session that wrote it and decodable by nobody else. Term dropped; both are in `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §7 and §8, marked proposed.)* **(1) ANSWERED 2026-08-05 — Bob: the AI SKILL's requirements include MINIMISING these effects.** Constrain the skill at the source rather than bolting a report onto the output; DEC-46's lens-may-be-preserved-not-applied is the standard the skill is written against. Recorded as a skill requirement in `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §11, not as a mechanism. The original proposal, for the record: The session is given the net bias (project + instance combined). It can use that two ways. Silently — letting the declared position shape what it proposes, which is the lens being APPLIED and is what DEC-46 forbade for import, where a travelling manifest may PRESERVE a lens and may not apply it. Or out loud — attaching to a suggestion: *this agrees with the position the project has declared; here is what I went looking for that would have cut against it, and here is what I found.* The second turns the bias from something that could quietly steer the run into something a member can CHECK, and it is a thing a member reviewing their own work cannot do for themselves: nobody can reliably notice when they are agreeing with themselves. **(2) A SUGGESTION CARRIES THE CONDITIONS IT WAS FORMED UNDER.** Bob, 2026-08-05: *"everything can change at the drop of a hat (bias, standard, claims)"*. So a suggestion is only interpretable against the bias, the evidence standard, and the claim set that were in force when it was written. Carried with it, a member opening a month-old suggestion can see it was formed under a different bar, or before three claims were accepted, or under a position the project has since revised. Not carried, a stale suggestion looks exactly like a current one. This is the shape DEC-54 already uses for a pinned policy — source, date and hash recorded, with cases naming the version. **(2) ANSWERED 2026-08-06 — Bob: a claim version does not need to know the version previously in place, nor the bias, nor the evidence standard.** The reason is internal to the versions design and the session's own argument for recording them was wrong: **a version is a COMPLETE ACCOUNT, not a patch.** Were versions diffs, what preceded them would matter; they are not, so it does not. The same logic disposes of the other two — a version is evaluated against the CURRENT standard, so which bar it was written under tells a reader nothing they would act on; and the bias ought not to have shaped it at all, since minimising that is a requirement ON THE SKILL (see (1)), so recording it would be recording something that should not have mattered. The session's argument was that a stale version reads as a current one: real, but the remedy was wrong and far heavier than needed, since a version carries a date and names its run anyway. **What IS kept, for a different reason: the run's OBSERVATION LOG** — where it searched and where it stopped — which is not about which rules applied but about what evidence was reachable at the time, and that is what a later reader needs to judge whether the account was well-founded. | M4 · closed 2026-08-06 (DEC-60, Bob) — both halves answered; the observation log survives as part of the run object, not as version metadata. Correction 2026-08-07 (`IS-SWEEP-2026-08-07.md` §1.2): the BIAS half of (2) was ALREADY RULED before this row was raised — `Content_Framework:1129-1141`: an assistant-surfaced focus must carry the bias manifest in force when it was surfaced, since unlike a member it will not remember — so the closure stands but its reasoning line must cite the standing ruling rather than dispose of the question fresh |
| D-216 | design | 2026-08-05 | **CHECKED AND ANSWERED 2026-08-08 (W0's slot-free model-check lane). THE ANSWER IS PER-PROJECT: `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §7 IS CORRECT AND CLONING IS NOT THE HONEST ANSWER.** The question was whether an inquiry shared across projects has ONE stance every referencing project must share, or a per-project one — §7 ruled per-project, Bob's first instinct was to CLONE the inquiry on divergence, and cloning was rejected because it duplicates the whole evidence trail and the copies immediately drift, so the shared investigation stops being shared. **DRIVEN, NOT READ — the instrument is `bio-plane/test/d216-sharing.probe.mjs` (38/38) with `d216-sharing.control.mjs` (3 arms as declared); the full record is in `MEASUREMENTS.md`, 2026-08-08.** Two projects stood on two different readings of one shared inquiry simultaneously, the plane refused neither, and after divergence each still saw every version and every leg of the other's; the sharing edge is a `cites` row in `refs` (`op=backlinks` reports it, `op=stats.refs` grew by exactly 2, and `op=strengthbarof` composes strictest-per-axis across BOTH citing projects — an answer no single-project walk can produce); the inquiry's own bytes carry NO stance and a read naming no project gets no `current` field at all. **NOTHING IN THE MODEL REQUIRES ONE SHARED STANCE** — the edge is load-bearing (severing it refuses the stance `VERSION_CURRENT_UNRELATED`), the mechanism is not two-only, and the one cross-project composition in the model is the BAR and not the stance. **TWO CORRECTIONS THE CHECK FORCED. (i) §7's and this row's own cited verification was WRONG: `op=linkproject` is link + project-as-a-VERB — it takes a `capture=<sha256>` and projects that capture's resolved links into `links_to` edges — and it neither takes a project bundle nor writes a `cites` edge. The conclusion was right; the evidence was a name collision. (ii) THE FINDING THAT OUTRANKS THE ANSWER: `op=cite` REFUSES an inquiry on the PROJECT arm (`NOT_INFORMATION`), so the edge the whole IS build hangs on has NO CURATED PRODUCER — a project draws on a question only by authoring its `bundle.md` and calling `op=promote`. PL-2's fixture hand-authored exactly that, which is why its suite drove the gate without discovering the gate has no door.** | M9 · measured 2026-08-08 — **IS-3 UNBLOCKED**; PL-13 survives with PL-2's pointer confirmed, its remaining scope the two notification slugs (asserted ABSENT) plus the new `op=cite`/`op=sever` project-arm gap |
| D-217 | design | 2026-08-05 | **BOTH HALVES ANSWERED 2026-08-06 by Bob.** Raised by DEC-60's versions design. **(a) REVIEW BURDEN — versions form a DERIVATION TREE, and accepting one prunes the branch.** The problem: a background run adds a version whenever its output differs from every existing one, so a claim under frequent investigation accumulates versions faster than anyone reads them — each correctly labelled a suggestion, the whole unexamined. Bob: *"make it easy to delete versions, or allow versions to be a tree that expresses how one derives from another, so a user can delete all previous derivations when they accept one as the one they want."* **The tree is already half-built into the design** — `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §6.3 has editing a version DERIVE a new one from it — so this makes the derivation edge EXPLICIT and adds the prune. Consequences for the builder: a version records what it was derived from (null for one a run composed fresh); accepting a version offers deletion of the ancestors it descends from, as an offer and not an automatic act, since a member may want a rejected reading kept as the record of what was considered; and deletion is a MEMBER act — the AI cannot delete a version any more than it can accept one. **(b) A REWORDED CLAIM IS USER SELECTABLE** — the member chooses whether rewording a claim produces a new VERSION of it or a NEW CLAIM. **So this is not a schema decision, it is a runtime one, and the schema must support both.** The reason both are legitimate: a tightened wording that leaves the legs meaning what they meant is a version; a reworded claim that changes what the legs are answering is a different claim wearing a similar sentence, and forcing either into the other loses something. It interacts with the question-sharpening suggestion (§8), which can reword an INQUIRY's question and thereby move every claim beneath it — that path should offer the same choice rather than deciding for the member. | M9 · answered 2026-08-06 (DEC-60, Bob) — closes when the derivation edge, the prune offer (HIDE, never delete — `IS-SWEEP-2026-08-07.md` §4b item 6) and the version-or-new-claim choice land with IS-1 |
| D-218 | design | 2026-08-05 | **UNMEASURED: whether a fleet-member Worker can hold a WHOLE investigative run inside the paid CPU ceiling.** Raised by DEC-60's integration architecture (`INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §14a), where Bob's Claude-account cascade (member, else project, else instance) settles that the agent loop runs in the GROUP'S OWN infrastructure — which preserves the installer's sovereignty promise and points at the fleet-member shape `pdf-worker` already established (I6). **THE REASONING THAT MUST NOT BE TRUSTED UNTIL MEASURED:** an agent loop spends most of its time WAITING on API responses, and Workers bill CPU rather than wall time, so a run of many minutes may sit well inside the ceiling. That is an argument, not a measurement, and this project has a standing rule against exactly this move — three claims the archive design rested on were wrong when finally measured, every one having sat in the design document for weeks. **WHAT IT DECIDES:** whether a fleet member can hold an entire run, or whether a run must be structured as many short invocations with its state carried between them, which is a materially different build. **RELATED MEASURED FACT:** Workers Paid was confirmed on this account 2026-08-04 (`MEASUREMENTS.md`), moving the CPU line from 10 ms to 30 s — so the question is not whether there is headroom but whether a whole run fits in it. NEEDS: a probe running a representative agent loop in a Worker and reporting CPU consumed against wall time, recorded in MEASUREMENTS.md before IS work that assumes either shape. | M9 · **MEASURED 2026-08-08 (FL-1) — the CPU question is ANSWERED and it is not the binding one.** `MEASUREMENTS.md`, FL-1 entry; instrument the Cloudflare GraphQL Analytics API (`workersInvocationsAdaptive.sum.cpuTimeUs`), read off a DEPLOYED probe, never the Workers own clock. **The reasoning this row said not to trust is CONFIRMED: 50.0 s of wall time waiting on subrequests cost 4.29 ms of billed CPU (~0.16 ms per awaited subrequest, independent of wait length).** A 25-turn run-shaped loop over REAL plane payloads costs 24.29 ms; 100 turns 189.28 ms; 400 turns 4,488.9 ms — CPU grows as ~n^1.9 (the transcript re-serialised every turn), putting ~1,100 turns inside the 30 s default and ~3,700 inside the 5-minute maximum (extrapolated from measured points, labelled as such). **BUT MEMORY BINDS FIRST BY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE: memoryUsageBytesP99 reaches 120.4 MB of the 128 MB isolate at 200 turns while CPU is still 2.5% of the ceiling.** So a fleet member CAN hold a long run as far as CPU is concerned, and CANNOT hold an unbounded one because of memory — which lands where §14a already put it: **the resumable shape is the one to build, and the number sizes the segment rather than deciding the shape.** OPEN only for FL-3 to consume; nothing further to measure here |
| D-219 | defect | 2026-08-06 | **A stored, member-facing method string says "with no captured basis" and reads as "with no basis", which is FALSE — the basis is REQUIRED.** Found 2026-08-06 while answering Bob's question *"What is testimony with no basis? Where did it come from then? Isn't there always context for how something got into the system?"* — and his instinct was right. `testifyResolution` (`store.mjs:8398`) REFUSES without one: `NO_BASIS` — *"grade D is recorded testimony: it carries the member's stated basis, with an author and a date"*. So a grade-D testimony always carries the member's stated reason, an author and a date. What it lacks is a CAPTURED DOCUMENT supporting the link, which is why it grades weakest. **But the method string written into the record reads** `testimony -- asserted by <who> with no captured basis (framework 8.1 grade D)` **, and a reader meeting "no captured basis" hears "no basis at all" — the record describing its own contents more weakly than they are.** This is the inverse of the usual defect class here (a comment claiming a stronger property than the code enforces); it is a stored string claiming a WEAKER one, and it is worse than a comment because it is member-facing and persisted on every grade-D resolution. NEEDS: the string to say what is actually missing — no captured document — while keeping the grade and the framework reference. Also worth a pass for siblings: any other place where "no basis" is used to mean "no captured basis". | M8 · open — one string plus its assertions; it rides whatever next touches the resolution path |
| D-220 | design | 2026-08-06 | **VERSIONS OF ONE DOCUMENT ARE ALREADY RECORDED AND NOTHING EXPOSES THEM, so the code that needs the version chain reconstructs it by full-text search and gets it wrong.** RULED by Bob 2026-08-06: *"Versions of the same document should be linked so that the system recognizes that reality. Each version should also be indexed by the same url (or id that refers back to the url.) These capabilities should be used in code that would benefit to know about this versioning reality."* **THE INDEX HE IS DESCRIBING ALREADY EXISTS** — verified in `schema.mjs` 2026-08-06: `captured_locators` is keyed `(address_norm, capture_sha, via)` with an index on `(address_norm, first_retrieved)`, carrying `first_retrieved`, `last_retrieved` and `observations`; `register` maps `capture_sha` to `bundle_id` and is indexed both ways. **So "every version at this address, in date order, with its bundle" is ONE INDEXED JOIN over two existing tables.** The versioning reality is recorded; no op answers it. **THEREFORE: DO NOT ADD AN EDGE BETWEEN VERSIONS.** An explicit `supersedes` relation would be a SECOND COPY of a fact the record already holds, which is the mirror-and-drift class this project refuses (D-164's solve-it-once; D-138's guard that guarded nothing). The link Bob asks for is the join, EXPOSED. **WHAT THE UNCHANGED CASE ALREADY DOES RIGHT, so it is not re-litigated:** a re-capture of identical content creates no bundle and no second register entry — it advances `last_retrieved` and increments `observations` on the existing row (Bob's own ruling, 2026-07-30, `LINK-FIDELITY.md`). What has no structural form is the CHANGED case, where a new bundle is created and its only tie to its predecessor is a SENTENCE OF PROSE in the body naming the earlier bundle id. **CODE THAT WOULD BENEFIT (Bob's third clause), in the order the benefit is largest:** (1) `heldMatch` in `civicos-ui/app.html`, which today searches `locator:"<url>"` and is the site of D-221; the join replaces the search and removes that defect by construction rather than patching it. (2) The **investigative session** (DEC-60): a run that reads sixty versions of one calendar as sixty documents has a distorted picture of what the record holds, which is the false-coverage hazard `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` names, arriving at the document level. (3) **Search result rendering** — sixty bundles at one address are sixty versions of one document, and a result list presenting them as sixty documents misrepresents the record. (4) **Monitoring** and (5) **`op=audit`'s cross-bundle duplicate sweep**, both of which D-60 records as comparing raw hashes; that line is partly stale (see D-60's correction) and the join gives either one its candidate set directly. (6) **A reader of a citation** — a case cites a specific bundle and that citation must never drift, but the LIVE record can show that later versions exist (a published case cannot be affected, and must not be). **RESEARCHED IN DEPTH 2026-08-06 (sub-session), and the fix is SMALLER than this row assumed — both halves already exist in code.** (i) **The read method exists and was deliberately left unwired:** `Store#capturedLocators({ addressNorm })` (`store.mjs:14779`, REC-26), whose own docstring says *"Deliberately not wired to a control-plane op."* **Its only caller anywhere in the repo is `test/monitor-cadence.test.mjs:415`** — in production nothing reads it. (ii) **The join already exists:** `#conditionBundlesForHost` (`store.mjs:9991`) already runs `captured_locators JOIN register ON r.capture_sha = cl.capture_sha`, scoped by GLOB to a host prefix and used to pick queue-condition subjects. So D-220 is *generalise an existing join and wire an existing method to an op* — not new machinery. (iii) **One write site, and it is unconditional:** `recordCapturedLocator` (`store.mjs:14747`), called only from `op=acquire` (`index.mjs:2938`) for every capture. New bytes at a known address INSERT a row; the same bytes seen again bump `observations` and widen the `MIN`/`MAX` interval — *"Seeing the same bytes again later is not a duplicate, it is the observation that proves the target held still in between."* `via` is in the key deliberately (D-96): an archive sighting of the same bytes is a different fact. Address normalisation is `normalizeAddress` (`subresources.mjs:520`) and is deliberately conservative — it does NOT strip trailing slashes or case-fold the path, because *"a normalisation MISS looks exactly like 'not captured'."* **CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN CONSUMER LIST:** consumer (5) named `op=audit`'s *cross-bundle duplicate sweep*. **No such sweep exists.** `auditPass` iterates bundles and calls `checkBundle` per bundle; `checkRegisterIntegrity` (`bio-checks.mjs:3536`) buckets within one bundle's `provenance.json` only. The phrase was inherited from D-60 and is a category error — there is nothing cross-bundle to move onto the join, which makes the join the thing that would CREATE that capability rather than improve it. **A CONSUMER THE ROW MISSED, and it is the largest one:** **MONITORING SELECTS PER BUNDLE, NEVER PER ADDRESS** (`store.mjs:15983` `#monitorCadencePlan`, `:16023` `#monitorCadenceTick`, keyed on `bundles.monitor_enabled/monitor_frequency/monitor_last_checked`). So sixty versions of one calendar are **sixty independent monitor subjects** — the same address fetched sixty times on sixty schedules. Monitoring an ADDRESS is what a member means; monitoring a bundle is what the code does. | M3 · open (Bob 2026-08-06) — design settled, no schema change, and the implementation is smaller than assumed: **generalise `#conditionBundlesForHost`'s join, wire `capturedLocators` to an op**, then consumers — (1) `heldMatch` (closes D-221), (2) monitoring per address, (3) the investigative session, (4) search rendering |
| D-221 | defect | 2026-08-06 | **The "changed from" sentence written into a new capture names the WRONG predecessor — almost certainly the OLDEST version at that address rather than the previous one.** Traced in source 2026-08-06. `heldMatch` (`civicos-ui/app.html:13614`) finds prior captures at an address with the query `locator:"<url>"`. **That is not an address comparison:** `locator` is a full-text-indexed field (`query.mjs:61`, `FTS_COLUMNS`), and a fielded query on a field carrying `fts` compiles to a TEXT ATOM (`query.mjs:499`, `if (f.fts) return textAtom(...)`), not to column equality. A text atom creates a rank arm, so the default order is RELEVANCE (`query.mjs:732-733`, `ctx.sort?.field || (rank ? "relevance" : "updated")`). Every capture at one address carries identical URL text, so the bm25 scores tie and the declared tiebreak — `bundle_id ASC` — decides the order. `heldMatch` then returns on the FIRST comparable row it walks. **So on a calendar captured weekly for a year, the record can state "the source has since published a changed document" against a snapshot twelve months old, and present it to the member as the immediately preceding version.** The sentence is written into the bundle body at `app.html:13485` and is permanent in that bundle. SECOND, SMALLER RISK, mechanism-only and NOT measured: because the match is a text search rather than an equality, two different URLs that tokenise alike — same host and path, different query string — can match each other. FIX: D-220's join, which answers by address and orders by date; ordering the existing search explicitly would work and would leave the fragile lookup in place. **UI's paths, so this is a DELEGATION and not an edit from BOB** — recorded here because BOB traced it. | M3 · **CLOSED 2026-08-08 by UI-50**, exactly as this row said it would be — by D-220's consumer rather than separately. `heldMatch`'s address arm asks `op=versionchain` (PL-10, I3 10.2.0) for the chain's `total` and seeks the LAST version at `offset = total - 1`; that version is the predecessor of a capture not yet written, and it is what the sentence names. **`at=<sha>` is NOT used and the reason is measured rather than preferred:** the chain joins `register`, whose row is written by `op=promote`, so at the moment this check runs the fresh capture is not IN the chain and cannot be an anchor. `predecessor: null` at index 0 has its analogue in `total: 0` and renders as an honest absence. Proved in `civicos-ui/test/version-predecessor.test.mjs` over SIXTY chain lengths with ground truth computed in JavaScript and never read back from the op, with the trap proved armed on every subset. The row's SECOND, unmeasured risk — two URLs that tokenise alike matching each other — **goes with it**: the chain is keyed on `address_norm` equality and consults no text index at all. **What does NOT go with it is the sentences ALREADY WRITTEN; see D-236.** |
| D-222 | design | 2026-08-06 | **THE MEANING LAYER HAS NO QUERY SURFACE, and the investigative session is the first consumer that genuinely needs one — so this is a PRECONDITION of DEC-60, not an enhancement.** Raised 2026-08-06 by the rigorous architecture pass Bob asked for. **THE MEASUREMENT, from `STORE-AS-CACHE.md` and re-verified:** BIO has two retrieval routes. **Route 1**, the query compiler (`query.mjs`), projects **34 filterable fields and exactly 5 FTS columns** (`title`, `body`, `meta`, `locator`, `authority`). **Route 2**, the meaning tables — `readings`, `reading_refs`, `resolutions`, `connections`, `inquiry_basis` — is **not reachable by the query compiler at all.** **WHY THE HOLE IS INVISIBLE, which is worse than the hole:** route 1 carries SCALAR SUMMARIES of the meaning layer onto the bundle row (`capture` → `inquiry_capture_strength`, `legs` → `inquiry_basis_count`, `connection` → `inquiry_connection_strength`, `resolution` → `action_resolution`), so a caller can filter by a finding's STRENGTH and never reach the legs that produced it. `STORE-AS-CACHE.md`'s own words: *the projection creates a false sense of coverage — the meaning layer is visible as a number and unreachable as a structure.* **WHY IT BLOCKS THE SESSION:** forming a version of a claim's legs (DEC-60) is meaning-layer work end to end. As `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §3 is written, the session's read scope is NOT ACHIEVABLE — it can see that a claim scores 0.7 and cannot see what it rests on, which is precisely the work it exists to do. **RELATED AND SAME-SHAPED: D-220**, where sixty versions of one document read as sixty documents is the identical false-coverage failure one axis over. **RESEARCHED IN DEPTH 2026-08-06 (sub-session), and the gap is wider than this row stated.** **(i) THIRTEEN TABLES, and about TWENTY-FOUR ops read them — NONE of which is a query surface.** Every one is a fixed-shape lookup keyed on exactly one of `capture_sha`, `ref`, `entity_id`, `relation_id`, `bundle_id`, `target_id` or `(progression_key, entity_id)`, or an unkeyed whole-table walk with a fixed output shape. **No predicate, no projection choice, no sort, no paging** (the one exception is `documentsNamingEntity`'s clamped limit). So the layer is not under-queried; it is UNQUERIED BY CONSTRUCTION. **(ii) GRADE IS A FILTER NOWHERE.** No op filters on grade at all, and **no grade column is indexed** — there is no `resolutions(grade)` or `connections(grade)`. The consequence with teeth: the C tier is explicitly *"FLAGGED for a member to confirm"* (`schema.mjs:876`) and **nothing can enumerate the flagged set** — `connectionsFor` refuses without a key (`store.mjs:8618`). A queue of work the system creates and cannot list. **(iii) NO CROSS-AXIS JOIN.** *"Every claim whose legs cite documents from authority X"* is two indexed columns and no join. **(iv) TIME IS A PREDICATE NOWHERE** — `at` is on nearly every table and filterable on none, so *"what resolved this week"* is unaskable. **(v) `readings.reading` IS AN OPAQUE JSON BLOB**, so nothing inside a reading is reachable except what `reading_refs`/`reading_ref_terms` already project out. **(vi) SEE D-223** — the sharpest single instance: hunch debt is publication-disqualifying and cannot be listed. NEEDS: a meaning-layer read surface. **Whether it extends the query compiler or is a second addressable surface is NOT a free choice — `query.mjs:701-705` states that a second query path is what the compiler's design exists to prevent, and D-15 gives visibility exactly ONE compilation point, enforced by a throw rather than a convention.** Options are being graded; D-223's question is acceptance on whichever wins. | M3 · open (DEC-60) — **BLOCKS the investigative session's read scope**; nothing else in the IS decomposition depends on it. Appended 2026-08-07: the options were GRADED 2026-08-06 in `INVESTIGATIVE-SESSION.md` §14c — recommendation D, staged as A then C; do not re-grade |
| D-223 | defect | 2026-08-06 | **HUNCH DEBT CANNOT BE ENUMERATED, and the schema itself calls it the one declared bias that DISQUALIFIES PUBLICATION.** Found 2026-08-06 by the meaning-layer research sub-session. `inquiry_basis` (`schema.mjs:1221`) carries `grade_source`, and `schema.mjs:1187` names a `hunch` leg as publication-disqualifying. **No op can answer *"every leg whose `grade_source` is hunch"*, corpus-wide or per project.** It is checked only PER BUNDLE at the two gates (`checkInquiryBasis`), which means the rule is enforced one document at a time and **the outstanding debt across a project is unknowable to the group that carries it**. The same is true of `role`, `ground` and `grade_axis` — so *"every inquiry with a `cuts_against` leg"* and *"every multi-ground basis"* (REC-42's OR branches) are equally unaskable. This is a case of the record enforcing a rule it cannot report on, and it bears directly on doctrine: a group cannot see its own exposure before it publishes, only be refused at the moment it tries. Bob's own vocabulary correction (D-188, *say HUNCH DEBT*) presumes the debt is visible as a quantity; it is not. | D-224 | design | 2026-08-06 | **`connections` grows QUADRATICALLY in the documents concerning one entity, and nothing caps, comments on, or measures it.** Found 2026-08-06 by the meaning-layer research sub-session. `deriveConnections` (`store.mjs:8548`) collapses `resolutions` to distinct captures per entity and then emits **every unordered pair** — a nested loop at `store.mjs:8566-8568`, so **k documents concerning one entity produce k(k−1)/2 rows**. At 100 documents that is 4,950 rows for one entity; at 1,000 it is 499,500. **There is no cap, no comment acknowledging the growth, and no measurement anywhere in the repo.** Three things make it worse rather than academic: (i) a heavily-covered subject — the entity a group's whole project is about — is exactly where k is largest, so the cost lands on the most important entity rather than a marginal one; (ii) the derivation is idempotent and alarm-driven (`connection-derive`, batch 100 entities per tick), so the write amplification arrives as sustained background work on a Durable Object whose storage ceiling is itself unrecorded debt (D-190); (iii) `stats()` counts `connections` but NOT `readings`, `reading_refs`, `reading_ref_terms` or `inquiry_basis`, so an operator watching for this has one number and no denominator. NEEDS: a measurement first — derive against a synthetic entity at k = 10/100/1000 and record rows, bytes and tick duration in `MEASUREMENTS.md` — then a decision about whether pairs are materialised at all above some k, or derived on read like progression instance grades already are (`#assembleInstance`, which stores nothing). **Do not cap it before measuring; the point of the row is that nobody knows the curve.** | M3 · **MEASURED 2026-08-08 (REC-66) — the measurement this row asked for, taken before anything was capped, and it is in `MEASUREMENTS.md` with its instrument (`bio-plane/test/connections-growth.measure.mjs`, re-runnable).** The curve is EXACTLY k(k-1)/2 at every level driven and the cost per row is FLAT at ~798 bytes: k=100 -> 4,950 rows / 3.9 MB / 53-65 ms; k=200 -> 19,900 / 15.9 MB / 268 ms; k=500 -> 124,750 / 100 MB / 2.0 s; **k=1,000 -> 499,500 rows / 398 MB / 10.3 s inside ONE synchronous transaction — about 4% of the 10 GB per-object ceiling D-190 records as a vendor claim, for ONE subject.** **THE NUMBERS SAID SO, so the cap landed with them:** REC-66 bounds the DERIVATION (not the answer) at 500 pairs by default and 5,000 at the ceiling, with the DOCUMENT bound derived as the inverse of the quadratic (32 and 100 documents), both figures the plane's own `#MEANING_LIMIT_*` pair. **STILL OPEN, and this is now the whole of the row:** whether pairs are MATERIALISED at all above some k, or derived on read like progression instance grades already are. The bound makes the cost knowable, refusable and stated; it does not decide the model. Also unmeasured: the sweep's cost across many dirty entities in ONE tick, and the read side over a large connection table |

| D-228 | defect | 2026-08-07 | **A QUOTED FIELD VALUE KEEPS ITS QUOTES, so `state:"open"` matches nothing and `title:"two words"` compiles to a mangled FTS5 expression.** Found 2026-08-07 by PL-8 while writing the meaning arm’s over-strictness control — an equally correct alternative phrasing that a member would type, and it did not work. The tokenizer has a branch for exactly this (`query.mjs`, `if (rest === "" && src[i] === a double quote)`, with a comment saying *`field:"two words"` splits at the quote, so back up and read the value properly rather than truncating it at the space*) and **that branch is UNREACHABLE**: the bare reader stops only at whitespace and parentheses, so it has already consumed the opening quote by the time `rest` is tested, and `rest` is never the empty string. MEASURED, through `compile()`: `state:"open"` binds the argument `"open"` WITH the quote characters, so an indexed equality against a corpus that stores `open` returns nothing and says nothing; `title:"two words"` binds the FTS5 expression `({title} : """two" AND "words""")`. **This is PRE-EXISTING and language-wide** — it belongs to every one of the 34 projected fields, not to the meaning arms, which merely inherit it. It is not fixed inside PL-8 because the fix changes what every selector matches (a query that returned nothing may start returning rows) and that is a change every retrieval suite must be re-run against, with its own control. **PINNED rather than left**: `meaningquery.test.mjs` asserts the CURRENT behaviour on both an arm and an ordinary field, so the fix flips a visible assertion instead of landing unnoticed. NEEDS: make the branch reachable (stop the bare reader at a quote, or split on the colon before reading the value), correct the two pins, and re-run the retrieval suites. | M3 · **CLOSED 2026-08-08 by REC-68.** THE MECHANISM, because the symptom was never the finding: the guard `rest === "" && src[i] === '"'` is UNSATISFIABLE against the reader that produces its inputs — the bare reader stopped only at whitespace and parens, so `src[i]` after it was whitespace, a paren or undefined and NEVER a quote; and a quote after the colon was CONSUMED, so `rest` began with `"` and was never empty. Each half falsified the other. THE FIX IS ONE TERMINATOR: the bare reader now stops at a quote, the rewind that undid its over-consumption is gone (its absence is what makes the branch reachable), and the branch is GENERALISED so a comparison lead (`created:>"2026-01-01"`, `fm:a.b="c"`) is not fixed-adjacent-but-broken. **MEASURED, and the two failure modes separate:** 28 of the 34 projected fields, the 3 meaning arms and `fm:` MATCHED NOTHING, which fails honestly; the 3 FTS columns MATCHED THE WRONG THING when the value was multi-word — `title:"Fund general"` returned a real row for a phrase in no title, because FTS5 binds a column filter to the phrase that FOLLOWS it, so only the first word was scoped. **And the sharpest, found by driving rather than by reading: a nothing-matching selector UNDER NEGATION returns THE WHOLE CORPUS** (`-state:"collected"` gave 3 of 3), so an honest empty answer became a confident complete one; `has:`/`sort:` dropped their arm with a warning, which widens the same way. PL-10's finding that the defect did not bite the FTS columns' SINGLE-word case is RE-MEASURED here rather than inherited and holds. PL-8's two pins CORRECTED with dated reasons, never exempted. Branch reachability is now asserted STRUCTURALLY as well as behaviourally, because an equivalent-mechanism revert round-trips every single-word value and only a structural pin can see it (NC arm d). Battery 123/123 at 7,805, +53 attributed across four suites. Rider done: `schema.mjs`'s grade_source comment named three sources against a catalogue of five and is now DRIVEN against `GRADE_SOURCES` by `hygiene.test.mjs`, in both directions and with a REACH delta. |

So CHECK 3's duplicate gate fired ONLY when both landed inside the SAME ONE-SECOND BUCKET. DRIVEN, not inferred: identical readings 0ms apart were refused, the same pair 1,200ms apart LANDED as a second version. **THE FLAKE WAS THE CHEAP HALF — the expensive half is that §6 rule 8 WAS UNENFORCED IN THE PLANE.** *A run adds its output as a new version ONLY IF it differs in substance from every existing one*, and any retry loop pausing a second between attempts wrote unbounded identical readings into a member's inquiry. The suite runs ~510ms and usually stayed inside one second standalone; under a loaded battery it did not, and a duplicate that LANDS makes the version count 7 where block 3 asserts 6 — which is exactly the 59/2 CONDUCT saw, while a boundary crossed only by the later renamed submission is the 60/1 PL-14 saw. **FIX: one expression, in the COMPARISON only** — the stamp is blanked out of the ground rows before comparing. Controls (D-231a) re-arms the clock and REPRODUCES the historical 2-failure signature deterministically; (D-231b) widens the exclusion by one field and the over-strictness arm fails instead. 12 arms run, 0 behaved other than declared. ## Dispositions, added 2026-07-31

Every open row now carries a leading DISPOSITION token in its Status column: a
milestone from `MILESTONES.md`, or `DOCTRINE (Bob)`, `ACCEPTED`, `WATCH, no task`, or
`NOT OURS TO MEASURE`. The reason is that this file had become the de facto backlog
while `QUEUE.md` held six items, so forward work was invisible inside a ledger nobody
could sort. **A row with no disposition is invisible work** — which is how Bob's
ruling that reused parts must be re-fetched at ratification went two design revisions
with nothing scheduling it and no row recording it.

The token says where the work is placed, not that it is scheduled. Only `QUEUE.md`
schedules. A new row takes a disposition at the moment it is written.

Two ledger corrections this pass found, both stale rather than wrong when written:

- **D-48** said `open, S-12 step 6`. It was closed by 0.28.0 (`PLAN.md` S-12 item 6
  names it explicitly) and the residue is D-50, the catalog half.
- **D-53** said it blocks S-11 step 5. That block is STALE: bulk release shipped in
  0.34.0 under Bob's 2026-07-27 ruling, which decoupled the operation from trust
  inheritance entirely. D-53 remains open as doctrine and blocks nothing today.

| D-242 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **THE MINTED-ID LEDGER (M0-17) IS SCOPED TO ONE CLONE ON ONE MACHINE, AND NOTHING SAYS SO AT THE MOMENT IT STOPS BEING TRUE.** `tools/mintid.mjs` takes an id by an exclusive create under `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` — the ONE `.git` every worktree of this checkout shares. That is deliberately the exact scope the collisions have: **every colliding pair on 2026-08-08 was two worktrees of one clone**, which is why this is the right mechanism today rather than a network allocator nobody would run. **What it does NOT cover: two clones, a CI runner, a second machine, or a network filesystem whose `O_EXCL` is not atomic (NFSv2 was not; APFS and ext4 are).** In any of those the ledgers are independent and two allocators can take the same number — back to the read-the-file convention, which is the graceful floor and not a silent failure, because the corpus floor still holds. **THE REAL COST IS NOT THE COLLISION, IT IS THE CONFIDENCE**: a worker that ran the tool believes its id is safe, and in a two-clone world it would be wrong for a reason no output mentions. NEEDS: either the tool DETECTS that its ledger is not the only one (there is no cheap local test for that, which is why it is a debt and not a fix), or the day workers run on more than one clone the allocator moves to something with a single writer — the plane's own `Store.allocId` already is one, under `ctx.storage.transactionSync`, and is the obvious destination. Relation: M0-17, D-238 (the stash defect, the same shared-common-gitdir fact from the painful side). **NARROWED 2026-08-08 (D-243's item), and the row's own sentence is what got tested: "there is no cheap local test for that" is TRUE of the two-clone half and was NOT true of the non-atomic-filesystem half.** `mint` now PROBES the real ledger directory before it trusts it — an exclusive create, a second exclusive create that must be refused `EEXIST`, and a re-read proving the first writer's bytes did not move — and **REFUSES `EXCL_NOT_HONOURED` rather than minting** if any of the three fails. That closes the NFSv2 line by measurement instead of accepting it on report. **The two-clone line is NOT closed and cannot be locally: nothing on this machine can see a second ledger.** What changed for it is that **the confidence is no longer silent** — every mint, every `--list` and every `--audit` now prints a SCOPE block naming what the take IS exclusive against (every worktree of this clone) and what it is NOT (any allocator on a different ledger), with a WARN when the ledger has been used from more than one host (shared storage, where a one-process probe cannot speak for cross-host atomicity) or when `BIO_IDALLOC_DIR` overrides the git-derived scope. **The decision, recorded: REFUSE when exclusivity is demonstrably absent, WARN and still mint when the scope is wider than what was tested, STATE always — because the happy path is where a false belief is formed.** **Still open:** two clones cannot be detected, and `--audit` section 2 reports an allocation above the ledger's high-water as a QUESTION precisely because a second ledger is one of its two explanations. | M0 · open — **WATCH, no task, and NARROWED rather than closed.** The trigger is unchanged and concrete: the first time a worker runs anywhere but a worktree of `/Users/sparky/ClaudeCodeBIO/bio`. |

| D-244 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **A JPEG PAGE THAT NEEDS TURNING CANNOT BE TURNED WITHOUT DESTROYING THE ONE THING THE PASS-THROUGH ROUTE IS FOR.** `pdf-worker/src/pagepixels.mjs` renders an image-only page by EXTRACTING its single embedded image (CPDF-12; DEC-42's observation verified at 24 of 24 image-only pages over 622 real Oakland pages). For `CCITTFaxDecode` the page's own `/Rotate` is applied exactly — a quarter turn lands a bit on a bit — and the result is `upright: true`. For `DCTDecode` the route hands on the publisher's own JPEG bytes UNTOUCHED, which is both the cheapest path and the strongest provenance position available (no transform of ours sits between the record and the pixels a reader checks a claim against); rotating it means decoding and re-encoding, which throws that away and is lossy besides. **MEASURED SIZE OF THE GAP: 3 of the 24 image-only pages are `DCTDecode` at `/Rotate 270`** (14 are `DCTDecode` at `/Rotate 0` and need nothing; 7 are `CCITTFaxDecode` at `/Rotate 270` and are handled). Those three come back `upright: false` with `rotate_deg` STATED — never silently sideways. **WHY IT MATTERS RATHER THAN BEING TIDINESS: a sideways page fed to an OCR engine does not fail, it INVENTS.** The same exhibit at `/Rotate 270`, un-rotated, scored **8.67% character accuracy with 355 MINTED DIGITS** in fluent confident prose and the engine announced nothing (MEASUREMENTS 2026-08-08); upright it scored 99.93% / 90-of-90 / 1. NEEDS one of: (a) a baseline JPEG decoder in the member (~600 lines, and it makes the route lossy — the honest cost is that the record then holds OUR re-encoding, so `text_source` would have to say so); (b) a LOSSLESS JPEG transform (a quarter turn is expressible as an MCU-block permutation for dimensions that are multiples of the MCU size, which these are not guaranteed to be); (c) the CONSUMER rotates, which is the cheapest and is what `upright: false` is for — CPDF-10 is the first consumer and this is a real input to its design. Relation: CPDF-12, CPDF-10, DEC-42. | M2 · open — **decide it in CPDF-10, where the consumer is.** Nothing is silently wrong today: the field says so. |

Stated as a decision in `kickoffs/CONTENT-PDF.md` with a provisional and its reversal cost. | D-250 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **THE RESIDUE REPORT'S TWO STATED BLIND SPOTS, WRITTEN DOWN SO THEY ARE A KNOWN LIMIT RATHER THAN A LATER SURPRISE.** Raised by D-237's reporting half in the same turn as the fix, over its own instrument. **(1) THE STANDING SCAN RECOGNISES ONE ARTIFACT.** It finds workerd persistence by miniflare's OWN directory naming (`miniflare-<Object>`, the vendor's spelling, the one `battery.mjs` already counts sandboxes by) at any depth under any shared root. **An ordinary fixture tree with an ordinary name is invisible to it** — `migrate.test.mjs`'s `/tmp/civicos-fixture` would have matched nothing. Such a directory is seen only while it is ARRIVING (the before/after diff) or being HELD (the pid chain), so **a suite that leaked one on some PREVIOUS run and no longer touches it is invisible to every arm**, which is the same eight-day silence D-237 is about, one artifact over. It is not closed by widening the recogniser: the alternative is a census of `/tmp`, measured at **861 top-level entries and 236 MB** on this machine, and a report whose first rows are always other people's work is a report nobody reads. **(2) THE PID-CHAIN ARM IS A SAMPLER.** `lsof` is called at 60 ms and 700 ms into each suite (17 ms scoped to node+workerd, against 118 ms unscoped — the cadence is a cost decision, measured). Suite durations run 32 ms to ~1.6 s with a MEDIAN OF 505 ms, so **10 of 133 suites finish before the first sample can be taken and the only conclusive arm never covers them** — and a file opened and closed between two samples is missed in any suite. **NEITHER IS SILENT, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS A GAP AND NOT A DEFECT:** the report prints `HELD arm: N lsof sample(s) covering M of K suite(s)` on every run and names the shortfall, so *we looked and found none* can never read as *we never looked*; both limits are stated in `scripts/residue.mjs`'s header; and the suite PINS them (arm (e) asserts the write-then-clean case produces nothing, arm (j) asserts the sampler actually sampled). WHAT WOULD CLOSE (1): a marker this estate's own sandboxes carry, so a ground is recognised by something we mint rather than by a name a vendor could rename. WHAT WOULD CLOSE (2): an in-process `fs` hook preloaded into every suite, which is conclusive and cheap but changes what every suite runs, and workerd writes its persistence from a separate binary that no node hook can see. Relation: D-237, D-186, D-233. | M0 · OPEN by decision — both limits are REPORTED on every run and PINNED by `test/battery-residue.test.mjs`; the row records what the instrument cannot reach, not a regression. |

With it, a layer whose producer names OCR software becomes `layer -> ocr(<product>)` with the product NAMED and the chain honest, and a layer with NO such marker stays `undetermined` — **never "authored", because an absent marker is an absent marker.** That last clause is the whole design and is why this is not a lookup table of OCR product names: the question cannot be answered in principle from a name string, so the DEFAULT must be undetermined and the classification must only ever be able to make the claim WEAKER. | D-252 | gap | 2026-08-08 | **TIER-3 ROUTING IS PER DOCUMENT, AND THE THING IT ROUTES IS PER PAGE.** `needsTier3` (`index.mjs`) answers TRUE when ANY page carries a `no_text_layer` marker, so a MIXED document — a text-layer report with three scanned exhibits stapled to the back, which is an ordinary shape for a Council packet — routes the WHOLE document to OCR. Today that costs nothing real, because no OCR member exists and the branch is untaken; the day one does, the cost is transcribing pages that already have perfectly good text and, worse, **REPLACING** them: the wire assigns `i2text = built.text` wholesale, so a good text layer would be overwritten by an OCR pass at cap `C` when the layer was better. **STATED AT THE PREDICATE rather than left to be discovered**, and it is a real limit rather than a hypothetical one: CPDF-9's exhibit was wholly image-only, so the mixed case was never in the measured corpus and nobody has counted how common it is. NEEDS: per-page routing — the member takes a PAGE LIST, and the wire merges its answer page-wise into the Tier-1/Tier-2 text rather than replacing the document. That needs a producer that works a page at a time, which is CPDF-12's to define, so the merge is written in the turn the member lands rather than guessed at now. Relation: CPDF-10 (the seam), CPDF-12 (the producer), I2's per-page text shape (which already has the right grain — `text.pages[]` — so nothing new is needed on the contract side). **RESOLVED 2026-08-09.** The row said the merge wanted writing in the turn that lands the member; establishing whether that turn had arrived was the first question and the answer is NO — CPDF-12 landed the RENDERER (`pdf-worker/src/pagepixels.mjs`), there is no `/transcribe` producer and no `OCR_WORKER` binding anywhere outside this suite's stub (grepped: the only two occurrences in the repository are the wire's own call and the test stub). **But the merge is not speculative and did not need one**, and that is the correction to the row: the two things being merged are BOTH landed shapes — the Tier-1/Tier-2 I2 text in hand, and `ocrTextFromMember`'s output, whose contract this file already fixes completely. So what was written is the CONSUMER-side rule, driven end to end through the op against the stub member that already existed. **WHAT LANDED: (1)** `tier3Pages` — the per-page grain, read from the same marker vocabulary as the routing predicate; `needsTier3` is DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED because a pageless `no_text_layer` marker must still select the document. **(2)** the page list travels in the `/transcribe` request as a HINT (the cost half — a member need not transcribe pages that already have text) and is re-checked on the way back, because a hint the consumer trusts is a contract enforced at the wrong end. **(3)** `mergeTier3Text` — an OCR page may FILL a page, never REPLACE one, on TWO conditions: the page carries the marker AND its base text is empty. The second is what makes the guarantee unconditional rather than dependent on a producer's own claim about its output. **(4)** the doctrine half, which is the part worth reading: a mixed document's chain is now the CONCATENATION of its parts, each derivation step carrying `extent: {kind:'pages', pages:[…]}`, and **a mixed document's `derivation_cap` is UNDETERMINED rather than the engine's letter** — because a text layer's fidelity is `null` and letting an OCR pass's `C` stand for the whole document would RESOLVE THAT NULL INTO A LETTER. That is not cosmetic: `gradeCeiling` reads null as "undetermined, which is a statement, not a permission" and `C` as permission up to C, so the merge would have handed a leg citing an unmeasured text layer a ceiling nobody measured. A leg citing a PAGE still gets that page's own cap (`derivationCap(chain, target)`), so the mixed document stays usable without the document-level claim being inflated. **(5)** a document that READ is now allowed to say what it could not read: `ocrNote` reached only the FAILED branch, so a mixed document read off its text layer said nothing at all about its exhibits. **A DOCUMENT WITH ONE PROVENANCE IS UNTOUCHED** — `mergedChain` of one part returns that part's chain unscoped, which is the over-strictness arm and is why every landed CPDF-10 assertion still passes unchanged. NEGATIVE CONTROLS: five new arms in `test/nc-cpdf10.mjs`, each armed ALONE; arm (g) restores the wholesale assignment and the suite fails 11 arms with THE HEADLINE first (the good page's references gone). | D-266 | gap | 2026-08-09 | **A FINDING CAN BE DISPOSITIONED ON EXACTLY ONE IDENTITY — A DEFINED PROGRESSION'S REAL STAGE — AND THREE LIVE KINDS CARRY NONE.** Raised by PL-13 at its own landing, and it is the PLANE half of the question UI-45 declined to answer and handed over by name. `proposal_dispositions`' primary key IS `(progression_key, stage_key)`; `proposeDispose` checks the pair against `progression_defs`/`progression_stages` and refuses `NO_SUCH_PROGRESSION`/`BAD_STAGE`. So Adopt/Defer/Dismiss are completable on `missing_predecessor` and `overdue_successor` and on nothing else. **PL-15's `out-of-inquiry-lead` and PL-13's two new slugs carry no such pair by design** — they are about a document, a question and a reading — so all three controls could only ever have been refused, which is exactly the live defect UI-45 found on the surface and fixed there. **WHAT PL-13 DID, AND IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT THIS ROW'S ANSWER: the plane now PUBLISHES the identity** on every queue item (`disposition: { available, keyed_on, key, reason, instead, detail }`, derived once at the mint by `#dispositionOf`), so a surface reads the answer instead of reconstructing the act's key from field names it learned by reading producers — the DEC-8 drift class. `civicos-ui/app.html`'s `notifDispositionKeyed` now reads it, keeping its old basis-shape read as a NAMED fallback for a plane built before this. **WHAT IS STILL OPEN IS A DOCTRINE QUESTION AND NOT PLUMBING.** Widening the act needs a second key shape, a migration, and — the hard part — a ruling on what DECLINING MEANS FOR A FINDING THAT IS RECOMPUTED ON EVERY READ: a dismissed stance-divergence must either un-dismiss itself when either project moves again, or silence a fact that has since changed, and neither is obviously right. That is D-222's grain problem at its third instance (PL-15 named the second). **A SECOND, SMALLER GAP IS FOLDED IN HERE RATHER THAN GIVEN ITS OWN ROW, because it has the same fix:** `new-version-arrived-from-another-team` reads its source team from `ai_runs.context` and MINTS NOTHING when a version was composed by hand or by a run whose context is not a project — a hand-composed reading of a shared question genuinely does reach the other team unannounced. That silence is DRIVEN (`bio-plane/test/current.test.mjs` §5) rather than described, and closing it needs the same thing: an identity the record can attribute a version's authorship to. NEEDS: the ruling on what a disposition means for a derived finding, then either a widened key or a published statement that these kinds are read-and-act-elsewhere and never dispositioned. Relation: D-222, UI-45's delegation, PL-15, REC-7/D-79. **THE RULING IS MADE AND IT WAS NOT BOB'S TO MAKE, 2026-08-09 (worktree-agent-a6db8a28ca10a37e0), because THE REPOSITORY ALREADY ANSWERED IT — four times, three of them at the site.** D-79: *AGE RATHER THAN VANISH … a finding that disappears is indistinguishable from one that was never made, and that rule does not relax because the finder was a machine.* `proposeDispose`'s own header: *Doctrine is SETTLED (D-79) … DECLINING IS NOT AUTHORING*, and at its validity check: *it does NOT require a gap to currently fire: the disposition is a standing decision keyed by identity, and D-79 keeps it until re-triaged even if the gap comes and goes.* `proposalsFeed`: the decision is returned *independent of whether the underlying gap still fires*, and a stage that later crosses a DEADLINE does **not** re-ask a member who set it aside — which is the record already accepting, in the sharpest available case, that a standing dismissal survives its inputs changing. **SO: A DISPOSITION IS A FACT ABOUT THE SUBJECT'S STABLE IDENTITY, NOT ABOUT THE INPUTS; IT STANDS UNTIL RE-TRIAGED; IT AGES THE FINDING AND NEVER DELETES IT.** Of the three records the question offered — *about the inputs*, *decays silently*, *re-derived too* — the answer is a fourth the record had already chosen, and it is the only one under which nothing quietly reopens. **AND THE ROW'S OWN PREMISE WAS MEASURABLY WRONG, which is what made the question answerable: the two kinds that ARE dispositionable are THEMSELVES recomputed on every read** — `proposalsFeed` rebuilds them from `progression_instances` at every call and `queueFeed` stamps them `age: { state: "undetermined", reason: "derived_on_read" }` with its own hand. Being derived was never what withheld the act; carrying no identity the act is keyed on was. **WHAT LANDED, and it is the ruling's teeth rather than a feature: `op=queue` PUBLISHES THE AGED DECISIONS** (`disposed: { personal:false, findings[], count, recorded, bound, truncated, detail }`, IC-57, additive), because this op had inherited `proposalsFeed`'s AGEING and published NONE of it — a dismissed finding and a never-derived one read identically on the one feed a member opens by habit, and `civicos-ui/app.html` had compensated with a PAGE-LOCAL `Map` of the dispositions that page performed, surviving neither a reload nor a second member (a second place a fact is stated, D-21/DEC-8). The published id is the item's OWN id (`FINDING::<progression>::<stage>`), written by two producers that never consult each other — driven in `bio-plane/test/proposedispose.test.mjs`, with `test/d266.control.mjs` arm 2 keying it on decider-and-instant instead and taking exactly that one assertion down. **THE FOLDED SECOND GAP IS NOW COUNTED RATHER THAN CLOSED** (`unattributed_readings`), and counted is the honest verb: the readings a run cannot attribute still mint no item and no team is invented, but *no reading arrived* and *readings arrived whose authorship this record cannot state* are no longer the same answer. Both branches are DRIVEN — `current.test.mjs` §5 gained the RUN_C/V4 fixture the second branch never had, and asserts the exact figure 2. **AND THE GAP'S ORIGIN IS MEASURED RATHER THAN GUESSED, which is the part most worth carrying: it was made by a CORRECT consolidation whose premise was true of the ITEMS and false of the ENVELOPE.** `civicos-ui/app.html` says so at its own site: *"`renderProposals`, `proposalGapHtml` and `loadProposals` are DELETED (UI-14, 2026-08-04). op=proposals is no longer read by any surface: op=queue carries the same derivation as FINDING items … a second read of the same finding through a second op is exactly the 'two surfaces answering one question' this item removes."* That reasoning is right and the consolidation should stand. What nobody checked is that **`op=queue` carried the DERIVATION and not the AGEING**, so the one fact only `op=proposals` published stopped reaching any surface at all — and UI-45, four days later, rebuilt it as a page-local `Map` because the record was no longer offering it. Two correct items and a silence between them, which is this record's most-repeated shape and is why the envelope is where the fix belongs. **WHAT REMAINS OPEN IS NARROW AND IS GENUINELY A RULING: may one team's dismissal silence another team's notification about that other team's stance?** `proposal_dispositions` is instance-wide by design (DEC-16: one act clears the finding under every case it appears in) while a stance is expressly a project's OWN property (§7, D-216), and `#findingsStanceDiverged` already refuses to offer `op=versioncurrent` as an option for exactly that reason. The two rules point opposite ways for this one case and the record settles neither. Until it is settled the act is NOT widened, which is the cheap-to-reverse provisional: no disposition has ever been recorded for these kinds, so there is nothing to migrate whichever way it goes. | M9 · open — **NARROWED, not closed. The doctrine question the row was raised for is ANSWERED and its answer is IMPLEMENTED; what is left is one scoping ruling and the widening behind it.** Nothing overclaims meanwhile: the plane says `available: false` with its reason, the surface withdraws the control rather than offering an act that would be refused, and an aged finding no longer reads like an absent one |

The fix belongs at the walk or at the projection. NEEDS: either `refs` gains a `status` column (a projection change touching `promote` and every reader) or `#queueAncestorEdges` confirms against the citing document's frontmatter as `#projectsDrawingOn` does (cheaper, one method, one extra read per candidate edge). | D-277 | defect | 2026-08-09 | **THE CHECK CATALOG WAS HARVESTED FROM RAW SOURCE, COMMENTS INCLUDED, SO WRITING AN ORDINARY EXPLANATORY COMMENT FAILED THE GATE.** `bio-plane/scripts/coverage.mjs` built the conformance catalog by matching a check-id shape over the bytes of `checks/bio-checks.mjs`. All four closed; the three exposed checks now have real FIRES / absent-on-clean proofs in `check-firing.test.mjs`. The op-reach matcher had the identical shape and was closed too, measured at 0 of 163 ops affected today, so that half is a hazard shut rather than a figure corrected. | D-258 | defect | 2026-08-09 | **`plan.meaning.columns` AND `plan.meaning.refs` ARE COMPUTED AND READ BY NOBODY — D-255's exact class, one field along, found by the instrument D-255 built rather than by reading.** `compile()`'s meaning descriptor (`query.mjs`, the `meaning:` block of the returned plan) carries EIGHT fields. `op=meaningrows` reads six of them at `store.mjs:1330-1336` — `arm`, `table`, `grain`, `identity`, `limit`, `offset` — and publishes all six.
