It is the COPIES placed on the plan that nothing asks for, which is why a name-based sweep reports both names as read and finds nothing. **NOT FIXED WITH D-255, and the reason is a choice rather than caution.** Unlike `atom.phrase`, these sit on a descriptor whose every SIBLING is published by an op, so "give them a reader" is a live alternative that deleting them forecloses — a surface building a table over meaning rows wants to be told which columns a row carries, and `grain`/`identity` are published for exactly that reason. Adding two fields to a published envelope is an interface change belonging to whoever owns `op=meaningrows` (PL-9's), which a D-255 worker may not take on its behalf. **Closing it is one of two one-liners:** delete both fields from the plan, or publish them in the op's envelope and file the IC. Whoever takes it should decide which and say so — and the arm is already written: `bio-plane/test/fieldread.control.mjs` ARM C. | M3 · **CLOSED 2026-08-09 — BOTH FIELDS DELETED, and the delete-or-publish decision is recorded AT THE SITE in `compile()` rather than only here.** **WHAT DECIDED IT, and it is a measurement rather than a preference: the fact `columns`/`refs` carry is ALREADY PUBLISHED, per arm, by `op=searchfields`** — `meaningVocabulary()` emits `rows: { grain, identity, columns, refs }` off the same registry, and that is the vocabulary route surfaces actually compose from. **MEASURED CONSUMERS of `op=meaningrows`, repo-wide 2026-08-09: `civicos-ui` NONE (zero occurrences); `agent-worker/src/index.mjs` ONE call site, which reads `.reached` and `.rows` and nothing else.** So publishing them would have added two fields to an envelope no surface reads, on behalf of a consumer that does not exist — and `columns` restates the keys the returned rows already carry, whereas `grain`/`identity` say what a row MEANS and how it is ADDRESSED, which the rows cannot say about themselves. That is the line the row asked for between a field a member can act on and a value published because it had already been computed. **NO IC ROW IS OWED — no published envelope changed**, pinned key-by-key through the op in `meaningread.test.mjs`; the PUBLISH alternative would have owed one, and reversing costs those two lines plus that row. **`refs` GOT THE TRIPWIRE ARM THE ROW SAID IT LACKED, and it was not inherited: ARM R2** (`fieldread.control.mjs`), plus its own positive control **ARM P3** — the tripwire on `refs` WITH a read injected into `store.mjs` in the unanticipated spelling `plan.meaning["ref"+"s"]`, which takes `bounds` and `meaningread` red and both REPRODUCE alone, so R2s green is not free. **THE CLASS WAS DECIDED, NOT LISTED: `--tripwire-sweep` gives every one of the descriptors fields a VERDICT** over a whole battery each — `arm`/`table`/`grain`/`identity`/`limit`/`offset` **LIVE** (the five D-255 could only assert are now measured), `columns`/`refs` **DEAD** — with a BASELINE row aiming the identical mechanism at a field that cannot exist. **TWO INSTRUMENT DEFECTS FOUND BEFORE ANY SUBJECT DEFECT AND BOTH RECORDED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED:** (i) the first version of the mode used a recording Proxy, whose BASELINE row failed `meaningread.test.mjs` — not workerd, but `meaningread.test.mjs:100` pinning the LITERAL SOURCE TEXT the Proxy wrap rewrites; that mattered because the same suite drives `grain`/`identity`, so a live published field could have been scored DEAD behind its own contamination, and the mechanism was replaced with D-255s throwing getter; (ii) `refs` came back **LIVE** on its first pass because `daemon-token.test.mjs` failed under SIX concurrent batteries — driven alone against the identical armed tree it passed 56/0 with the tripwire never firing, and a whole-battery re-run of the same arm was 141/142 with only the ratchet, so **every failing suite is now RE-DRIVEN ALONE before it counts as a read**. A red battery is not evidence of a read. **Evidence no answer moved: the answer-determining digest is `66593efe4289784d…` over 3,680 compilations, byte-identical either side** (and it is a `--digest` MODE now; D-255s was a one-off that left nothing re-runnable). **TWELVE ARMS, all AS DECLARED, tree PRISTINE by sha256 AND `cmp` on BOTH subjects.** The ratchet is three structural pins in `query.test.mjs` plus the envelope key-set pin in `meaningread.test.mjs`. **D-255s ARM C was CORRECTED, not exempted** — its anchor no longer occurs, so it re-adds the field first and declares the exact suite set. The sweep also turned up an unrelated live defect in the one real consumer: **D-276**. |
| D-271 | defect | 2026-08-09 | **A CHECK DESCRIBED A GUARANTEE IT DID NOT PROVIDE, AND THE ACCEPT CEREMONY WAS BUILT ON TOP OF IT — both halves MEASURED by driving the ops, not by reading them.** Found from the surface side by UI-43 while building DEC-32 §12's accept ceremony; verified from the plane by this item, and **UI-43's reading was right on both halves and NARROWER than the truth on one.** (a) `op=suggest`'s CHECK 4 carried the sentence *"the derivation is PUBLISHED on every version that passes, so the member affirming independence at §12's accept ceremony is affirming it against what the record can see."* `shared_origins`/`origins_complete` went out on `op=suggest`'s WRITE answer and on NO read — not `op=basisversions`, not `op=versionstrength` — so a member at the ceremony, who is reading, affirmed against nothing. **AND THE WRITE HALF CARRIED NO INFORMATION EITHER**, which UI-43 did not claim and this item measured: CHECK 4 REFUSES every non-empty result, so a version that passes necessarily carries `[]`, and a one-part reading (where the origin walk never runs) published byte-identical bytes to a two-part reading that was genuinely walked — `[]` conflating *looked and found nothing* with *there was nothing to look for*, which is D-129 at the field grain. (b) DEC-32 clause 4 requires independent sufficiency be affirmatively claimed PER PART by an act carrying a member's name; **no version act had anywhere to put it**, so the record could not distinguish an accept made after affirming each part from one made by a surface that never asked. RESOLVED: `#independenceOf` is now ONE implementation with TWO consumers — CHECK 4 (which refuses on it) and `op=versionstrength` (which publishes it to the ceremony), **recomputed against the record rather than replayed from the write**, so a shared origin recorded AFTER a version landed is visible at the moment it matters and `checked:false` says when nothing was walked; and `op=versionaccept` takes `affirmed`, refuses an incomplete or unknown naming of the parts (C-25.33) on readings declaring more than one, stores the PART NAMES (never a bare boolean) on `inquiry_basis_versions.affirmed_parts`, and `op=basisversions` publishes them. The CHECK's sentence is CORRECTED at its site with the measurement, never exempted. IC-52. | resolved 2026-08-09 (D-271) · IC-52 filed, version bump and RESOLUTION are CONDUCT's |
| D-283 | gap | 2026-08-09 | **THE TIER-2 ESCALATION REPLACES A DOCUMENT'S TEXT WHOLESALE TOO, AND IT IS THE SAME CLASS AS D-252 WITH A DIFFERENT RULE.** Found by D-252's class sweep, which asked in principle where the plane takes a DOCUMENT-level decision about a per-PAGE fact and then assigns a producer's whole answer over it. Two sites answer, and only one is closed: `needsTier3` (closed 2026-08-09) and `needsTier2` (`index.mjs`), where `i2text = t2.text` replaces every page's text with the pdf-worker's. **IT IS NOT THE SAME DEFECT AND MUST NOT BE FIXED THE SAME WAY**, which is why it is a row rather than a patch: Tier 1 and Tier 2 are both `layer` derivations of the SAME source carrying the SAME `null` cap, so no cap is overclaimed and no provenance is misattributed — where Tier 3's rule is "never replace a page that has text", Tier 2's whole purpose is that its decode of a page is expected to be BETTER than Tier 1's, so that rule would disable it outright. What is unbounded is TEXT LOSS: the predicate is a document-level ratio (`undetermined > chars`), so a document where Tier 1 decoded page 0 well and failed page 1 escalates whole, and if Tier 2 recovers page 1 but does worse on page 0 the plane keeps the worse page silently. NOT MEASURED, and that is stated rather than guessed — measuring it needs pdf.js against a corpus of partially-decodable PDFs, which no fixture in this repository has. NEEDS: a per-page rule for which of two decodes of the same layer wins, which is a real question (character count is exactly the instrument CPDF-9 argued against) and is a different item from D-252. Note `needsTier2` already carries a deliberate per-page-aware guard — it refuses to escalate when EVERY marker is `no_text_layer` — so the mixed case was considered there and closed on purpose for the routing half; it is the ASSIGNMENT half that is open. | M2 · open — CONTENT-PDF; needs a measurement before a rule |
| D-284 | gap | 2026-08-09 | **`reading.text_tier` IS A DOCUMENT-LEVEL SUMMARY OF WHAT IS NOW A PER-PAGE FACT, AND D-252 MADE THAT VISIBLE RATHER THAN CAUSING IT.** A merged document is stamped `text_tier: 3` when any page was transcribed, while its other pages came through tier 1 — so a consumer reading only `text_tier` reads a document-level claim that is true of part of the document. **THE CHAIN IS THE AUTHORITY AND IT CARRIES THE TRUTH**: each derivation step names the pages it covers, and `derivationCap(chain, {page})` answers per page. Deliberately NOT closed here: IC-39 froze `text_tier`/`text_container` unchanged so that a consumer reading only those is unaffected, and widening them in the same turn that changed `text_source` would be an undeclared second interface change wearing the costume of tidiness. The honest options are (a) leave it as the highest tier that contributed, with the chain as the authority — what runs today — or (b) let it go `null` for a mixed document, which is more honest and breaks every consumer that reads it as a number. NEEDS: FRAMEWORK's call on which, in the turn IC-39 is resolved and I2 is versioned. | M2 · open — raised by D-252; CONDUCT/FRAMEWORK own the resolution with IC-39 |
| D-281 | gap | 2026-08-09 | **THE EPHEMERAL PORT RANGE IS A FINITE SHARED RESOURCE THIS ESTATE CONSUMES IN PROPORTION TO CONCURRENCY, AND NOTHING MEASURES IT — A DIFFERENT PORT HAZARD FROM D-249's, WHICH IS WHY IT IS A ROW OF ITS OWN.** D-249 asked whether a suite PINS a port and the answer measured EMPTY. This is the question that turned out to have a live answer, found while measuring that one. **THE THREE FACTS, EACH MEASURED 2026-08-09 by `bio-plane/test/d249-port.probe.mjs` on darwin 25.5.0, not reasoned about:** (1) the range is **49152–65535 = 16,384 ports** (`sysctl`); (2) **exhaustion is REACHABLE — `EADDRNOTAVAIL` at 14,459 simultaneous loopback connections**, so the range and not the file-descriptor limit is the binding constraint, and the probe says which bit rather than assuming; (3) **TIME_WAIT CONSUMES that pool — 0 of 400 TIME_WAIT ports were re-handed-out while 3,000 fresh ones were allocated**, and the arm carries an over-strictness check that the allocator was LIVE (3,000 fresh, 3,000 outside the minted set) so that "withheld" cannot be reported by a probe that simply allocated nothing. `net.inet.tcp.msl` is 15000 ms, so a port is held out for **30 s** after use. **THE CONSUMPTION TRACKS THE BATTERY COUNT:** peak TIME_WAIT was **6,895 (42.1% of the whole range) with 4 concurrent batteries** and **6,565 (40.1%) with 3**, falling to 458–2,955 (2.8%–18%) when none ran. **131 of 151 suites construct a Miniflare and each binds; the entry socket belongs to workerd, a CHILD process.** WHY IT MATTERS HERE SPECIFICALLY: ~100 worktrees of one clone share one machine and one kernel, and the stated ceiling is eight concurrent batteries — this is a MACHINE-WIDE resource, so it is the one hazard a per-worktree sandbox (D-186) structurally cannot fence. **WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED, AND IT IS THE HEADLINE:** extrapolating the 4-battery figure to 8 lands at **119%–174% of the range**, i.e. exhaustion — **but EIGHT BATTERIES WERE NEVER RUN AT ONCE, so this is NARROWED, NOT PROVEN, and an extrapolation is not a measurement.** The confirming experiment was **deliberately not run**: the `headroom` ramp exhausts the range machine-wide and would have inflicted `EADDRNOTAVAIL` on the four other worktrees' batteries measurably running at the time, corrupting their baselines. **The instrument IS the hazard, which is corroboration of a kind and not proof.** RELATION TO THE UNEXPLAINED FAILURES: other sessions have hit `SocketError: other side closed`, `ECONNREFUSED` and a suite hanging past 300 s, each attributed to "load" without investigation. **This row does NOT claim those were port exhaustion** — no failure was captured with a socket-level errno beside it, and saying otherwise would be inventing an attribution. It claims only that a mechanism which would produce exactly those three signatures is measurably present and unmeasured. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: a sampler in `scripts/battery.mjs` recording peak TIME_WAIT and the concurrent battery count per run (report-only, like `residue.mjs`), so the correlation stops being anecdotal; and one honest 8-battery run on a quiet machine to settle the extrapolation. Relation: D-249 (the pinned-port half, closed empty), D-237, D-186, D-231. | M0 · OPEN — the mechanism is measured and real; its reach at the stated concurrency ceiling is NARROWED and not established, and that is stated rather than rounded up. |
| D-282 | defect | 2026-08-09 | **A SUITE THAT FAILS WITH A LARGE ENOUGH DUMP LOSES ITS OWN TALLY UNDER THE BATTERY, BECAUSE `process.exit()` DISCARDS UNFLUSHED PIPE WRITES — D-93's SHAPE THROUGH A MECHANISM D-93 NEVER NAMED.** Found 2026-08-09 by D-249's control arm catching ITSELF: the `widen` arm reported a tally of `-1` and a verdict of NOT AS DECLARED, and **the arm was right while the HARNESS was wrong**. `hygiene.test.mjs` ends `process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0)`, as `hygiene`'s own dispose rule requires. When stdout is a **PIPE** those writes are ASYNCHRONOUS and `process.exit` returns to the OS without flushing them. **MEASURED ON ONE ARMED TREE, SAME RUN, TWO CAPTURES: 191,434 bytes reached a FILE and 89,329 bytes reached a PIPE — 102,105 bytes of TAIL discarded, and the tail is where the tally lives.** **IT IS NOT `maxBuffer`: raising it to 64 MB changed nothing**, because the bytes were never written; that hypothesis was tested and killed rather than assumed. **THIS IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THAT ONE CONTROL. `scripts/battery.mjs:413` spawns every suite with DEFAULT stdio, which is a pipe**, so any suite failing with a dump past roughly the pipe buffer loses its tally under the battery while the early lines survive — the reader sees `FAILED:` lines and no count, or a truncated count, and D-93 exists precisely because a suite that reports no tally reads as a suite that was never run. **THE AMPLIFIER IS `t()` ITSELF:** the failure LABEL is capped at 8 entries by D-237's cap, but `t()` then prints `want … got …` with the **FULL** array, so a check that floods produces a single 146,820-character line and the cap defends nothing at the only moment it matters. **WHAT IS NOT ESTABLISHED:** the exact threshold was not bisected — 89 KB survived and 191 KB did not, on one machine, with one pipe buffer — and no historical battery failure has been re-examined for a truncated tail, so how often this has already happened is UNKNOWN and is stated as unknown. WHAT CLOSING IT TAKES: either cap `t()`'s `got` dump the way its label is already capped (the smaller fix, and it makes every large failure readable rather than merely survivable), or have suites flush before exiting; plus an assertion that a deliberately-flooding suite still reports its tally through a pipe. Relation: D-93 (the tally that never arrives), D-237 (the cap this defeats). | M0 · OPEN — mechanism measured and reproduced; the threshold and the historical reach are both UNDETERMINED and named as such. |
